6. Design Principles

6.1 Host-Safety First

  • Statement: any RASP code path that can fault (panic, OOM, deadlock, infinite loop) MUST be caught and resolved before propagating into the host application.
  • Mechanism: every hook entry is wrapped in std::panic::catch_unwind; every allocating operation has a fallback; timeouts on analyzers via bounded iteration.
  • Tradeoff: small constant overhead per hook call (~1–3 µs catch_unwind frame setup) in exchange for unconditional host survival.
  • Operational implication: RASP telemetry must log internal faults without crashing; fault rate is a primary health metric.
  • Violation consequence: a RASP bug in rasp-detect parser (e.g., stack overflow on crafted SQL) panics the thread, killing the request; if panic is in async context it can abort the executor.

6.2 Inline Latency Budget is Non-Negotiable

  • Statement: total added latency per sink call ≤ 1 ms p99; p95 ≤ 0.3 ms; untainted args short-circuit after taint check (<20 µs).
  • Mechanism: rasp-protect first evaluates taint presence before dispatching to analyzers; all detection loops have iteration caps; regex-free hot paths.
  • Tradeoff: may miss exotic multi-hop taint paths that require expensive traversal; this is accepted — bounded cost is mandatory, false-negative rate can be tuned.
  • Operational implication: overhead dashboard (p50/p95/p99 per sink type) is always on; budget breach triggers alert before prod deployment.
  • Violation consequence: SQLi analyzer running unbounded tokenization on 10 MB blobs adds 50 ms+ per query; app latency SLA breaks; RASP gets disabled.

6.3 Deterministic Block Decisions

  • Statement: given identical (HookEvent, RequestContext, Policy), the Allow/Block action is always the same, with identical Detection.evidence and taint_path.
  • Mechanism: no random sampling in the decision path; no time-dependent logic; policy rule evaluation order is fixed (ascending rule_id).
  • Tradeoff: determinism forbids adaptive/ML scoring in the blocking path (only in monitoring side-channel).
  • Operational implication: incident replay: given logged event + policy snapshot, reproduce decision exactly for forensics.
  • Violation consequence: non-deterministic Block causes inconsistent behavior under load (request A blocks, identical request B does not); compliance audit fails; false-positive investigation becomes impossible.

6.4 Context Isolation Per Request

  • Statement: RequestContext is strictly scoped to one request; no taint map, call stack, or correlation id leaks across concurrent requests or async tasks.
  • Mechanism: thread-local storage for sync runtimes; tokio::task_local! for async; context dropped at request boundary; no global mutable state in rasp-context.
  • Tradeoff: context passing overhead in deeply nested async chains; use propagation helpers, not direct TLS reads.
  • Operational implication: context lifetime events (started, ended) logged to detect leaks; correlation-id cardinality monitoring.
  • Violation consequence: taint from request A contaminates request B's sink check → wrong block or miss; in worst case, attacker crafts a request to poison context for subsequent legitimate request.

6.5 Monitor Purity

  • Statement: in Policy.mode == Monitor, the inline guard emits telemetry and returns Action::Allow unconditionally — zero behavioral impact on the app.
  • Mechanism: rasp-protect checks mode before calling any blocking path; block code paths statically unreachable from Monitor dispatch.
  • Tradeoff: Monitor mode gives zero protection; it exists only for baselining + FP tuning before switching to Block.
  • Operational implication: Monitor mode deployment is the required first step in every new environment; block rate from Monitor data informs policy tuning.
  • Violation consequence: Monitor mode that occasionally blocks (e.g., bug in mode check) breaks the promise used to sell production deployment; causes app outage; erodes trust in RASP.

6.6 Taint Soundness Within Budget

  • Statement: within the configured propagation budget (max_taint_ops), no propagation operation silently drops taint; if budget is reached, the value is marked TaintStatus::BudgetExceeded (conservatively tainted) rather than clean.
  • Mechanism: rasp-taint propagation counters per TaintedValue; budget exceeded → retain taint, emit metric taint.budget_exceeded.
  • Tradeoff: budget-exceeded values may produce false positives at sinks; tradeoff against unbounded cost.
  • Operational implication: taint.budget_exceeded rate monitored; if high, increase budget or identify the propagation chain causing it.
  • Violation consequence: taint silently dropped → attacker chains 10 string operations to escape taint tracking → SQLi passes undetected.

6.7 Observability by Default

  • Statement: every sink call emits structured telemetry (allow or block, taint-hit or miss, latency, rule matched); no silent decisions.
  • Mechanism: rasp-telemetry is called unconditionally on every hook path; structured AttackEvent or AllowEvent with full context.
  • Tradeoff: telemetry write cost per request; mitigated by async buffered sink with backpressure; never synchronous I/O on hot path.
  • Operational implication: telemetry volume proportional to traffic; storage planning required; sampling allowed for Allow events only, never for Block events.
  • Violation consequence: silent allow in an environment with a misconfigured rule produces zero signal; attack succeeds and is invisible to SOC.

6.8 Fail-Safe Defaults

  • Statement: when RASP cannot make a determination (internal error, timeout, context missing), the action defaults to Policy.fail_mode — FailClosed blocks by default; FailOpen allows.
  • Mechanism: rasp-protect::decide returns Err(RaspError) → dispatch to fail_mode handler, never unwrap.
  • Tradeoff: FailClosed increases false-positive rate under RASP faults; FailOpen reduces availability impact; deployment context determines which is appropriate.
  • Operational implication: fail_mode must be explicit in every Policy; no implicit default in code; fail_mode events separately counted in metrics.
  • Violation consequence: implicit fail-open after internal RASP crash → all sink calls allowed during outage window → attackers exploit during RASP instability.

6.9 No Shared Mutable Global State in Hot Path

  • Statement: the inline guard hot path (hook entry → action) reads only immutable or thread/task-local state; no Mutex or RwLock contention on per-request data.
  • Mechanism: Policy loaded once at startup into Arc<Policy> (read-only); RequestContext in task-local; taint map per-context; global lock only for policy hot-reload (infrequent).
  • Tradeoff: policy changes require atomic Arc swap, not per-request re-read; brief inconsistency window during swap acceptable.
  • Operational implication: lock contention metrics on policy reload path; reload events timestamped in audit log.
  • Violation consequence: Mutex<TaintMap> shared across requests → lock contention under load → tail latency spikes → RASP disabled; attacks proceed undetected.

6.10 Semantic Detection Over Pattern Matching

  • Statement: detection analyzers operate on parsed structure (tokens, AST, URL components) not raw regex over full strings wherever feasible.
  • Mechanism: rasp-detect SQLi tokenizes query first (libinjection-inspired); path traversal normalizes then checks; SSRF parses URL components.
  • Tradeoff: parser overhead vs accuracy; short-circuit on untainted input avoids parser cost for benign traffic.
  • Operational implication: parser failures (malformed SQL) are a detection signal themselves; emit DetectEvent with confidence: Low.
  • Violation consequence: naive regex SELECT.*FROM on raw SQL string → bypass via case variation, comment injection, encoding → high false-negative rate in prod.

6.11 Evidence-Complete Blocking

  • Statement: every Action::Block carries a Detection with rule_id, evidence (the tainted substring + position), and taint_path (source → propagation chain → sink).
  • Mechanism: rasp-detect analyzers return Option<Detection>; Block without Detection is a type error.
  • Tradeoff: evidence construction cost (taint path traversal) on detected attacks; acceptable since attacks are rare relative to total traffic.
  • Operational implication: SOC triage uses taint_path to identify the injection source and affected parameter; reduces MTTR.
  • Violation consequence: block with no evidence → developer cannot distinguish false positive from true attack → RASP disabled to stop unexplained request failures.

6.12 Virtual Patching is Time-Bounded

  • Statement: virtual patch waivers in Policy.waivers carry a mandatory expires_at timestamp; expired waivers are treated as non-existent (not as permanent allow).
  • Mechanism: rasp-policy::eval_waivers checks expires_at against wall clock; expired waiver logs WaiverExpired event.
  • Tradeoff: waiver expiry can cause sudden block after prolonged allow if team forgets to renew; this is intentional — unintentional permanent exposure is the worse risk.
  • Operational implication: waiver expiry approaching alerts in rasp-telemetry; waiver lifecycle tracked in audit log.
  • Violation consequence: indefinite waiver masking a real CVE → vulnerability window extends indefinitely; waiver purpose forgotten over time.

6.13 Defense-in-Depth Layering

  • Statement: RASP is not the only control; it does NOT replace parameterized queries, input validation, or WAF (project 338) — it is an inner runtime layer.
  • Mechanism: RASP documentation and telemetry call out when a sink is structurally vulnerable (unparameterized query) even on allow; emits SinkQualityWarning.
  • Tradeoff: RASP detecting a structurally vulnerable but unexploited call causes alert noise; suppress behind a flag.
  • Operational implication: SinkQualityWarning feeds back to dev teams as a code quality signal, separate from attack telemetry.
  • Violation consequence: over-reliance on RASP as sole control → if RASP disabled (maintenance, performance), no other protection for injections.

6.14 Configuration as Code, Validated at Load

  • Statement: Policy is loaded from version-controlled config, validated against schema at startup; invalid config aborts startup (not silently ignored).
  • Mechanism: rasp-policy::load returns Result<Policy, RaspError>; calling code panics at startup on Err (startup-time panic is acceptable; runtime panic is not).
  • Tradeoff: strict validation may reject valid-but-future-format configs; versioned schema + migration path required.
  • Operational implication: config schema version in policy file; rasp-cli validate subcommand for CI gate.
  • Violation consequence: silently ignored invalid rule → intended block rule never active → attack succeeds; discovered only post-incident.

6.15 Backpressure on Telemetry Output

  • Statement: the telemetry write path MUST apply backpressure rather than blocking the hot path; if the sink is full, events are dropped with a counter increment, not buffered unboundedly.
  • Mechanism: bounded tokio::sync::mpsc channel between hot path and telemetry writer; full channel → try_send fails → telemetry.dropped_events counter incremented.
  • Tradeoff: event loss under telemetry saturation; acceptable over blocking the request thread.
  • Operational implication: telemetry.dropped_events > 0 triggers alert; telemetry sink capacity must be sized to peak attack traffic + normal traffic.
  • Violation consequence: synchronous SIEM write on hot path → 200 ms SIEM write latency → 200 ms added to every request → immediate production incident.

7. System Invariants

7.1 Host-Safety Invariant

  • Statement: a RASP fault (panic, OOM, timeout, logic error) NEVER propagates into the host application as a crash, hang, or visible exception.
  • Why: RASP is an optional protection layer; it must not become the source of outages — this is the primary adoption blocker for inline agents.
  • Enforced in: rasp-protect::run_hook wraps all calls in catch_unwind; rasp-hook trampoline catches all Err returns; per-analyzer timeout guard (iteration cap).
  • Violation detection: absence of HookReturned telemetry event after HookEntered within threshold; host application crash rate correlated with RASP deploy.
  • Telemetry emitted: rasp.internal_fault{sink, error_kind} counter; rasp.hook_panic counter; rasp.timeout_exceeded{sink} counter.
  • Recovery: on fault, fall back to policy.fail_mode; emit fault event; increment counter; return control to host normally.
  • Debugging: enable RASP_FAULT_TRACE=1 to capture panic backtraces to a separate log file without crashing the host; correlate context_id to request log.

7.2 Monitor Purity Invariant

  • Statement: when Policy.mode == Monitor, Action::Block is never returned to the host; app behavior is identical to running without RASP.
  • Why: Monitor mode is the contract for safe production rollout; violating it causes unexplained outages and destroys deployment trust.
  • Enforced in: rasp-protect::enforce — static match mode { Monitor => Action::Allow, Block => … }; no flag or runtime condition bypasses this branch.
  • Violation detection: rasp.action{action=Block, mode=Monitor} counter must be permanently zero; alerting on any non-zero value.
  • Telemetry emitted: rasp.monitor_would_block counter (for attacks that would be blocked in Block mode); never rasp.action=Block in Monitor.
  • Recovery: no recovery needed if invariant holds; if counter non-zero, roll back RASP build immediately.
  • Debugging: unit test monitor_never_blocks in rasp-protect asserts Action::Allow for all known attack payloads when mode=Monitor; run in CI on every commit.

7.3 Block Determinism Invariant

  • Statement: decide(hook_event, context, policy) → Action is a pure function; same inputs always produce same output and same Detection.evidence.
  • Why: incident forensics requires exact replay; inconsistency breaks postmortem analysis and FP investigation.
  • Enforced in: rasp-protect::decide has no global mutable state reads; no rand, no timestamps, no external I/O in decision path; policy is immutable Arc.
  • Violation detection: determinism fuzz test: run same HookEvent through decide 1000× with same policy snapshot, assert identical Action and evidence.
  • Telemetry emitted: policy snapshot version attached to every Detection event; enables replay with versioned policy.
  • Recovery: if non-determinism detected in test, bisect to the offending non-pure read and eliminate.
  • Debugging: rasp-bench replay harness: feed logged HookEvent + policy version → assert deterministic output.

7.4 Context Isolation Invariant

  • Statement: TaintMap and RequestContext of request A are never readable or writable by request B, regardless of concurrency model (thread pool, async executor, connection reuse).
  • Why: taint leakage across requests produces both false positives (legitimate B incorrectly blocked using A's taint) and false negatives (attack in A cleared by B's context).
  • Enforced in: rasp-context uses thread_local! for sync; tokio::task_local! for async; context initialized on request start, dropped on request end (RAII guard).
  • Violation detection: concurrent request load test with distinct taint patterns per request; assert no cross-contamination after 10k request pairs.
  • Telemetry emitted: rasp.context_leak counter (incremented if context still active after request end deadline); context lifetime histogram.
  • Recovery: context RAII drop triggered at request end; panic-safety: Drop impl on RequestContextGuard always clears TLS slot.
  • Debugging: context_id logged on every event; cross-request context_id reuse is a bug signal; grep logs for duplicate context_id on non-overlapping request windows.

7.5 Taint Soundness (Bounded) Invariant

  • Statement: within max_taint_ops budget, a taint tag on byte range [s, e] of a TaintedValue is never lost through any tracked propagation operation; ops exceeding budget mark value conservatively tainted.
  • Why: silent taint loss is a false-negative source; attacker can craft chains of innocuous-looking string ops to escape tracking.
  • Enforced in: rasp-taint::propagate decrements per-value op counter; zero budget → TaintStatus::BudgetExceeded; all tracked ops enumerated in PropagationOp enum.
  • Violation detection: taint soundness property test: apply all tracked ops to a tainted value, verify tags present at sink; fuzz with random op chains.
  • Telemetry emitted: rasp.taint.budget_exceeded counter; rasp.taint.ops_per_request histogram.
  • Recovery: BudgetExceeded → taint retained (conservative); analyze ops histogram to tune max_taint_ops per deployment.
  • Debugging: enable RASP_TAINT_TRACE=1 to log per-op tag state for a sampled request; compare pre/post op tag set.

7.6 Overhead Budget Invariant

  • Statement: inline guard adds ≤1 ms p99, ≤0.3 ms p95 per sink call; untainted path ≤20 µs; never blocks request thread on I/O.
  • Why: inline RASP rejected in production if it causes measurable application latency; 5% added latency SLA at system level.
  • Enforced in: rasp-bench overhead harness runs benign corpus at target QPS; CI gate fails if budget breached; analyzers have explicit iteration caps.
  • Violation detection: p99 overhead metric rasp.hook_latency_ns{sink, taint_hit} emitted per call; breach triggers alert.
  • Telemetry emitted: rasp.hook_latency_ns histogram per (sink_type, taint_hit: bool, action); rasp.analyzer_iters{sink} histogram.
  • Recovery: if budget breached, disable expensive analyzer for that sink type via policy flag; emit rasp.analyzer_disabled event; alert ops.
  • Debugging: rasp-bench overhead --sink SqlQuery --qps 5000 reports p50/p95/p99 latency breakdown by phase (taint check, detection, policy eval, telemetry).

7.7 No Blocking I/O on Hook Path Invariant

  • Statement: the code path from HookEvent receipt to Action return contains zero blocking I/O, zero synchronous network calls, zero file reads.
  • Why: any blocking call on the hot path propagates its latency to every instrumented request; a 5 ms SIEM write adds 5 ms to every SQL query.
  • Enforced in: rasp-telemetry write is async try_send to bounded channel; policy loaded into memory at startup; taint + detection operate on in-memory structures only.
  • Violation detection: strace / dtrace on RASP-enabled process under load; assert no blocking syscalls (read, write, connect) from hook call stack; latency anomaly detection.
  • Telemetry emitted: rasp.io_on_hotpath_violation counter (should be permanently zero; instrumented via #[cfg(debug_assertions)] checks).
  • Recovery: if async channel full, try_send failure path drops event and increments telemetry.dropped_events — never blocks.
  • Debugging: flamegraph of hook call path; any I/O system call appearing in hot path is an immediate bug.

7.8 Policy Completeness Invariant

  • Statement: every SinkType has at least one Rule in the loaded Policy; a sink with no matching rule defaults to fail_mode action (never silently to Allow).
  • Why: unconfigured sink falls through to implicit allow → unprotected attack surface; operator assumes protection that does not exist.
  • Enforced in: rasp-policy::validate checks rule coverage for all SinkType variants at policy load; missing coverage → RaspError::PolicyIncomplete.
  • Violation detection: policy validation test in CI; rasp-cli validate <policy.toml> must pass in deployment pipeline.
  • Telemetry emitted: rasp.policy.uncovered_sink{sink} counter; rasp.policy.fallthrough_action{sink, action} for transparency.
  • Recovery: add a catch-all Monitor rule for each sink type as default; fail-closed only when explicitly configured.
  • Debugging: rasp-cli inspect policy.toml --coverage lists each SinkType and its matched rules; gaps are clear.

7.9 Evidence Integrity Invariant

  • Statement: Detection.evidence contains the verbatim tainted substring that triggered the rule, byte-exact from the original TaintedValue.value, with correct byte_range.
  • Why: forensic use of evidence requires it to be the actual attack payload, not a reconstructed or summarized version; incorrect evidence invalidates incident report.
  • Enforced in: rasp-detect analyzers extract evidence slice from original TaintedValue.value[tag.byte_range]; no transformation allowed on evidence string before attaching to Detection.
  • Violation detection: property test: run known attack payloads through detector; verify evidence is a substring of input value at stated byte_range.
  • Telemetry emitted: rasp.evidence_integrity_fail counter (debug builds only; production assumes enforced); logged with full context on failure.
  • Recovery: if evidence extraction fails (range out of bounds), mark Detection.confidence = Low, emit rasp.evidence_extraction_fail, continue with block/monitor per policy.
  • Debugging: rasp-bench replay --event <event.json> re-runs detection and dumps evidence alongside original value for visual diff.

7.10 Waiver Non-Bypass Invariant

  • Statement: a Waiver only suppresses a specific (rule_id, request_path, method) combination; it cannot suppress all rules or all sinks globally.
  • Why: blanket waivers defeat the entire protection model; scoped waivers are auditable and reviewable.
  • Enforced in: rasp-policy::Waiver type has no wildcard variants for rule_id; * as rule_id is rejected at policy load validation.
  • Violation detection: rasp-cli validate rejects wildcard rule_id; unit test asserts type rejects wildcard construction.
  • Telemetry emitted: rasp.waiver_applied{rule_id, path, method} on each waiver match; rasp.waiver_expired{rule_id} on expiry.
  • Recovery: expired waiver reverts to rule's configured action; no manual intervention required.
  • Debugging: rasp-cli waivers list --active shows all current waivers with expiry; --expired shows recently expired ones.

7.11 Taint Source Completeness Invariant

  • Statement: all externally-controlled HTTP inputs (params, headers, body, path segments, cookies) are registered as TaintSource before any sink can be reached in a request.
  • Why: untapered source → taint tracking has no starting point → all sink checks are taint-miss → attacker data flows uninspected.
  • Enforced in: rasp-context::capture_http marks all HttpInfo fields as taint sources at request start; rasp-taint::register_sources called before any HookEvent dispatch.
  • Violation detection: integration test: send attack payload in each HTTP input location; verify taint presence at sink.
  • Telemetry emitted: rasp.source.registered{source_type} counter per request start; rasp.source.missing{source_type} if capture fails.
  • Recovery: if HTTP capture fails (e.g., body parse error), mark all body-derived values as conservatively tainted (TaintStatus::CaptureFailed).
  • Debugging: RASP_SOURCE_TRACE=1 logs each registered source and its byte range at request start; compare against expected source list for the adapter.

7.12 Concurrency Safety Invariant

  • Statement: rasp-core types shared across threads (Policy, Rule, RuleSet) are Send + Sync and immutable after construction; mutable per-request state is never shared.
  • Why: data races on policy or taint map produce non-deterministic behavior, security holes, and UB in unsafe sections.
  • Enforced in: Policy is Arc<Policy> (read-only ref); RequestContext is !Send (cannot be moved across task boundaries accidentally); TaintMap is !Sync.
  • Violation detection: cargo test with --cfg loom for concurrent model checking of shared state; ThreadSanitizer in CI on integration tests.
  • Telemetry emitted: N/A (compile-time enforcement preferred); runtime: rasp.race_condition_detected counter in debug builds only.
  • Recovery: type system prevents most races; loom tests catch ordering issues; TSan findings require immediate fix before merge.
  • Debugging: RUSTFLAGS="-Z sanitizer=thread" integration test run; cargo test --cfg loom concurrent model check.

8. Domain Model — Runtime Taint-Propagation & Sink-Interception

8.1 Entity Catalogue

Entity Crate Cardinality Lifetime
RequestContext rasp-context 1 per request request boundary
TaintMap rasp-taint 1 per request same as RequestContext
TaintedValue rasp-taint 0..N per request per propagation op
HookEvent rasp-hook 1 per sink invocation inline; stack-allocated
Detection rasp-detect 0..M per sink invocation emitted to rasp-protect
Action rasp-policy 1 per Detection inline decision
Rule rasp-policy loaded at startup global; RCU-updated
Policy rasp-policy 1 global + per-route waivers loaded; hot-reloadable
AttackEvent rasp-telemetry 1 per Block/Monitor emission async queued
SinkArgs rasp-hook 1 per HookEvent borrowed from sink call frame

8.2 Core Type Definitions (normative)

SinkType :: SqlQuery | Exec | FileOpen | HttpRequest
          | Deserialize | LdapQuery | XmlParse | NoSqlQuery

TaintSource :: HttpParam | HttpHeader | HttpBody | HttpPath | Cookie

TaintTag   :: { source: TaintSource, byte_range: Range<usize>, generation: u32 }

TaintedValue :: { value: Bytes, tags: Vec<TaintTag> }

TaintMap   :: HashMap<ValueId, TaintedValue>   // keyed by intern handle

HookEvent  :: { sink: SinkType, args: SinkArgs, context_id: ContextId,
                phase: Entry | Exit, timestamp: MonotonicNs }

RequestContext :: { id: ContextId, http: HttpInfo, taint_map: TaintMap,
                   call_stack: SmallVec<[FrameInfo;8]>, started: MonotonicNs }

Detection  :: { rule_id: RuleId, sink: SinkType, severity: Severity,
               confidence: f32, evidence: Bytes, taint_path: TaintPath }

Action     :: Allow | Block | Monitor

Rule       :: { id: RuleId, sink: SinkType, kind: Signature | Semantic,
               action: Action, priority: u16 }

Policy     :: { mode: Monitor | Block, rules: Vec<Rule>,
               waivers: Vec<Waiver>, fail_mode: Open | Closed }

RaspError  :: HookAbiError | ContextMissing | TaintOverflow
            | DetectorPanic | PolicyLoadFail | TelemetryDrop

8.3 State Machine — Request Lifecycle

States: Idle → Active → Completing → Done

         request-start hook
  Idle ─────────────────────────────────────────────► Active
         (allocate RequestContext, seed taint from HTTP headers/params/body)

         sink invocation (0..N)
  Active ─────────────────────────────────────────────► Active
         (HookEvent → taint-check → detect → policy → Allow/Block)
         ↓ Block
         Active ──────────────────────────────────────► Active
                (abort op; inject safe error to app; emit AttackEvent)

         request-end hook (or thread reclamation)
  Active ─────────────────────────────────────────────► Completing
         (flush pending telemetry, reclaim TaintMap)

  Completing ──────────────────────────────────────────► Done
         (context slot released; correlation-id archived)

  Any state ──── RASP internal panic ──────────────────► Active (fail_mode)
         (host-safety invariant: catch_unwind + log; never propagate)

Invariant: RequestContext allocated exactly once per request entry; NEVER shared across concurrent requests. Context slot must be released at Done to prevent leak.

8.4 State Machine — Taint Propagation

States per TaintedValue: Clean | Tainted(tags) | Overflowed | Dropped

  Clean ──── source-tagged at HTTP ingress ────────────► Tainted(tags)
  Tainted ── string-concat / format / slice ───────────► Tainted(merged_tags)
  Tainted ── untracked op (regex-replace, etc.) ────────► Dropped (documented gap)
  Tainted ── tag-set exceeds TAINT_TAG_LIMIT ───────────► Overflowed
  Overflowed ─────────────────────────────────────────────► treat as fully-tainted (sound)
  Dropped ────────────────────────────────────────────────► treat as clean (FN risk; documented)
  • TAINT_TAG_LIMIT default: 64 tags per value; configurable, not unbounded.
  • Overflowed → conservative: assume all bytes tainted from all sources.
  • Propagation budget: max PROPAGATION_OPS (default 4096) per request; on breach → Overflowed on remaining ops.

8.5 Hook Event Sequencing — Sequence Diagram

 App Thread                rasp-hook          rasp-context    rasp-taint    rasp-detect   rasp-policy   rasp-protect
     │                         │                   │               │              │              │             │
     │── sink_entry(args) ────►│                   │               │              │              │             │
     │                         │── context_id? ───►│               │              │              │             │
     │                         │◄── ctx ───────────│               │              │              │             │
     │                         │── check_taint(args,ctx) ─────────►│              │              │             │
     │                         │                   │◄── tainted? ──│              │              │             │
     │                         │   [if tainted] ───────────────────────────────►  │              │             │
     │                         │                   │               │   analyze()──►│              │             │
     │                         │                   │               │◄── Detection? │              │             │
     │                         │   [if Detection] ─────────────────────────────────────────────► │             │
     │                         │                   │               │              │ decide()─────►│             │
     │                         │                   │               │              │◄── Action ────│             │
     │                         │   [Block] ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────►│
     │◄── Err(Blocked) ────────│                   │               │              │              │  abort_sink()│
     │                         │── emit_event(Detection, Action) ──────────────────────────────────────────────►│(telemetry async)
     │                         │   [Allow or Monitor] ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────► │
     │◄── Ok(proceed) ─────────│                   │               │              │              │             │
  • All steps inside the hook are synchronous, bounded-time.
  • Telemetry emit is non-blocking (channel send; drop if full → metric incremented).
  • If any step panics: catch_unwind in rasp-hook; apply fail_mode; never propagate.

8.6 Per-Sink Analyzer State Machines

8.6.1 SQLi Analyzer

Input: (query_string: &str, tainted_ranges: &[Range<usize>])

States:
  Tokenize → [tokens]
  Inject_Check(token_at_tainted_range) →
      Keyword-in-taint? → Detection(SqlInjection, High)
      Quote-break? → Detection(SqlInjection, High)
      No injection syntax? → Allow
  Error (tokenize fails) → Detection(Suspicious, Medium) [conservative]
  • Tokenizer: libinjection-style; pure Rust re-implementation in rasp-detect.
  • Tainted ranges mapped to token boundaries; injection flagged if tainted region contains SQL keyword or structural token (', --, ;, UNION, OR 1=1 patterns).
  • Parameterized queries (args passed as typed bind params, not string-interpolated): no taint in query string → Allow immediately.

8.6.2 Command Injection Analyzer

Input: (cmd: &OsStr | &[OsStr], tainted_ranges)

States:
  Shell-invoked (sh -c "...") → parse shell metachar in tainted range
      Metachar { |, ;, &, $(), ``, >, < } in taint → Detection(CmdInjection, Critical)
  Execve-style (argv[]) → tainted argv element is whole field
      User-controlled argv[0] → Detection(CmdInjection, Critical)
      User-controlled arg with path-sep → Detection(PathTraversal, High)
  No taint in cmd/args → Allow

8.6.3 Path Traversal Analyzer

Input: (path: &Path, tainted_ranges)

States:
  Normalize(path) → canonical
  Tainted segment contains "../" or "%2e%2e" → Detection(PathTraversal, High)
  Canonical path outside ALLOWED_ROOT_SET → Detection(PathTraversal, High)
  Symlink target outside ALLOWED_ROOT_SET → Detection(PathTraversal, Medium)
  No escape → Allow
  • ALLOWED_ROOT_SET configured in Policy; default empty = alert on any traversal attempt.

8.6.4 SSRF Analyzer

Input: (url: &str, tainted_ranges)

States:
  Parse URL → scheme + host + port
  Host in DENY_LIST (169.254.0.0/16, 10.0.0.0/8, 127.0.0.0/8, ::1, metadata endpoints) → Detection(SSRF, Critical)
  Tainted host not in ALLOW_LIST → Detection(SSRF, High)
  Tainted scheme (file://, gopher://, dict://) → Detection(SSRF, Critical)
  Parse error on tainted input → Detection(SSRF, Medium)
  Allow → proceed
  • DNS resolution NOT performed inline (overhead + TOCTOU); alert at URL-parse level.
  • ALLOW_LIST required for Block mode; Monitor mode logs all outbound to tainted hosts.

8.6.5 Deserialization Analyzer

Input: (format: SerFormat, payload: &[u8], tainted: bool)

States:
  tainted=false → Allow (payload not from HTTP input)
  format=Java|PHP|Python-pickle → Detection(DeserGadget, Critical) [always deny tainted]
  format=JSON|CBOR → structural scan: class-name fields gadget-list → Detection if found
  format=XML → delegate to XmlParse analyzer (XXE check)
  Unknown format → Detection(Suspicious, Medium)
  • Gadget list: known ysoserial chains, Commons-Collections payloads (hex/class-name signatures).
  • JSON deserialization: only flag @class, _type, __type__ with known gadget class names.

8.6.6 XXE Analyzer

Input: (xml_bytes: &[u8], tainted: bool)

States:
  tainted=false → quick DOCTYPE scan (defense-in-depth)
  DOCTYPE present + SYSTEM/PUBLIC entity → Detection(XXE, High)
  External entity ref (http://, file://, jar://) → Detection(XXE, Critical)
  Parameter entity (%name;) in tainted input → Detection(XXE, High)
  Well-formed, no external entity → Allow

8.6.7 LDAP/NoSQL Injection Analyzer

Input: (query: &str | Value, tainted_ranges)

LDAP:
  Tainted region contains metachar { (, ), *, \, NUL } → Detection(LdapInjection, High)

NoSQL (MongoDB-style):
  Tainted string contains "$" operator prefix → Detection(NoSqlInjection, High)
  Tainted value parsed as JSON object (not scalar) → Detection(NoSqlInjection, High)

8.7 TaintMap Data Model

TaintMap layout (per-request, heap-allocated, owned by RequestContext):

  key: ValueId (u64 intern handle, assigned at source-tag time)
  value: TaintedValue { value: Bytes, tags: Vec<TaintTag> }

  TaintTag: { source: TaintSource, byte_range: Range<usize>, generation: u32 }
    generation: incremented each propagation step; aids forensic taint-path reconstruction

  TaintPath (for Detection evidence):
    Vec<TaintStep { op: TaintOp, src_id: ValueId, dst_id: ValueId, generation: u32 }>
    TaintOp :: Concat | Slice | Format | HttpIngress | PropagateOpaque
  • Map cleared atomically at request-end; no cross-request sharing.
  • ValueId collision probability negligible (u64 counter per context, not global hash).
  • Memory limit: TAINT_MAP_MAX_BYTES (default 4 MiB per request); on breach → oldest entries evicted + metric fired; Detection(TaintOverflow, Low) emitted.

8.8 Policy Evaluation Model

Input:  Detection + RequestContext + Policy
Output: Action (Block | Monitor | Allow)

Evaluation order:
  1. Waiver match (exact route + rule_id + ip range) → Allow (waiver wins)
  2. Policy.mode == Monitor → Monitor (no block regardless of rule action)
  3. Rule lookup by (sink, rule_id, priority desc):
       first matching rule with action=Block → Block
       first matching rule with action=Allow → Allow
       no match → Policy.fail_mode: Closed→Block, Open→Allow
  4. Result annotated with matched rule_id + waiver_id (or "default")

State transition of a Detection through Policy:

Detection ──── waiver? ──Yes──► Allow (waiver_id tagged)
     │ No
     ▼
  mode=Monitor? ──Yes──► Monitor (emit; no block)
     │ No (Block mode)
     ▼
  rule match? ──Yes──► rule.action (Block or Allow per rule)
     │ No match
     ▼
  fail_mode: Open → Allow; Closed → Block
  • virtual_patch: a Rule with kind=Signature targeting a known CVE pattern; allows per-pattern disable without app redeploy.
  • Waiver fields: { route_pattern, rule_id, expires_at, requester, ticket_id } — all waivers are time-bounded and audited.

8.9 HookEvent Protocol / Wire Format

HookEvent (in-process; no serialization — direct struct pass across FFI boundary):

C ABI layout (for runtime-adapter FFI):
  struct RaspHookEvent {
    sink_type:   u32,         // SinkType discriminant
    context_id:  u64,         // ContextId
    phase:       u8,          // 0=Entry, 1=Exit
    _pad:        [u8; 3],
    timestamp_ns: u64,
    args_ptr:    *const u8,   // SinkArgs serialized as msgpack in adapter-allocated buffer
    args_len:    usize,
  }

SinkArgs variants (msgpack schema per SinkType):
  SqlQuery:    { query: str, params: [Value] }
  Exec:        { program: bytes, argv: [bytes], env: [(bytes,bytes)] }
  FileOpen:    { path: bytes, flags: u32 }
  HttpRequest: { method: str, url: str, headers: [(str,str)], body: bytes }
  Deserialize: { format: u8, payload: bytes }
  LdapQuery:   { filter: str, base_dn: str }
  XmlParse:    { fragment: bytes }
  • args_ptr buffer owned by the adapter; must remain valid for duration of hook call.
  • RASP never writes back through args_ptr; read-only borrow.
  • context_id resolved in rasp-context via thread-local or task-local lookup.

8.10 AttackEvent Wire Format (Telemetry / SIEM)

AttackEvent (JSON internal; CEF on emission):

{
  "event_id":    "<uuid-v7>",
  "timestamp":   "<rfc3339>",
  "context_id":  "<u64-hex>",
  "request":     { "method", "path", "remote_ip", "correlation_id" },
  "sink":        "<SinkType>",
  "rule_id":     "<str>",
  "severity":    "critical|high|medium|low|info",
  "confidence":  0.0..1.0,
  "action":      "block|monitor|allow",
  "evidence":    "<base64-truncated-512B>",
  "taint_path":  [{ "op", "src_id", "dst_id", "generation" }],
  "waiver_id":   "<str|null>",
  "overhead_ns": "<u64>"
}

CEF mapping:
  CEF:0|RASP|rasp-engine|1.0|<rule_id>|<rule_id>|<sev-int>|
    src=<remote_ip> request=<path> act=<action> cs1=<evidence-hash> cs2=<taint_path_len>
  • evidence truncated to 512 bytes before telemetry; full evidence stored in local audit ring (rasp-telemetry in-process ring buffer, 65536 entries).
  • overhead_ns = wall-clock from hook entry to policy decision (excludes telemetry emit).

8.11 Context Isolation — Concurrency Model

Thread model:
  thread_local!(static RASP_CTX: RefCell<Option<RequestContext>>)
  set on request-start hook; cleared on request-end hook.

Async model (tokio):
  task_local!(static RASP_CTX: Arc<Mutex<RequestContext>>)
  spawned task inherits parent's context_id for correlation but NOT its TaintMap.
  Child task sees its own TaintMap (child_of parent, tagged with parent_id for tracing).

Cross-task taint propagation:
  Values passed across task boundaries via channel → taint tags embedded in a
  TaintCarrier wrapper; rasp-context extracts tags on recv side.
  Values passed via Arc<T> (shared read) → tainted at read site if origin tainted.
  No automatic cross-task taint; explicit TaintCarrier required (documented gap).
  • Invariant: RequestContext.taint_map never accessible from a different request's thread.
  • Mutex in async model: uncontended fast path (single writer per request task); contention metric tracked.

8.12 Failure-Path Examples

Example A — SQLi Block Path

HTTP GET /items?id=1' OR '1'='1
  → HttpParam("id") → TaintedValue { value: "1' OR '1'='1", tags: [HttpParam, 0..15] }

App calls: db.query("SELECT * FROM items WHERE id = " + user_input)
  → rasp-hook fires: HookEvent { sink: SqlQuery, args: { query: "SELECT...1' OR '1'='1" } }
  → rasp-context: resolves ctx for this thread
  → rasp-taint: range [27..42] of query string is tainted (tag: HttpParam)
  → rasp-detect: tokenize → " OR '1'='1" contains keyword OR + quote-break → Detection(SQLi, Critical, conf=0.97)
  → rasp-policy: mode=Block, rule R-001 matches → Action::Block
  → rasp-protect: abort DB call; return Err(QueryAborted) to app ORM
  → rasp-telemetry: emit AttackEvent (async); overhead_ns recorded

Example B — Path Traversal Monitor Path

HTTP GET /download?file=../../etc/passwd
  → HttpParam("file") → TaintedValue { value: "../../etc/passwd", tags: [HttpParam] }

App calls: File::open(base_dir + "/" + user_input)
  → HookEvent { sink: FileOpen, path: "/var/app/../../etc/passwd" }
  → rasp-taint: path contains tainted segment
  → rasp-detect: normalize → "/etc/passwd"; outside ALLOWED_ROOT_SET="/var/app"
    → Detection(PathTraversal, High, conf=0.99)
  → rasp-policy: mode=Monitor → Action::Monitor
  → rasp-protect: Allow op to proceed (monitor mode)
  → rasp-telemetry: emit AttackEvent with action=monitor
  App returns /etc/passwd contents (not blocked — monitor only)

Example C — SSRF on Cloud Metadata

HTTP POST /fetch body: {"url":"http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/"}
  → HttpBody tainted → TaintedValue

App calls: reqwest::get(user_url)
  → HookEvent { sink: HttpRequest, url: "http://169.254.169.254/..." }
  → rasp-detect SSRF: host 169.254.169.254 in DENY_LIST → Detection(SSRF, Critical)
  → Policy: Block → rasp-protect aborts HTTP call
  → App receives Err(RequestBlocked)

Example D — Deserialization Gadget

HTTP POST /api/restore body: <binary Java-serialized payload with CommonsCollections chain>
  → HttpBody tainted
App calls: deserialize(body_bytes)
  → HookEvent { sink: Deserialize, format: Java, payload: body_bytes, tainted: true }
  → rasp-detect: format=Java + tainted → Detection(DeserGadget, Critical, conf=1.0)
  → Block; app receives Err(DeserializationBlocked)

Example E — RASP Internal Error (Host-Safety)

rasp-detect SQLi tokenizer panics on malformed UTF-8 input:
  → catch_unwind in rasp-hook catches panic
  → log error internally; increment rasp_internal_errors counter
  → apply fail_mode:
      Open  → Allow (proceed; miss potential attack)
      Closed → Block (safe-default; may FP)
  → emit internal-error AttackEvent (severity=info, no blocking action logged as reason)
  → protected app never sees panic; thread state is clean post-unwind

8.13 Edge-Case Catalogue

Edge Case Behavior Invariant
Context missing at sink (async task with no TaintCarrier) ContextMissing → apply fail_mode host-safety
Taint tag count exceeds TAINT_TAG_LIMIT Mark Overflowed; treat as fully tainted taint soundness
Propagation op count exceeds PROPAGATION_OPS Remaining ops → Overflowed overhead budget
Regex-replace on tainted string (untracked) Taint Dropped; logged as gap documented FN
Parameterized SQL (no string interpolation) No taint in query string → Allow; no FP block determinism
Waiver expired (expires_at in past) Treat as no waiver; re-evaluate rule policy correctness
Telemetry channel full Drop event; increment rasp_telemetry_drops host-safety
Double request-start hook (bug in adapter) Second call replaces context; first context reclaimed; log warning context isolation
Block in Monitor mode Impossible by invariant (Monitor purity); if triggered → bug + panic in debug, log in release monitor purity
SinkArgs msgpack decode failure Detection(Suspicious, Medium); fall to policy host-safety

8.14 Metrics Model (rasp-telemetry)

Metric Type Labels Semantics
rasp_hook_calls_total counter sink, phase total hook invocations
rasp_tainted_args_total counter sink hook calls where args were tainted
rasp_detections_total counter sink, rule_id, severity detections fired
rasp_blocks_total counter sink, rule_id blocks enforced
rasp_monitor_events_total counter sink, rule_id monitor events emitted
rasp_hook_latency_ns histogram sink p50/p95/p99 inline guard overhead
rasp_taint_map_bytes gauge — current TaintMap size
rasp_taint_overflow_total counter — taint budget exceeded events
rasp_internal_errors_total counter subsystem RASP internal panics/errors
rasp_telemetry_drops_total counter — dropped events (channel full)
rasp_policy_reload_total counter status policy hot-reload attempts
rasp_waiver_matches_total counter rule_id waiver hits

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
ADR-001 Implementation language Rust 2021, MSRV 1.78 JVM agent (Java/Kotlin), .NET CLR profiler, Node.js native addon, Go plugin Zero-cost abstractions; memory safety on untrusted inputs; no GC pauses inline; precise FFI for hook ABI; catch_unwind for host-safety JVM agent wins when the target application is pure JVM and bytecode weaving (via Java Instrumentation API) provides richer hook coverage than C-ABI FFI; Go plugin wins when instrumenting a Go HTTP service where CGO overhead is acceptable and the team lacks Rust expertise
ADR-002 Build system Cargo workspace (rasp-core, …, rasp-bench, 10 crates) Bazel, Buck2, CMake Cargo is Rust standard; workspace unifies builds; no polyglot toolchain needed Bazel/Buck2 win in a monorepo where RASP shares build graph with non-Rust services (e.g., C++ adapters, Python bindings) and hermetic cross-language caching is required
ADR-003 Dependency policy cargo deny allowlist; no unsafe in dependencies without audit; SemVer pinned in Cargo.lock Unpinned deps, vendoring everything Reproducibility + supply-chain safety; cargo deny blocks unknown licenses/advisories Full vendoring wins in air-gapped environments where no external network access is available at build time, or when regulatory compliance mandates an immutable, audited source snapshot
ADR-004 Async runtime Tokio 1.x (task-local context, JoinSet, channels) async-std, smol, bare futures Tokio is ecosystem-dominant; task-local! macro for context propagation; good catch_unwind integration async-std wins for embedded targets or environments where Tokio's work-stealing scheduler is prohibited by RTOS constraints; bare futures win in no_std environments where any runtime is too heavy
ADR-005 Concurrency primitives std::thread_local! (sync), tokio::task_local! (async), parking_lot::RwLock for Policy std::sync::RwLock, crossbeam channels parking_lot lower overhead; task-local avoids context leakage; RCU-style policy swap std::sync::RwLock wins when linking to a no_std-compatible target where parking_lot is unavailable; crossbeam channels win when bounded backpressure between producers and consumers is needed (telemetry pipeline redesign)
ADR-006 Policy hot-reload arc-swap crate (lock-free Arc swap) Mutex swap, reload-via-signal, full restart Zero-reader-delay on policy update; consistent read-once per hook invocation Mutex swap wins at very low concurrency (single-threaded CLI tool, test harness) where lock contention does not arise; full restart wins under strict immutable-infrastructure policy where dynamic state change is prohibited
ADR-007 SinkArgs serialization (adapter→core) MessagePack (rmp-serde) JSON, Protobuf, FlatBuffers, Cap'n Proto Compact binary; no schema IDL; fast decode in Rust; smaller inline overhead vs JSON Protobuf wins when RASP must interoperate with non-Rust adapters across a service mesh where schema evolution and cross-language code generation justify the IDL overhead; FlatBuffers wins for zero-copy access when SinkArgs are very large (e.g., full HTTP body)
ADR-008 AttackEvent serialization (internal→SIEM) JSON (serde_json) for internal storage; CEF string for SIEM export OTLP, NDJSON, Avro JSON: human-readable audit log; CEF: SIEM interoperability standard; no extra schema registry OTLP wins when the organization runs an OpenTelemetry Collector pipeline where all telemetry (traces, metrics, logs) is unified; Avro wins in high-throughput event-streaming systems (Kafka) where schema registry and columnar compaction matter
ADR-009 SQLi detection algorithm libinjection-inspired tokenizer (pure Rust, rasp-detect) PCRE/regex, full SQL parser (sqlparser-rs), ML model Tokenizer matches libinjection accuracy benchmark; no regex-ReDoS; full parser too heavy inline; ML non-deterministic Full SQL parser (sqlparser-rs) wins in an offline pre-execution analysis mode (batch audit, DAST scanner) where latency is not inline; ML model wins when the corpus is domain-specific (proprietary query patterns) and a labeled training set is maintained
ADR-010 SSRF detection URL-parse + IP-range deny-list (no DNS resolution inline) DNS-resolve + compare, network egress hook DNS inline = latency + TOCTOU; URL-level detection catches most SSRF patterns; egress hook = runtime-specific DNS-resolve + compare wins when DNS rebind attacks are the primary concern and a dedicated fast resolver (local DoH) can be queried within SLA; network egress hook wins in kernel-bypass or eBPF-enforced environments where syscall interception is already in place
ADR-011 Taint tracking granularity Byte-range tags on string values; no per-character shadow memory DFT (dynamic flow tracking) shadow memory, LLVM DataFlowSanitizer Shadow memory requires full instrumentation; byte-range is sufficient for HTTP injection detection; bounded cost DFT shadow memory wins for security research and offline program analysis (not inline production RASP) where completeness is valued over performance; LLVM DataFlowSanitizer wins for native binaries compiled under your control where sanitizer passes are applied at compile time
ADR-012 TaintMap data structure HashMap<ValueId, TaintedValue> (std HashMap, per-request) persistent trie, reference-counted taint DAG, global taint store Per-request map: isolated, cheap to clear; DAG = complex reclamation; global = contention Persistent trie wins when taint must survive request boundaries (e.g., cross-request session-tainting for stored-XSS detection); ref-counted DAG wins when provenance chains must be retained for forensic reconstruction of multi-hop propagation
ADR-013 Telemetry channel tokio::sync::mpsc unbounded-ish with explicit capacity cap; drop on full crossbeam-channel, flume, blocking send Non-blocking send preserves host-safety invariant; drop-with-metric preferred over backpressure Blocking send wins when the RASP operates in an audit-only mode where no event must be dropped (regulated financial services, PCI-DSS audit log completeness); crossbeam-channel wins in synchronous (non-Tokio) runtimes where tokio::sync is unavailable
ADR-014 Metrics format Prometheus exposition (metrics crate + metrics-exporter-prometheus) StatsD, OTLP metrics, custom binary Prometheus: pull-model; widely supported; metrics facade allows backend swap StatsD wins in environments where all metrics are push-based and no Prometheus scraper is deployed; OTLP wins when all telemetry flows through an OpenTelemetry Collector with a unified backend (Grafana Cloud, Honeycomb)
ADR-015 Tracing tracing crate with tracing-subscriber; per-hook span (disabled by default, enable via env) log crate only, OpenTelemetry SDK tracing structured + low overhead when disabled; spans enable per-request latency profiling OpenTelemetry SDK wins when distributed tracing context (W3C TraceContext propagation) must flow from RASP spans into the application's existing APM pipeline (Jaeger, Zipkin, Datadog APM) without a bridging layer
ADR-016 Fuzzing cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) for all parser inputs (SQL tokenizer, URL parser, msgpack decode, XML scanner) AFL++, honggfuzz, property-testing only cargo-fuzz integrates natively; sanitizer support; OSS-Fuzz compatible AFL++ wins when coverage feedback is needed across language boundaries (C adapter + Rust core) in a single fuzzing harness; honggfuzz wins when hardware performance counters (perf_event_open) are required for branch-coverage feedback on Linux
ADR-017 Testing strategy Unit (each crate), integration (rasp-bench attack corpus + benign corpus), property (proptest), fuzz Manual testing only, snapshot tests only Attack corpus covers OWASP test cases; benign corpus measures FP rate; proptest for taint invariants Snapshot tests win for CLI output and human-readable report formats where exact formatting must not drift; property-testing alone wins during early prototyping when an attack corpus is not yet curated
ADR-018 Benchmark harness criterion microbenchmarks per analyzer; rasp-bench end-to-end throughput; overhead budget <5% p95 Custom timing loops, iai-callgrind only criterion statistical soundness; end-to-end captures real app overhead; wall-clock + perf-counter modes iai-callgrind wins for deterministic, noise-free instruction-count benchmarks in CI environments where wall-clock variance is high (shared cloud runners); custom loops win in embedded targets where criterion's statistical infrastructure is too heavy
ADR-019 CI/CD pipeline GitHub Actions: cargo test, cargo clippy --deny warnings, cargo deny, cargo fuzz (short runs), bench regression gate Jenkins, CircleCI, self-hosted only GH Actions: zero infra; bench gate catches overhead regressions per PR Self-hosted runners win when benchmark reproducibility requires dedicated hardware (bare-metal, pinned CPU frequency) to avoid cloud-runner variance; Jenkins wins in enterprises with existing pipeline infrastructure and compliance controls on build environments
ADR-020 Hook ABI target (primary) C ABI (extern "C") with stable struct layout for adapter FFI Rust ABI (unstable), WASM component model, RPC C ABI: stable across compiler versions; required for JVM JNI, CPython C-ext, Node N-API adapters WASM component model wins when RASP must run in a sandboxed plugin host (e.g., Envoy WASM extension, Spin serverless) where native shared libraries are disallowed; RPC (gRPC/Unix socket) wins when the hook path can tolerate >1 ms latency and process isolation is a security requirement
ADR-021 Error handling model Result<T, RaspError> throughout; catch_unwind at hook boundary; no unwrap/expect in hot paths anyhow for all error types, panic-based, thiserror only thiserror for typed errors; catch_unwind at boundary enforces host-safety invariant anyhow wins in application-layer (CLI, integration test harness) code where error context richness matters more than typed matching; panic-based wins in pure library code during rapid prototyping before the error taxonomy is stable
ADR-022 Logging tracing::error!/warn!/info! structured; JSON log format via tracing-subscriber JSON layer println!, env_logger, syslog direct JSON: machine-parseable; tracing: correlated to request span; no stdout in library crates Syslog direct wins in environments where the RASP is deployed as an OS service (systemd unit) and all logs must flow through the kernel syslog facility for auditability; env_logger wins for extremely minimal builds where compile-time log level filtering is sufficient
ADR-023 Config format TOML (Policy, Rules, ALLOW_LIST, DENY_LIST, limits) parsed via toml crate YAML (ambiguous), JSON (no comments), custom DSL TOML: unambiguous; Rust-ecosystem standard; comments supported YAML wins when the RASP is managed by a Kubernetes operator where configuration is rendered from Helm charts that already produce YAML; JSON wins when policy is machine-generated by an API and human readability is not required
ADR-024 Packaging / distribution Shared library (.so/.dylib) + static library (libRASP.a) + rasp-cli binary Docker-only, kernel module, LD_PRELOAD only Shared lib: runtime-adapter loads via dlopen; static: link-time embedding; CLI: ops tooling Kernel module wins when the deployment target is a controlled Linux server where eBPF is unavailable and kernel-level syscall interception (via ptrace or LSM hooks) is the only approved interception mechanism; LD_PRELOAD wins for intercepting glibc syscall wrappers in unmodified binaries without source access
ADR-025 Reproducible builds cargo with locked Cargo.lock; SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH pinned; Docker base image SHA-pinned Best-effort builds, floating base images Reproducible: auditability + supply-chain; SHA-pinned base images prevent upstream injection Floating base images win in rapid prototyping/development where always-latest security patches in the base image are more valuable than bit-for-bit reproducibility; best-effort builds win in ephemeral CI jobs where artifacts are not published externally
ADR-026 Security tooling cargo audit (RUSTSEC advisory check); cargo deny (license + ban); clippy::undocumented_unsafe_blocks lint Manual review only, Snyk cargo audit in CI catches known CVEs; undocumented unsafe blocks fail CI Snyk wins when the organization already has a Snyk enterprise license covering multi-language monorepos (Rust + JS + Python) and unified SCA dashboards are required; manual review wins for third-party audits where tool-assisted review must be supplemented by human expert judgment
ADR-027 Docs rustdoc for API docs; ADR table in blueprint; per-crate ARCHITECTURE.md; mdbook for operator guide Confluence, Notion, wiki-only rustdoc co-located with code; mdbook for human-readable operator runbook; no external wiki drift Confluence/Notion win in large enterprises where documentation discovery, search, and access control are managed centrally and developers cannot host static sites; wiki-only wins for internal tools where the audience is small and co-located with the codebase
ADR-028 Licensing Apache-2.0 OR MIT (dual) for library crates; Apache-2.0 for CLI/bench GPL (incompatible with commercial embedding), proprietary Dual license: embedded in commercial apps without copyleft; Apache patent clause protection GPL wins when the project is a standalone security tool (not embedded in apps) and copyleft is desired to ensure improvements flow back to the community; proprietary licensing wins when the RASP is the organization's core commercial product and source disclosure is prohibited
