2.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
Stale thread-local after request (thread pool reuse) next request reads previous request's context ContextGuard not dropped (early return / ? propagation) cross-request taint data in detection ContextGuard MUST be outermost RAII; drop infallible
tokio::spawn without with_async_context spawned task sees no context (None) async boundary breaks task-local scope "no_request_context" warn on all sub-task hook calls document: all spawned tasks MUST use with_async_context or inherit via scope
Arc<RequestContext> cloned per get_context() call atomic refcount increment on hot path get_context() returns Arc clone atomic contention on refcount use `with_context(
TaintMap shared mutably across concurrent sink hooks in same request data race single request, multiple concurrent async sinks ThreadSanitizer; logical taint inconsistency TaintMap::inner wrapped in RwLock; reads dominate
CallFrame with &'static str from macro — non-'static literal compile error or dangling ref std::file!() returns 'static; custom strings do not compiler error restrict CallFrame fields to macro-generated &'static str only
body_sample containing binary data from multipart/form-data garbled hex in logs raw bytes stored in Bytes; logged as Debug log output shows binary log body_sample as hex-encoded or redacted; add content-type check
HttpInfo header name/value containing null bytes (HTTP/2 malformed) CompactStr panics or truncates null bytes in header value from adversarial input fuzz corpus hit strip null bytes in HttpCapture::capture; log "header_sanitized"
Nested ContextGuard (middleware wraps handler which also creates guard) inner guard's drop clears context before outer guard sees it double-guard creation missing context mid-request ContextGuard::new checks if context already set; returns existing if present (re-entrant safe)

2.7 Performance Concerns

  • Thread-local read: RefCell::borrow() + Arc::clone() → ~20–50 ns total; avoid per-sink-call clone by using with_context(closure).
  • ContextGuard::new: Uuid::new_v4() (CSPRNG, ~100 ns) + Instant::now() (~30 ns) + 1 allocation for Arc<RequestContext>; amortized over request lifetime (once per request).
  • CallStack::push: ArrayVec::push = 1 bounds check + 1 copy (zero alloc); fits in L1 cache.
  • HttpInfo headers: SmallVec<[(CompactStr, CompactStr); 16]> — up to 16 headers inline (no heap); 17+ spill to heap.
  • TaintMap: HashMap backed by default hasher (FxHashMap in Phase 3); in Phase 2 it's empty on most paths; lookup is O(1) + ~5–15 ns.
  • Async task-local: tokio::task_local! access ~10 ns; scope creation ~50 ns; no lock.
  • clear_context() in drop: RefCell::borrow_mut() = None → drops Arc; if refcount reaches 0, RequestContext dropped on same thread; TaintMap + CallStack freed inline in drop — avoid large maps to prevent drop latency spike in drop path.
  • Tail latency: outlier is Uuid CSPRNG init at first call per thread; subsequent calls use pre-seeded ChaCha → amortized.

2.8 Edge Cases

  • ContextGuard::new called on thread with existing context (nested middleware) → second call detects existing context; returns a re-entrant guard that does NOT clear on drop (only the outermost guard clears).
  • get_context() called from a thread that never had a guard (background thread) → returns None; hook uses ephemeral ID.
  • HttpInfo::url > 8 KB (adversarial URL) → truncate to 8 KB, set url_truncated = true.
  • source_ip from X-Forwarded-For with multiple IPs (proxy chain) → take leftmost (client); log chain length.
  • RequestContext outlives ContextGuard due to Arc clone held in TaintMap or telemetry → context data persists; context-local state (taint) remains valid; no stale write possible (context is immutable post-request for taint reads).
  • callstack.push called from signal handler (async signal safety) — undefined behavior; document as not async-signal-safe; hook ABI MUST NOT be called from signal handlers.
  • Clock skew between Instant::now() at guard creation and later elapsed() — Instant is monotonic per-thread; no skew within request; cross-machine comparison uses wall clock from HttpInfo::timestamp (if provided).
  • TaintMap with 10 000 keys (extremely large form) → memory spike; Phase 3 introduces per-request taint budget.

2.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery Operator Signal
Stale context leak (guard not dropped) next request reads wrong HttpInfo, wrong id affected request + downstream analysis RAII enforcement; lint must_use on ContextGuard fix middleware; redeploy rasp_context_stale_reads_total counter
Thread-local poisoned (RefCell borrow panic) panic in get_context single thread; all requests on thread catch_unwind in hook_entry → fail-open restart thread or process rasp_context_borrow_panics_total
Async scope not set; all sub-tasks unprotected "no_request_context" warn for all async sinks all async sink calls pass-through (fail-open) log-level alert if warn rate > threshold wrap all spawns in with_async_context rasp_context_missing_async_total
Arc<RequestContext> not freed after request memory leak; heap grows per-leak ~8 KB; steady-state leak if many heap profiler alert (DHAT, jemalloc stats) find and drop spurious Arc clones process_resident_set_bytes rising
TaintMap RwLock writer starved insert blocks indefinitely single request taint updates stall writer priority or try_write with timeout use parking_lot::RwLock (no writer starvation) lock-wait histogram spike
CallStack overflow silently (flag not checked) missing frames in detection evidence reduced detection quality overflow flag surfaced in Detection::evidence enforce frame cap; log overflow rasp_callstack_overflow_total

2.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: rasp_context_created_total, rasp_context_cleared_total, rasp_context_stale_reads_total, rasp_context_missing_async_total, rasp_callstack_overflow_total, rasp_request_duration_seconds histogram.
  • Tracing: tracing::span!("request", id=?ctx.id, method=?ctx.http.method, url=?ctx.http.url) wraps ContextGuard lifetime; child spans for each sink call include ctx_id.
  • Logging: DEBUG for context create/clear; WARN for stale, missing, or overflow; ERROR for borrow panic.
  • Body redaction: HttpInfo::body_sample redacted (replaced with <redacted>) when rasp.context.redact_body = true config flag; default OFF (needed for taint analysis).
  • Header allowlist: configurable rasp.context.sensitive_headers = ["Authorization", "Cookie"] → values replaced with <redacted> in logs (not in taint analysis).
  • Deployment: rasp-context is a library; no configuration file in Phase 2; config flags added Phase 9.
  • Compatibility: RequestContext is #[non_exhaustive]; new fields additive; callers must not destructure.
  • Rollback: stateless library; rollback = redeploy host binary with previous rasp-context version; no persistent state.
  • Debugging: RASP_DUMP_CONTEXT=1 env var → serialize RequestContext (sans body) to stderr on each request start/end for offline analysis.

15. Phase 3 — Sink interception catalog — define + intercept dangerous sinks (SQL, exec, file, outbound HTTP, deserialize, LDAP, XXE), argument extraction + normalization

3.1 Objectives

  • Define exhaustive SinkType enum covering: SqlQuery, Exec, FileOpen, HttpRequest, Deserialize, LdapQuery, XmlParse, NoSqlQuery, TemplateRender, ReflectInvoke.
  • Implement rasp-hook: registration table, ABI entry/exit interception, safe trampoline dispatch, multi-sink fanout.
  • Implement SinkArgs extraction per sink — raw args captured at hook entry before app mutation.
  • Normalize args to canonical form (SQL text, argv vec, file path, URL, payload bytes, LDAP filter, XML string) for downstream analysis.
  • Constraint: hook ABI is extern "C" for cross-runtime linkage; no heap allocations on the hot path that can panic.
  • Constraint: arg extraction is sink-specific; adapters implement extract_args(raw: &RawArgs) -> SinkArgs.

3.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

  • rasp-hook/src/lib.rs (public surface):
pub fn register_sink(sink: SinkType, adapter: Box<dyn SinkAdapter>) -> Result<(), RaspError>;
pub extern "C" fn rasp_hook_entry(raw: *const RawHookFrame) -> HookDecision;
pub extern "C" fn rasp_hook_exit(raw: *const RawHookFrame);
  • rasp-hook/src/registry.rs: SinkRegistry: DashMap<SinkType, Vec<Box<dyn SinkAdapter>>> (lock-free read path, exclusive on registration); registration at app startup only, hot path reads without write lock; SinkAdapter trait: fn extract(&self, frame: &RawHookFrame) -> Result<SinkArgs, RaspError> + fn sink_type(&self) -> SinkType. Sink taxonomy completeness follows R6 (OWASP AppSensor detection-point taxonomy) and B8 (Secure Coding in C and C++, sink-type coverage).
  • rasp-hook/src/trampoline.rs: per-sink trampoline wraps rasp_hook_entry, catches panics via std::panic::catch_unwind, returns HookDecision::Proceed on internal error (host-safety invariant); HookDecision enum: Proceed, Block(RaspError); entry writes HookEvent { sink, args, context_id, phase: Entry, timestamp: Instant::now() } into per-request queue; exit writes HookEvent { phase: Exit, ... } for latency measurement.
  • rasp-hook/src/sink_args.rs — SinkArgs normalization per sink:
SinkType Raw input Normalized form
SqlQuery query string + param array SqlArgs { query: Cow<str>, params: SmallVec<[SqlParam; 8]> }
Exec argv0, argv vec, env ExecArgs { program: PathBuf, argv: Vec<OsString>, env_keys: Vec<String> }
FileOpen path bytes, flags FileArgs { path: PathBuf, flags: OpenFlags }
HttpRequest method, url, headers, body ref HttpArgs { method, url: Url, headers: HeaderMap, body_len: usize }
Deserialize format tag, payload bytes DeserArgs { format: DeserFormat, payload: Bytes, class_hint: Option<String> }
LdapQuery base_dn, filter str, attrs LdapArgs { base_dn: String, filter: String, attrs: Vec<String> }
XmlParse xml bytes, parser flags XmlArgs { payload: Bytes, dtd_allowed: bool, external_entities: bool }
  • Url parsing via url crate — invalid URLs produce SinkArgs::Malformed (still forwarded to detect, never dropped); Bytes extraction is a zero-copy slice reference tied to the hook frame's lifetime, copied into owned Bytes only when detection needs persistence; OpenFlags bitfield normalizes O_RDONLY | O_WRONLY | O_RDWR | O_CREAT | O_TRUNC.
  • rasp-hook/src/dispatch.rs (multi-sink dispatch): iterates adapters for SinkType from registry; calls rasp_protect::inspect(event: HookEvent) synchronously on entry; Action::Block → sets thread-local BLOCKED flag + HookDecision::Block; Action::Allow/Monitor → HookDecision::Proceed.
  • rasp-hook/src/abi.rs (extern "C" stable ABI):
#[repr(C)] pub struct RawHookFrame { sink_id: u32, args_ptr: *const u8, args_len: usize, context_id: u64 }
  • sink_id maps to SinkType via const array (no match on hot path); args_ptr valid for hook frame duration only — adapter must copy if needed past frame.
  • Test harness (rasp-hook/tests/): test_registration_roundtrip (register adapter, fire synthetic frame, assert extraction matches expected SinkArgs); test_panic_in_trampoline (adapter panics, assert HookDecision::Proceed — host-safety); test_multi_adapter_fanout (two adapters on same sink, both called, first block wins).

3.3 Deliverable

  • rasp-hook compiles; all sinks registerable; rasp_hook_entry/rasp_hook_exit callable from FFI.
  • SinkArgs structs fully populated from synthetic RawHookFrame inputs for all 8 sink types.
  • Integration smoke test: mock runtime fires 10 k hook entries; all dispatched, no panics, no memory errors under Miri.
  • Operators can inspect: registered sinks via rasp-cli sinks list; per-sink event counts in telemetry metrics.

3.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • 100% of unit tests pass (cargo test -p rasp-hook).
  • Miri clean on all extraction paths.
  • rasp_hook_entry overhead: ≤2 µs p99 on benign (no detection) path (measured in rasp-bench).
  • All 8 SinkType variants have registered adapters with round-trip SinkArgs extraction in CI.
  • Panic in any adapter → Proceed (never Block) verified by test_panic_in_trampoline.
  • No heap allocation in RawHookFrame → SinkArgs fast path for SqlQuery and Exec (verified via cargo +nightly alloc_count).

3.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture file Purpose Expected output
fixtures/hooks/sql_select_simple.bin Minimal SELECT frame SqlArgs { query: "SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = ?", params: [SqlParam::Int(1)] }
fixtures/hooks/exec_ls.bin /bin/ls -la /tmp frame ExecArgs { program: "/bin/ls", argv: ["-la", "/tmp"] }
fixtures/hooks/fileopen_etc_passwd.bin /etc/passwd O_RDONLY FileArgs { path: "/etc/passwd", flags: O_RDONLY }
fixtures/hooks/http_ssrf_candidate.bin URL http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/ HttpArgs { url parsed, method GET }
fixtures/hooks/deser_java_object.bin Java serialized stream header AC ED 00 05 DeserArgs { format: JavaObject, payload: Bytes }
fixtures/hooks/ldap_filter_injection.bin (&(uid=*)(userPassword=*)) filter LdapArgs { filter: "..." }
fixtures/hooks/xml_dtd_bomb.bin XML with DOCTYPE + external entity XmlArgs { dtd_allowed: false triggers warn }
fixtures/hooks/malformed_url.bin Non-UTF-8 URL bytes SinkArgs::Malformed — no panic, forwarded
fixtures/hooks/empty_sql.bin Zero-length query SqlArgs { query: "" } — forwarded, detect handles

3.6 Pitfalls

  1. Adapter registered after first request: race between late registration and live traffic → detection misses sink. Root cause: DashMap read-before-write. Debug: rasp.sink.miss counter increments. Fix: require registration complete before app accepts connections (enforced by rasp-cli validate pre-flight).
  2. arg_ptr lifetime escape: adapter stores raw pointer beyond hook frame → use-after-free. Manifestation: heap corruption under load. Debug: Miri catches in test; ASAN at runtime. Recovery: SinkAdapter::extract must copy to owned types before returning.
  3. Trampoline re-entrancy: hook fires while inside another hook (e.g., logging SQL inside HTTP handler which calls SQL). Manifestation: stack overflow or deadlock on BLOCKED flag. Fix: thread_local! { HOOK_DEPTH: Cell<u8> } — skip re-entrant depth > 2.
  4. sink_id out of range: buggy runtime adapter passes unknown sink_id. Manifestation: index OOB panic. Fix: const array lookup returns None → SinkArgs::Unknown(id) → log + proceed.
  5. Normalization of non-UTF-8 paths: Windows MBCS or malformed UTF-8 in FileArgs::path. Manifestation: silent truncation or panic. Fix: OsString throughout; convert to String with lossy only for logging.
  6. Clock skew on timestamp: Instant::now() at entry vs exit spanning thread migration on heterogeneous cores. Manifestation: negative latency in telemetry. Fix: use monotonic clock; clamp negative deltas to zero in telemetry.

3.7 Performance Concerns

  • Hot path allocation: SinkArgs enums must fit in 128 bytes on stack; SmallVec<[SqlParam; 8]> avoids heap for typical parameterized queries.
  • DashMap sharding: default 16 shards; contention spike at startup registration. Pre-size to expected adapter count.
  • Trampoline overhead: catch_unwind costs ~50 ns even without panic; benchmark confirms < 200 ns total for benign dispatch.
  • Instant::now() syscall: Linux CLOCK_MONOTONIC_COARSE via vDSO, ~10 ns; use Instant (wraps vDSO on Linux).
  • Arg normalization: URL parse (url crate) ~1–3 µs; skip if taint tracking shows args are clean (short-circuit in rasp-protect).
  • Multi-adapter fanout: iterate adapter vec; for typical 1-adapter case, direct call, no vec alloc.

3.8 Edge Cases

  • Zero-arg Exec call (empty argv vec): ExecArgs { argv: [] } — valid, forward to detect.
  • SQL query > 64 KB: truncate to 64 KB + append [TRUNCATED] marker; record truncated: true in SqlArgs.
  • FileOpen with null bytes in path: PathBuf::from_bytes returns error → SinkArgs::Malformed.
  • Deserialize with empty payload (0 bytes): DeserArgs { payload: Bytes::new() } — forwarded, detect returns Action::Allow.
  • Concurrent sink registration from multiple threads during startup: DashMap write lock per insert; order nondeterministic but safe.
  • Hook fires with context_id = 0 (no active request): rasp-context returns RequestContext::ambient() — global/background context, not attributed to any request.

3.9 Failure Analysis

  • Full adapter crash (adapter impl SinkAdapter panics every call): catch_unwind in trampoline catches all; HookDecision::Proceed; rasp.hook.adapter_panic counter increments; alert fires at threshold 10/min; blast radius = that sink type unguarded (monitor gap, not availability impact).
  • Registry corruption (DashMap poisoned): impossible (no lock poisoning in DashMap); RwLock variant would propagate poison — rejected in favor of DashMap.
  • Trampoline stack overflow (re-entrant depth): depth guard returns Proceed at depth > 2; log rasp.hook.reentrant counter.
  • ABI mismatch (runtime adapter passes wrong args_len): bounds check in extract; mismatch → SinkArgs::Malformed + rasp.hook.abi_mismatch counter.
  • Operator visibility: all failure modes surface as labeled Prometheus counters; alert rules in rasp-telemetry/alerts/hooks.yaml.

3.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics exported: rasp_hook_entry_total{sink}, rasp_hook_decision{sink, decision}, rasp_hook_adapter_panic{sink}, rasp_hook_abi_mismatch{sink}, rasp_hook_entry_latency_us{sink, quantile}.
  • Tracing: tracing::span!(Level::TRACE, "hook.entry", sink=?event.sink, ctx=event.context_id) — disabled by default, toggled by RASP_TRACE_HOOKS=1.
  • Deployment: adapters registered at app init via rasp_hook::register_sink; no hot reload in Phase 3 (planned Phase 11).
  • Rollback: adapter deregistration not supported in Phase 3; full app restart required. rasp-cli sinks list shows active adapters.
  • Compatibility: RawHookFrame ABI versioned by abi_version: u16 field; mismatch emits error + proceeds (host-safety).

16. Phase 4 — Taint tracking core — taint sources (HTTP params/headers/body/path), tags, propagation through string ops, sink-time taint check, bounded overhead

4.1 Objectives

  • Implement rasp-taint crate: TaintSource, TaintTag, TaintedValue, TaintMap with full propagation through common string operations.
  • Mark HTTP inputs as taint sources at RequestContext injection time.
  • Propagate taint through: concat, slice, format, to_lowercase/uppercase, trim, split, replace, URL-decode, base64-decode.
  • Implement sink-time taint check: fn check_tainted(val: &str, map: &TaintMap) -> Option<Vec<TaintTag>>.
  • Bounded-cost invariant: propagation budget per request (MAX_TAINT_OPS: usize = 4096); budget exhaustion → conservative (all remaining values treated as tainted or tracking disabled per policy).
  • Constraint: no per-character allocation; taint carried as Vec<TaintTag> on TaintedValue, not inline in String.
  • Constraint: TaintMap is per-request; no cross-request sharing (context isolation invariant).

4.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

  • rasp-taint/src/types.rs:
pub enum TaintSource { HttpParam, HttpHeader, HttpBody, HttpPath, Cookie }
pub struct TaintTag { pub source: TaintSource, pub byte_range: Range<usize>, pub param_name: Option<Arc<str>> }
pub struct TaintedValue { pub value: Bytes, pub tags: SmallVec<[TaintTag; 4]> }

byte_range tracks which bytes of the ORIGINAL source string are present in the derived value; TaintedValue::is_clean() = tags.is_empty(). Byte-range granularity follows P11 (Newsome & Song, TaintCheck) and operationalizes the noninterference property behind §7 Invariant 7.5 (Taint Soundness Bounded), rooted in P8 (Denning & Denning, information-flow lattice).

  • rasp-taint/src/map.rs (TaintMap):
pub struct TaintMap { inner: HashMap<TaintKey, TaintedValue>, ops_budget: Cell<usize> }
pub enum TaintKey { Param(Arc<str>), Header(HeaderName), BodyField(Arc<str>), PathSegment(u8), Cookie(Arc<str>) }

keyed by TaintKey, HashMap with AHasher (cache-friendly, no RNG seeding on insert); ops_budget decremented on each propagation call, 0 → budget_exhausted() → Err(TaintBudgetExhausted); TaintMap::new(budget) defaults MAX_TAINT_OPS = 4096.

  • rasp-taint/src/sources.rs (source injection): inject_http(ctx: &mut RequestContext, req: &HttpInfo) iterates req.params, req.headers, req.path_segments, req.cookies, req.body_fields, creates a TaintedValue per item, inserts into ctx.taint_map; byte_range set to 0..value.len() at injection (full string tainted at source); param name stored in TaintTag::param_name for Detection evidence.
  • rasp-taint/src/propagate.rs — propagation operations:
Op Input Output taint rule
concat(a, b) TaintedValue a, TaintedValue b Merge tags; shift byte_range of b tags by a.len()
slice(v, start, end) TaintedValue v Filter tags overlapping [start,end); adjust ranges
to_lower/upper(v) TaintedValue v Tags preserved (byte positions unchanged for ASCII; Unicode: conservative, keep full range)
trim(v) TaintedValue v Shift ranges by leading whitespace removed
split(v, sep) -> Vec TaintedValue v Propagate each segment with adjusted ranges
replace(v, pat, rep) TaintedValue v Conservative: if pat could be tainted, output fully tainted
url_decode(v) TaintedValue v Taint preserved; decoded form may be shorter → byte_range remapped
base64_decode(v) TaintedValue v Conservative: output fully tainted if input has any tag
format!(tmpl, args..) N args Union of all arg tags applied to full output string

Each op checks budget, decrements, computes, returns Result<TaintedValue, TaintBudgetExhausted>; budget decrement is 1 per op (not per byte).

  • rasp-taint/src/check.rs (sink-time check):
pub fn check_tainted(val: &str, map: &TaintMap, key: &TaintKey) -> Option<Vec<TaintTag>>
pub fn check_tainted_bytes(val: &[u8], map: &TaintMap, key: &TaintKey) -> Option<Vec<TaintTag>>

looks up key in map; if present and !is_clean(), returns Some(tags.to_vec()); also does a substring check (if val is a suffix/substring of a tainted value, propagate — configurable, default off for performance); returns None if clean or key not in map.

  • rasp-taint/tests/: test_inject_http_params (inject 3 params, assert all keys in map with full byte_range); test_concat_propagation (concat tainted + clean → output tags cover first segment only); test_slice_propagation (slice [3..7] of tainted [0..10] → tag range [0..4]); test_budget_exhaustion (fire 4097 ops, assert TaintBudgetExhausted on 4097th); test_context_isolation (two parallel RequestContexts, taint in one not visible in other); test_clean_short_circuit (untainted value → check_tainted returns None in <50 ns).

4.3 Deliverable

  • rasp-taint compiles; TaintMap injectable from RequestContext via rasp-context integration.
  • HTTP request fixture → taint map populated with all params/headers/cookies tagged.
  • Propagation through 5 string ops verified via unit tests.
  • check_tainted returns correct TaintTag vec with source + byte_range for all fixture cases.
  • Operators can inspect: per-request taint summary in debug log (RASP_LOG_TAINT=1); rasp.taint.ops_total, rasp.taint.budget_exhausted_total metrics.

4.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • All rasp-taint unit tests pass; Miri clean.
  • check_tainted on untainted value: ≤100 ns (HashMap lookup only).
  • inject_http on request with 20 params + 10 headers: ≤5 µs total.
  • Budget exhaustion path tested; no panic; returns Err correctly.
  • Context isolation verified: 1 000 parallel fake contexts; no cross-contamination (randomized test with 3 seeds).
  • Propagation ops each tested with tainted + clean inputs; range arithmetic verified against 50 hand-computed fixtures.

4.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
fixtures/taint/inject_get_params.json ?id=1&name=alice Map keys: Param("id"), Param("name"); full ranges
fixtures/taint/inject_headers.json X-Custom: injected Map key Header("x-custom"); full range
fixtures/taint/concat_tainted_clean.json concat(tainted("ab"), clean("cd")) Output tags cover bytes [0..2] only
fixtures/taint/slice_interior.json slice(tainted[0..10], 3, 7) Output tag [0..4]
fixtures/taint/url_decode_tainted.json url_decode("%3Cscript%3E") Output tainted; decoded form <script>
fixtures/taint/budget_exhaustion.json 4097 concat ops 4097th returns Err(TaintBudgetExhausted)
fixtures/taint/malformed_utf8_param.bin Non-UTF-8 param bytes TaintedValue { value: Bytes, tags: [full range] } — no panic
fixtures/taint/empty_param.json id= (empty value) TaintedValue { value: Bytes::new(), tags: [0..0] }
fixtures/taint/unicode_case_fold.json Turkish I lowercased Conservative: full range tainted

4.6 Pitfalls

  1. byte_range drift after Unicode normalization: NFC normalization changes byte lengths; byte_range becomes stale → false negatives at sink. Root cause: ops assume 1:1 byte mapping. Fix: Unicode-aware ops use conservative full-range taint; document which ops are conservative.
  2. TaintMap not cleared between requests: thread-local map reused without reset → taint leak across requests (context isolation violation). Debug: rasp.taint.context_leak counter (compare request IDs on map access). Fix: RequestContext::reset() clears TaintMap at request start.
  3. Budget exhaustion → silent false negatives: budget hits 0 mid-request; remaining sinks see no taint → attacker exploits complex propagation to exhaust budget before target sink. Mitigation: budget_exhausted policy: ConservativeBlock (all sinks treated as tainted) vs Allow (default); configurable per Policy.
  4. AHasher seed not randomized across restarts: same seed → HashDoS via crafted param names filling one bucket. Fix: ahash::RandomState::new() (per-map random seed, not global).
  5. SmallVec spill: >4 tags on one TaintedValue (e.g., path + param + header all contribute via format!) → heap alloc on hot path. Acceptable; document; monitor via rasp.taint.tag_spill_total.
  6. Split propagation explosion: split(",", large_tainted_string) → 1000 segments, each a TaintedValue. Budget consumed rapidly. Mitigation: count each segment as 1 op; at ≥100 segments, conservatively taint all.

4.7 Performance Concerns

  • HashMap insert per param: AHasher ~10 ns/insert; 20 params = ~200 ns inject total — acceptable.
  • Tag vec allocation: SmallVec<[TaintTag; 4]> — stack-resident for ≤4 tags (typical); heap only for multi-source propagation.
  • check_tainted on every sink arg: O(1) HashMap lookup; skip if map is empty (fast path for clean requests).
  • ops_budget cell contention: Cell<usize> (non-atomic); per-thread, no contention. Async: stored in task-local; no cross-task sharing.
  • base64_decode + url_decode conservatism: avoids byte-position remapping cost; trades precision for speed. Revisit in Phase 10 (optimization pass).
  • Propagation on every string op: not all app string ops go through RASP; only explicit rasp_taint::ops::* calls from instrumented code. Adapter coverage gap is a documented limitation.

4.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty string param (id=): TaintedValue { value: Bytes::new(), tags: [0..0] } — tagged but zero-length; check_tainted returns Some([]) (tagged, trivially).
  • Param value = 64 KB+: tag covers 0..65536; truncated value still fully tagged.
  • Async task migration between OS threads: TaintMap in task-local storage (tokio::task_local!) — migrates with task, no context loss.
  • Request with 0 params, 0 headers: TaintMap::new() with empty inner; check_tainted always returns None; inject_http is no-op.
  • Concurrent access to same TaintMap (should not occur — isolation invariant): TaintMap is !Sync; compiler enforces.
  • byte_range overflow: param value ≥ usize::MAX bytes — impossible in practice; assert value.len() < u32::MAX at injection.

4.9 Failure Analysis

  • inject_http panics (bug in source iterator): catch_unwind in rasp-protect call path; budget exhausted → ConservativeBlock or Allow per policy; rasp.taint.inject_error_total counter.
  • TaintMap memory leak (map not cleared): detected by monotone growth of rasp.taint.map_size_bytes metric; alert at >1 MB per-request map; postmortem: check RequestContext::reset() call site in adapter.
  • Propagation logic bug → false negatives: taint not propagated through a new op; attacker bypasses via that op. Detection: red-team test suite (fixtures/taint/regression/); FP/FN rate tracked per release.
  • Budget set too low → legitimate request blocked (ConservativeBlock mode): rasp.taint.budget_exhausted_total spikes; operator raises MAX_TAINT_OPS in config or switches to Allow exhaustion policy.

4.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: rasp_taint_inject_params_total, rasp_taint_ops_total{op}, rasp_taint_budget_exhausted_total, rasp_taint_map_size_bytes{quantile}, rasp_taint_tag_spill_total.
  • Debug logging: RASP_LOG_TAINT=1 emits per-request taint map summary at request end (source count, op count, budget remaining).
  • rasp-cli taint dump --request-id <id> (requires debug telemetry buffer): prints taint map + propagation trace for post-incident analysis.
  • Config: taint.max_ops: 4096, taint.budget_exhaustion_policy: Allow|ConservativeBlock, taint.substring_check: false.
  • Upgrade path: bump max_ops if budget_exhausted_total > 0.1% of requests; re-evaluate propagation coverage in Phase 10.

17. Phase 5 — SQL injection detection — query tokenization/lexing, injected-syntax vs parameterized, taint-reaches-keyword, confidence scoring; monitor mode

5.1 Objectives

  • Implement rasp-detect::sqli module: SQLi analyzer consuming SqlArgs + TaintedValue → Option<Detection>.
  • Tokenize/lex SQL query string into SqlToken stream (keyword, ident, literal, operator, comment, whitespace, error).
  • Detection strategy 1 — structural analysis: taint reaches into a SQL keyword / comment / operator position → high-confidence injection.
  • Detection strategy 2 — syntax change detection: tokenize query with and without tainted segments; if token types differ at tainted positions, injection.
  • Detection strategy 3 — libinjection heuristic port: fingerprint-based SQLi scoring (ported pattern set, not FFI to C lib — memory safety).
  • Parameterized query fast path: SqlArgs::params non-empty + query contains only ?/$N placeholders at tainted positions → Action::Allow (no taint in query structure).
  • Output: Detection { rule_id, sink: SqlQuery, severity, confidence: f32 [0.0,1.0], evidence: String, taint_path: Vec<TaintTag> }.
  • Monitor mode: emit Detection but return Action::Monitor (no blocking); Block mode: threshold confidence ≥ 0.85.

5.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

  • rasp-detect/src/sqli/mod.rs (public API):
pub fn analyze(args: &SqlArgs, taint: Option<&[TaintTag]>, policy: &Policy) -> Option<Detection>

returns None if taint.is_none() or empty (short-circuit — untainted args never SQLi); calls lexer → struct_check → syntax_diff → fingerprint, picks max-confidence detection.

  • rasp-detect/src/sqli/lexer.rs (SqlLexer):
pub fn tokenize(query: &str) -> Vec<SqlToken>
pub enum SqlToken { Keyword(KeywordKind), Ident(Range<usize>), Literal(Range<usize>), Op(OpKind), Comment(Range<usize>), Whitespace, Error(Range<usize>) }
pub enum KeywordKind { Select, Insert, Update, Delete, Drop, Union, Or, And, Exec, Xp, Cast, Convert, Sleep, Benchmark, ... }

hand-written DFA, no regex (predictable latency, no ReDoS); case-insensitive keyword matching via to_ascii_uppercase on first char + match table; comment styles --, /* */, MySQL #; unknown/unclassifiable byte sequence → Error token (still tracked — may be obfuscation); consecutive whitespace → single Whitespace token, NUL bytes → Error.

  • rasp-detect/src/sqli/structural.rs: maps each TaintTag::byte_range to SqlToken positions; tainted byte within Keyword(_)/Op(Comment*) ⇒ confidence=0.95, rule_id="SQLI-001-KEYWORD-TAINT"; tainted byte in Literal(_) only ⇒ confidence=0.2 (low, may be parameterized-like); tainted bytes spanning Literal→Op/Keyword boundary ⇒ confidence=0.90, rule_id="SQLI-002-BOUNDARY-ESCAPE".
  • rasp-detect/src/sqli/syntax_diff.rs: redact_tainted(query, tags) -> String replaces tainted ranges with ? placeholder; tokenizes both original and redacted, compares token-type sequences; differing token type at any position ⇒ injection confirmed, confidence=0.92, rule_id="SQLI-003-SYNTAX-CHANGE"; cost is 2× tokenization (both strings), acceptable for ≤64 KB queries (typical <1 µs).
  • rasp-detect/src/sqli/fingerprint.rs (libinjection-style fingerprint): computes a token-type fingerprint string (e.g. "kokso" = keyword-op-keyword-string-op); matches against a static phf::Map<&str, f32> compiled from libinjection's fingerprint file (~7700 patterns, vendored in rasp-detect/data/sqli_fingerprints.txt); phf = perfect hash, zero runtime allocation, O(1) lookup; match ⇒ confidence = phf_confidence_value, rule_id = "SQLI-004-FINGERPRINT-{fp}". This fingerprint strategy directly implements P1 (Sounthiraraj et al. / Galbreath, libinjection) and is the locked choice recorded in ADR-009 (SQLi detection algorithm).
  • rasp-detect/src/sqli/scoring.rs (confidence aggregation):
pub fn aggregate(scores: &[(f32, &str)]) -> (f32, &str)

takes max; if two strategies agree (both > 0.7), boost by 0.05, cap at 1.0; severity: High if confidence ≥ 0.85, Medium if ≥ 0.5, else Low.

  • rasp-detect/src/sqli/paramcheck.rs (parameterized query fast path): if args.params.len() > 0 and the query contains only bind-param markers at positions where taint reaches, returns Action::Allow early with no detection; is_bind_marker_at(query, range) -> bool checks ? or $\d+ in range.
  • Test harness (rasp-detect/tests/sqli/): test_classic_or_1eq1 (... WHERE id = '1' OR '1'='1', taint covers ' OR '1'='1', expect confidence ≥ 0.9); test_union_select (tainted UNION SELECT username,password FROM users appended, expect SQLI-001/SQLI-003 ≥ 0.9); test_comment_termination (1'; -- tainted, expect SQLI-002 ≥ 0.85); test_parameterized_clean (WHERE id = ?, params=[1], taint on ? position, expect Allow); test_benign_name (WHERE name = 'Alice', taint covers Alice, expect confidence < 0.3); test_stacked_queries (1; DROP TABLE users-- tainted, expect SQLI-001 ≥ 0.95); test_obfuscated_comment (1/**/OR/**/1=1 tainted, expect detection via comment tokens); test_unicode_bypass (SEL\u{1}ECT — lexer emits Error token; conservative flag).

5.3 Deliverable

  • rasp-detect::sqli::analyze compiles and passes all unit tests.
  • Attack corpus (25 classic SQLi payloads from OWASP cheat sheet) + benign corpus (25 legitimate query patterns) both processed; FP/FN rates measured.
  • Parameterized fast path verified: 100% of parameterized fixtures return Action::Allow.
  • Monitor mode functional: all detections emitted as Detection with evidence; no request blocked in Monitor mode.
  • rasp-bench sqli reports per-fixture latency; p99 ≤ 10 µs for queries ≤ 4 KB.

5.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • Attack corpus: detection rate ≥ 95% (≥ 24/25 fixtures at confidence ≥ 0.85).
  • Benign corpus: FP rate ≤ 2% (≤ 1/25 false detections).
  • Parameterized fast path: 100% allow rate on 25 parameterized fixtures.
  • p99 latency for SQLi analysis on 4 KB query: ≤ 10 µs (measured rasp-bench sqli).
  • No panics under Miri; no allocations in fast path (untainted args).
  • Monitor mode: all detections logged; Action::Monitor returned; app behavior unaltered.
  • Lexer handles all 8 comment/whitespace/obfuscation styles in sqli_edge_cases.json.

Fixture Purpose Expected detection
fixtures/sqli/or_1eq1.json Classic ' OR '1'='1 SQLI-002, confidence ≥ 0.90
fixtures/sqli/union_select.json UNION SELECT appended SQLI-001 or SQLI-003 ≥ 0.90

fixtures/sqli/unicode_bypass.json Null byte + homoglyph obfuscation Error token path; conservative flag
fixtures/sqli/64kb_query.json 64 KB query string Completes ≤ 10 µs; no OOM
fixtures/sqli/empty_query.json Zero-length query None detection; no panic
fixtures/sqli/no_taint.json Full query, no taint tags Fast-path None in < 100 ns

5.6 Pitfalls

  1. ReDoS in lexer: naive regex-based lexer → crafted query causes exponential backtracking. Fix: hand-written DFA; no regex. Verify with regexplode or equivalent.
  2. Fingerprint false negatives via encoding: hex_encode, char() bypass structural analysis; fingerprint must include encoded-form patterns. Mitigation: pre-decode 0x... hex literals before fingerprinting.
  3. Parameterized false negative: app concatenates unsanitized input even with ? markers in other positions (mixed query). paramcheck only fires when ALL tainted bytes map to bind markers; partial match does not suppress analysis.
  4. Confidence threshold drift: setting threshold too high (0.95) → low-confidence real attacks pass in Block mode. Operator must tune; default 0.85 is calibrated on OWASP corpus. Monitor-first deployment recommended to calibrate before enabling Block.
  5. Binary column data misidentified: app stores binary (e.g., image bytes) in a column; SqlArgs::query includes that literal in the generated SQL. Lexer emits Error token; confidence < 0.3; no block. Edge: if binary data happens to contain SQL keywords — conservative but low confidence, does not block.
  6. Unicode SQL keywords (MySQL ＳＥＬＥＣＴ): full-width characters not in lexer keyword set → Ident token → missed keyword detection. Mitigation: normalize full-width ASCII before lexing; document as known gap.
  7. Comment-in-keyword obfuscation (SEL/**/ECT): lexer must recognize SEL as partial ident, not keyword — syntax diff approach catches this because redaction changes token structure. Structural analysis alone may miss; rely on multi-strategy scoring.

5.7 Performance Concerns

  • Lexer throughput: hand-written DFA on 4 KB query: ~200 ns; 64 KB: ~3 µs. Single allocation for Vec<SqlToken> pre-allocated to query.len() / 8 (empirical token density).
  • Fingerprint lookup: phf::Map lookup: O(1), ~10 ns, zero allocation.
  • Syntax diff: two tokenizations + sequence comparison: 2× lexer cost + O(n) comparison = ~400 ns for 4 KB.
  • Short-circuit: if taint.is_none(), entire analyzer is skipped before any allocation (~5 ns HashMap miss).
  • Confidence aggregation: 3 scores, max + possible boost: O(1), branch-free.
  • Combined budget: structural + syntax diff + fingerprint for 4 KB: ≤ 1 µs typical, ≤ 10 µs p99 budget.
  • Cache: phf::Map in .rodata; likely L2-resident for active workloads — no cold-start penalty after first request.

5.8 Edge Cases

  • Query is entirely tainted (full query from user input — broken app): all tokens tainted → SQLI-001 at 0.95; block immediately in Block mode.
  • Empty query string: tokenize("") → empty vec; analyze returns None.
  • Query with only whitespace: tokens = [Whitespace]; None detection.
  • Taint tag with byte_range = 0..0 (empty taint, see Phase 4 edge case): no structural match; confidence = 0.0; None.
  • SQL dialect variation: || string concat (PostgreSQL) vs + (TSQL) — lexer recognizes both as Op; structural check applies regardless.
  • Very long comment /* ... 100KB ... */: lexer emits one Comment token; skipped in structural analysis (comments not SQL-executable); but taint-in-comment → lower-confidence detection.
  • Taint starts mid-keyword (e.g., taint covers NION in UNION): byte_range maps to Keyword(Union) token → detected; SQLI-001.
  • NUL byte in query: lexer emits Error; note in evidence; conservative flag in detection (does not suppress).

5.9 Failure Analysis

  • Lexer crash on malformed input: DFA is total-function (all inputs produce a token stream); Error tokens absorb unknown bytes. Impossible to panic from well-formed Rust.
  • phf map compile failure (fingerprint file corruption): build-time failure, not runtime; CI catches before deployment.
  • False positive spike (new app query pattern misidentified): rasp.detect.sqli.fp_suspected counter (operator marks FP in waiver); add waiver to policy; SQLI-FP-WAIVER rule suppresses.
  • False negative on novel attack: detection rate drops below 95% in regression suite after query pattern change → CI fails; rollback fingerprint set to previous version.
  • Monitor → Block mode transition: operator flips policy.mode = Block; no restart required (policy hot-reloaded Phase 11); first block event logged to audit; rasp.policy.block_total counter.
  • Blast radius of complete analyzer failure: catch_unwind in rasp-protect; returns Action::Allow on panic (fail-open) or Action::Block (fail-closed) per Policy::fail_mode; rasp.detect.analyzer_panic_total counter.

5.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: rasp_detect_sqli_total{result: detected|allowed|fp_waived}, rasp_detect_sqli_confidence{quantile}, rasp_detect_sqli_latency_us{quantile}, rasp_detect_sqli_rule{rule_id}.
  • Tracing: tracing::event!(Level::INFO, rule_id, confidence, evidence) on detection; Level::DEBUG on allow.
  • SIEM export: Detection serialized to CEF via rasp-telemetry; CEF:0|RASP|340|1.0|SQLI|SQL Injection|High|....
  • Monitor-first deployment: ship with policy.mode = Monitor; review rasp_detect_sqli_total for 1–2 weeks; confirm FP rate < 2%; then switch to Block.
  • Waiver workflow: rasp-cli policy add-waiver --rule SQLI-001 --request-pattern "/api/admin/raw-query" --expires 2025-12-31 — suppresses detection for known-safe raw query endpoint.
  • Fingerprint update path: replace rasp-detect/data/sqli_fingerprints.txt; cargo build regenerates phf::Map; redeploy binary; no config change.
  • False-negative reporting: rasp-cli detect test --fixture <file> runs analyzer against a fixture; reports confidence + rule; used by red team to validate detection before production rollout.

18. Phase 6 — Command injection + path traversal — shell metachar/argv analysis + taint into exec; path normalization + escape detection for file sinks

6.1 Objectives

  • Implement CmdInjectionAnalyzer in rasp-detect/src/cmd_inject.rs: detect shell metacharacters and argument boundary violations in SinkType::Exec hook events.
  • Implement PathTraversalAnalyzer in rasp-detect/src/path_traversal.rs: detect ../ escape, symlink escape, NUL byte, and drive-prefix tricks in SinkType::FileOpen hook events.
  • Extend rasp-taint: add propagate_concat and propagate_format to track taint through string interpolation into OS command/path strings.
  • Extend rasp-hook: register Exec and FileOpen sink types in the hook dispatch table; validate SinkArgs variants match registered types.
  • Integrate both analyzers into rasp-protect's inline guard pipeline (after SQLi from Phase 5); share RequestContext resolution and taint checks.
  • Constraint: no shell spawn or filesystem stat in the hot path — analysis is purely string-structural + taint-metadata; path normalization uses byte-level ops only.
  • Overhead budget: <1.5 µs p99 added latency per Exec call; <0.8 µs p99 per FileOpen call on benign (untainted) args (short-circuit path).

6.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

  • rasp-core (extend enums/types): SinkArgs::Exec { program: &str, argv: &[&str], env: Option<&[(&str,&str)]> } (argv[0] = program, argv[1..] = arguments); SinkArgs::FileOpen { path: &str, flags: u32 } (raw path string before OS resolution); add DetectionKind::CmdInjection, DetectionKind::PathTraversal to Rule.kind (extends existing Signature|Semantic).
  • rasp-taint (new ops in propagate.rs): propagate_concat(a, b) -> TaintedValue unions tags, shifts b's byte_range by a.value.len(); propagate_format(template, args) -> TaintedValue per-arg offset tracking via format_offsets(template, args); propagate_split(src, sep) -> Vec<TaintedValue> for argv split detection; chain depth capped at ≤64 nodes (taint.max_chain_depth), excess → TaintTag { source: Saturated, .. }.
  • rasp-detect/src/cmd_inject.rs (CmdInjectionAnalyzer): fields shell_meta_set: AsciiSet (;|&$\(){}[]<>!?*), arg_merge_re: Regex(precompiled, cached per instance);analyze(event, ctx) -> Option<Detection>— (1) short-circuit if no argv entry has aTaintedValueinctx.taint_map⇒None; (2) scan each tainted argv[i]'s taint ranges for shell_meta_setchars ⇒MetacharFound; (3) if argv.len()==1(program only) and program is tainted ⇒ProgramTaint(highest severity); (4)ShellArgMerge: multiple argv coalesced into one string with metachar at a taint boundary ⇒ ArgBoundaryViolation; (5) semantic check tokenizes argv[0] basename against known shells (sh,bash,zsh,cmd,powershell) to escalate confidence; (6) score: Criticalif metachar in tainted range,Highif ProgramTaint, confidence = tainted_bytes/total_arg_bytes; (7) buildDetection { rule_id, sink: Exec, severity, confidence, evidence: EvidenceList, taint_path }`. Metachar/argv analysis draws on B4 (Hacking: The Art of Exploitation, Ch. 4 shellcode/injection) and applies §6.10 (Semantic Detection Over Pattern Matching) rather than raw regex.
  • rasp-detect/src/path_traversal.rs (PathTraversalAnalyzer): normalize_path(raw) -> (String, Vec<TraversalSignal>) — signals DotDot (..), NulByte (\0), AbsoluteEscape (starts / after taint-injected prefix), DrivePrefix (C:\), UncPath (\\), SymlinkSuspect (/proc/self etc.), DoubleSlash, EncodedDot (%2e%2e,%252e); normalization collapses duplicate /, resolves . segments, expands %xx one pass (no recursion >1 level, avoids ReDoS); analyze(event, ctx) -> Option<Detection> — (1) short-circuit if no taint in path arg ⇒ None; (2) normalize_path + collect signals; (3) intersect taint byte ranges with signal positions to confirm injection; (4) validate canonical path within policy.allowed_root_paths (allowlist from rasp-policy) — escape ⇒ EscapeDetected; (5) severity Critical for DotDot+NulByte, High for DotDot/AbsoluteEscape, Medium for DrivePrefix/UncPath; (6) build Detection with evidence carrying normalized path + signal set + taint byte ranges.
  • rasp-protect (pipeline extension, guard.rs): adds match arms SinkType::Exec => guard.analyze_exec(event, ctx) and SinkType::FileOpen => guard.analyze_file(event, ctx), both sharing resolve_context(context_id) + lookup_taint(ctx, args); analyze_exec/analyze_file call their analyzers and route Detection to rasp-policy::evaluate.
  • rasp-hook (registration): register_sink(SinkType::Exec, cmd_inject_hook_fn), register_sink(SinkType::FileOpen, path_traversal_hook_fn); hook_fn signature unsafe extern "C" fn(event_ptr: *const HookEvent) -> ActionCode maps ActionCode → Action.
  • Tests (rasp-detect/tests/cmd_inject_tests.rs, path_traversal_tests.rs): positive "ls; rm -rf /" in tainted argv[1] ⇒ Detection{Critical}; negative ["ls","-la","/tmp"] all untainted ⇒ None; positive path tainted "../../../etc/passwd" ⇒ Detection; negative path benign untainted /var/app/data/report.pdf ⇒ None.

6.3 Deliverable

  • rasp-detect: CmdInjectionAnalyzer + PathTraversalAnalyzer compile, pass unit tests.
  • rasp-taint: propagate_concat, propagate_format, propagate_split implemented and tested.
  • rasp-hook: Exec and FileOpen sink types registered, dispatch verified via integration test.
  • rasp-protect: end-to-end guard pipeline handles Exec and FileOpen events, emits Detection or Allow.
  • rasp-bench: benign Exec + FileOpen overhead measured at <1.5 µs and <0.8 µs p99 respectively.
  • Operator-visible: rasp-telemetry emits cmd_injection_detected_total, path_traversal_detected_total counters; per-detection log with rule_id, signal_set, taint_path.

Criterion Target

cargo test -p rasp-taint (propagation suite) 100% pass
CmdInjection true-positive rate on corpus ≥ 98% (corpus: fixtures/cmd_inject_corpus.toml)

Filename Purpose Expected Output
fixtures/cmd_inject/inject_semicolon.toml ;-separated payload in tainted argv Detection{severity:Critical, kind:CmdInjection}
fixtures/cmd_inject/inject_backtick.toml backtick subshell in tainted arg Detection{severity:Critical}
fixtures/cmd_inject/inject_pipe.toml ` ` pipe metachar
fixtures/cmd_inject/inject_program_taint.toml program itself is tainted Detection{severity:Critical, confidence>=0.9}

fixtures/cmd_inject/benign_grep_untainted.toml grep with static pattern None

fixtures/propagation/chain_overflow.toml chain depth 65 → Saturated tag TaintTag{source:Saturated}

6.6 Pitfalls

  1. Metachar in untainted segment false-positive: path has ; from static template, taint byte_range doesn't cover it → mistakenly flagged. Root cause: range check skipped. Debug: log (taint_range, signal_pos) pairs. Signal: high FP rate metric. Fix: strict range intersection check before raising signal.
  2. Percent-encoding double-decode ReDoS: normalizer applies %xx expansion in a loop; crafted input %252525...2e expands exponentially. Root cause: recursive decode. Debug: single-pass decoder, cap iterations at 3. Signal: hook latency spike. Fix: decode_depth_limit = 1 hard cap.
  3. argv coalescence detection gap: app builds command as "ls " + user_input then passes as single argv[0] without shell. RASP sees single arg, metachar present → correctly flagged. But if app uses execvp with proper argv split, each arg may look clean individually — taint boundary check needed across argv[i] boundaries. Fix: cross-argv taint-boundary-and-metachar scan.
  4. Symlink resolution in path check: allowed root is /var/app; symlink /var/app/link -> /etc bypasses canonical path check. Root cause: no realpath in hot path (syscall). Mitigation: flag known high-risk symlink directories (/proc/self, /dev/fd) as SymlinkSuspect signal; document that full symlink resolution requires async stat outside inline guard.
  5. NUL byte truncation discrepancy: some runtimes null-terminate the path before RASP sees it; NUL-byte injection silently truncated. Root cause: adapter layer strips \0. Debug: verify SinkArgs::FileOpen.path includes raw bytes via adapter test. Signal: NUL-byte fixture returns None unexpectedly.
  6. Chain-depth Saturated tag → false-negative: taint travels 65+ ops, marked Saturated; downstream sink check may treat Saturated as untainted. Fix: policy must treat Saturated as tainted (conservative fallback). Add treat_saturated_as_tainted: bool to Policy.
  7. Taint propagation format offset mis-tracking: propagate_format miscalculates offset when template has multi-byte UTF-8 chars before interpolation point → byte_range off by 1–4 bytes. Fix: use char_indices not chars for offset accumulation; test with CJK template strings.

6.7 Performance Concerns

  • shell_meta_set scan: use AsciiSet (128-bit bitset) for O(1) per-byte lookup — no regex in metachar check.
  • Regex arg_merge_re: instantiate once per CmdInjectionAnalyzer, not per call; store in Arc<Regex> in guard struct.
  • normalize_path allocates String; on untainted path (short-circuit before normalize), zero allocation.
  • Taint map lookup: TaintMap is HashMap<ArgId, TaintedValue> with FxHasher; argid is u32 index → fast.
  • propagate_concat clones Vec<TaintTag> — on high-frequency concat chains, use SmallVec<[TaintTag; 4]> to avoid heap on short tag sets.
  • FileOpen hook is extremely hot (every file read in app) — short-circuit on untainted path must be first branch, predicted taken; measure branch prediction impact with perf stat.
  • Lock contention: RequestContext is thread-local / task-local, no mutex in critical path; verify with rasp-bench under 50 concurrent "requests".

6.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty argv (argv.len() == 0): guard must handle gracefully — emit Detection{severity:High, evidence: EmptyArgv} if program is tainted.
  • Path length > PATH_MAX (4096 on Linux): normalizer must cap at 4096 bytes and raise PathTooLong signal, not panic.
  • Non-UTF8 byte sequences in path (raw OS path): SinkArgs::FileOpen.path exposed as &str; adapter must validate UTF-8 or use OsStr; fallback: treat non-UTF8 as OpaqueBytes, flag as High if tainted.
  • Concurrent requests with overlapping taint maps: context isolation invariant — each request has its own TaintMap; test with 100 concurrent tasks writing to separate maps simultaneously.
  • Taint byte range start >= end: degenerate tag from malformed propagation; guard must treat as zero-length range, not panic.
  • Exec sink with empty tainted string "" as argv[1]: no metachar, but zero-length tainted arg — low severity Monitor only.
  • Path with only whitespace " ": normalize to empty string, flag AmbiguousPath as Low severity.
  • Double-percent-encoded NUL (%2500): decode once → %00, which is a literal %00 string, NOT a NUL; document behavior explicitly in fixture.

6.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery
CmdInjectionAnalyzer panics in hook rasp_hook_panic_total counter spike Single request; host-safety invariant: catch_unwind wraps every hook Log panic, apply fail_mode (Open/Closed) Reload policy; if persistent, disable Exec sink hook
normalize_path stack overflow (deep recursion) Hook timeout > 2 ms Single request Recursion hard-capped at depth 8 Return PathTooComplex signal, treat as suspicious
Taint map corruption (propagation bug) FP/FN rate deviation in metrics All requests processed after corruption Context isolation: per-request TaintMap reset at request end Force context reset via ctx.clear_taint()
Regex engine hangs (ReDoS) Latency p99 > 10 µs All Exec hooks Time-limit regex with fancy-regex timeout; fall back to metachar scan Disable regex arm; file bug
False-block rate spike rasp_false_positive_rate metric All file operations blocked Switch to Monitor mode via live policy reload Root-cause: check taint range intersection logic

Postmortem guidance: capture (raw_path, taint_map_snapshot, normalized_path, signals) in telemetry on every Detection; replay with rasp-bench --replay against fixture.

6.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics (Prometheus labels): rasp_cmd_injection_detections_total{severity,confidence_bucket}, rasp_path_traversal_detections_total{signal_type}, rasp_hook_latency_us{sink="Exec",percentile="p99"}, rasp_hook_latency_us{sink="FileOpen",percentile="p99"}.
  • Tracing: tracing::span!(Level::DEBUG, "cmd_inject_analyze", argv_len, tainted_args) — disabled in production unless RASP_TRACE=debug.
  • Structured log on Detection: {rule_id, sink, signal_set, taint_path, request_id, confidence, action} → SIEM/CEF.
  • Live policy reload for allowed_root_paths via rasp-policy watch: no restart needed; paths update atomically via ArcSwap<Policy>.
  • Deployment: Exec hook registration is additive; hooking can be disabled per-sink via policy.disabled_sinks list without recompile.
  • Rollback: set policy.disabled_sinks = [Exec, FileOpen] to zero overhead instantly; re-enable after fix.
  • Compatibility: SinkArgs::Exec and SinkArgs::FileOpen variants are non-exhaustive (#[non_exhaustive]); adapters must handle SinkArgs::Unknown variant.

19. Phase 7 — SSRF + deserialization detection — URL parse + allowlist/denylist + taint for HTTP sinks; dangerous-class/gadget detection for deserialization

7.1 Objectives

  • Implement SsrfAnalyzer in rasp-detect/src/ssrf.rs: parse URLs from SinkType::HttpRequest args, enforce host allowlist/denylist, detect SSRF patterns (private IP ranges, metadata endpoints, CNAME tricks).
  • Implement DeserializeAnalyzer in rasp-detect/src/deserialize.rs: inspect SinkType::Deserialize hook args for dangerous class names, gadget chain signatures, and magic byte patterns in Java/Python/PHP/Ruby serialized formats.
  • Extend rasp-taint: add propagate_url_param(url: &TaintedValue, key: &str) -> TaintedValue for query-parameter taint extraction; propagate_bytes(src: &TaintedValue) -> TaintedValue for binary blob passthrough.
  • Extend rasp-policy: add ssrf_policy: SsrfPolicy { allowed_hosts: Vec<HostPattern>, denied_cidr: Vec<IpCidr>, deny_private: bool, deny_loopback: bool, denied_metadata_endpoints: Vec<Url> } and deserialize_policy: DeserializePolicy { denied_classes: Vec<String>, format_blocks: Vec<SerialFormat> }.
  • Constraint: URL parsing must be zero-allocation on cache-hit path; deserialization analysis must not deserialize the payload (pure structural inspection only).
  • Overhead budget: <2 µs p99 per HttpRequest sink call; <3 µs p99 per Deserialize sink call on benign payloads.

7.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

  • rasp-core (extend types): SinkArgs::HttpRequest { url: &str, method: &str, headers: &[(&str,&str)], body: Option<&[u8]> }; SinkArgs::Deserialize { format: SerialFormat, payload: &[u8] }; SerialFormat enum: JavaSerial | Pickle | PhpSerial | RubyMarshal | JsonUntyped | MsgpackUntyped | Unknown; new DetectionKind::Ssrf, DetectionKind::DeserializationGadget variants.
  • rasp-detect/src/ssrf.rs (SsrfAnalyzer, deps url + ipnetwork): struct SsrfAnalyzer { allowed_hosts: Arc<HostMatcher>, denied_cidrs: Arc<CidrMatcher>, denied_endpoints: Arc<Vec<Url>> }; HostMatcher = prefix-tree of allowed hostnames + wildcard (*.internal.corp), match_host(host) -> bool; CidrMatcher = sorted Vec<IpCidr> with binary search, covers RFC1918 (10/8,172.16/12,192.168/16), loopback (127/8,::1), link-local (169.254/16 — AWS/GCP metadata), ::ffff:0:0/96 (IPv4-mapped); analyze(event, ctx) -> Option<Detection> — (1) short-circuit if URL arg not in ctx.taint_map ⇒ None; (2) url::Url::parse(raw), parse error ⇒ Detection{Medium, MalformedUrl} if tainted; (3) extract host, resolve to IpAddr if numeric literal (no DNS), else flag ResolveRequiredForDynamic; (4) CidrMatcher private-IP hit ⇒ Detection{Critical, Ssrf, PrivateIp}; (5) denied_metadata_endpoints (169.254.169.254, fd00:ec2::254, metadata.google.internal) ⇒ Detection{Critical, MetadataEndpoint}; (6) host not in HostMatcher allowlist ⇒ Detection{High, UnallowedHost}; (7) dangerous scheme (file://,gopher://,dict://,ftp://) if tainted ⇒ Detection{High, DangerousScheme}; (8) taint-path propagation identifies the sourcing HTTP param, included in Detection.evidence. URL parsing/normalization follows S1 (RFC 3986, URI Generic Syntax) under the locked design ADR-010 (SSRF detection: URL-parse + IP-range deny-list, no inline DNS).
  • rasp-detect/src/deserialize.rs (DeserializeAnalyzer): magic-byte table MAGIC_BYTES — JavaSerial: [0xAC,0xED,0x00,0x05], Pickle: [0x80,0x02..0x05] (PROTO opcode), PhpSerial: b"O:"/b"C:", RubyMarshal: [0x04,0x08]; ClassNameScanner scans Java stream for TC_CLASSDESC tokens (0x72), extracts class names, matches against denied_classes (HashSet<String>); GadgetSignatureSet: HashMap<SerialFormat, Vec<GadgetPattern>> — Java: org.apache.commons.collections, com.sun.org.apache.xalan, org.springframework, com.rometools.rome, org.apache.xbean (ysoserial chains); Pickle: opcode sequence GLOBAL+REDUCE (b'c'+b'R') ⇒ arbitrary callable exec; PHP: __destruct+__wakeup in tainted stream; analyze(event, ctx) -> Option<Detection> — (1) detect format from magic bytes or event.args.format; (2) short-circuit if payload untainted ⇒ None; (3) scan for gadget signatures (byte pattern matching, no deserialization); (4) Java: extract class names, intersect denied_classes, match gadget chains; (5) Pickle: scan for GLOBAL+REDUCE pair within tainted range; (6) score: gadget match ⇒ Critical, dangerous class ⇒ High, recognized format + tainted but no gadget ⇒ Medium; (7) build Detection with evidence: {format, matched_gadget, class_name, byte_offset}.
  • rasp-taint (new ops): propagate_url_param(url, key) -> Option<TaintedValue> parses query string, locates the param value's byte range within the original tainted URL, returns a sub-range TaintedValue; propagate_bytes(src) -> TaintedValue passthrough for binary blob taint (preserves source + tags).
  • rasp-policy (ssrf_policy.rs, deserialize_policy.rs): SsrfPolicy deserialized from TOML/JSON, hot-reloadable via ArcSwap<SsrfPolicy>; DeserializePolicy { denied_classes: HashSet<String>, format_blocks: Vec<SerialFormat>, allow_unknown_format: bool }; evaluate_ssrf(detection, policy) -> Action; evaluate_deserialize(detection, policy) -> Action.
  • rasp-protect (guard.rs): matches SinkType::HttpRequest => guard.analyze_ssrf(event, ctx) and SinkType::Deserialize => guard.analyze_deserialize(event, ctx); both resolve context → check taint → call analyzer → evaluate policy → enforce action.
  • rasp-bench: adds SSRF attack corpus (fixtures/ssrf/) and deserialize gadget corpus (fixtures/deserialize/); benign URL corpus for FP measurement.
  • Tests (rasp-detect/tests/ssrf_tests.rs, deserialize_tests.rs): SSRF tainted http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data ⇒ Detection{Critical}; SSRF tainted http://192.168.1.1/admin ⇒ Detection{Critical}; SSRF allowed host http://api.partner.com/v1 ⇒ None; deserialize Java stream with CommonsCollections gadget, tainted ⇒ Detection{Critical}; deserialize Pickle GLOBAL+REDUCE, tainted ⇒ Detection{Critical}; deserialize clean JSON body, untainted ⇒ None.

7.3 Deliverable

  • rasp-detect: SsrfAnalyzer + DeserializeAnalyzer compile, pass unit + integration tests.
  • rasp-policy: SsrfPolicy + DeserializePolicy loaded from config; hot-reload tested.
  • rasp-taint: propagate_url_param, propagate_bytes implemented and tested.
  • rasp-protect: guard handles HttpRequest and Deserialize sink events end-to-end.
  • rasp-bench: SSRF + deserialize overhead measured; benign URL corpus FP rate measured.
  • Operator-visible: metrics rasp_ssrf_detections_total{evidence_type}, rasp_deserialize_detections_total{format,gadget_matched}.

fixtures/ssrf/private_192.toml RFC1918 192.168.x.x Detection{Critical, PrivateIp}
fixtures/ssrf/loopback_127.toml http://127.0.0.1:8080/admin Detection{Critical, PrivateIp}
fixtures/ssrf/gopher_scheme.toml gopher://evil/x tainted Detection{High, DangerousScheme}
fixtures/ssrf/file_scheme.toml file:///etc/passwd tainted Detection{High, DangerousScheme}
fixtures/ssrf/allowed_host.toml https://api.partner.com in allowlist None
fixtures/ssrf/malformed_url.toml http://[::invalid parse error, tainted Detection{Medium, MalformedUrl}
fixtures/ssrf/ipv6_private.toml http://[::1]/admin tainted Detection{Critical, PrivateIp}
fixtures/ssrf/regression_cname.toml hostname resolves to private IP at runtime — documented: no DNS; behavior: UnallowedHost if not in allowlist Detection{High}
fixtures/deserialize/java_commons_coll.bin CommonsCollections1 ysoserial gadget, tainted Detection{Critical, GadgetChain}
fixtures/deserialize/pickle_global_reduce.pkl Pickle GLOBAL+REDUCE, tainted Detection{Critical, GadgetChain}
fixtures/deserialize/php_wakeup.ser PHP __wakeup chain, tainted Detection{High}
fixtures/deserialize/java_clean.bin Benign Java serialization (no gadget), tainted Detection{Medium, TaintedSerial} (format recognized)
fixtures/deserialize/json_untainted.json Clean JSON, untainted None
fixtures/deserialize/unknown_magic.bin Unknown magic bytes, tainted Detection{Medium, UnknownFormat} if allow_unknown_format=false

7.6 Pitfalls

  1. DNS rebinding not caught: attacker pre-resolves hostname to public IP, passes allowlist; after TTL, re-resolves to private IP. Root cause: RASP checks at hook time (pre-resolution). Mitigation: documented limitation; recommend network-layer SSRF controls; log ResolveRequiredForDynamic signal; note in threat model.
  2. IPv4-mapped IPv6 bypass: http://[::ffff:192.168.1.1]/ parsed as IPv6 by URL parser but maps to private IPv4. Fix: after IpAddr::V6 parse, check .to_ipv4_mapped() and re-evaluate against CidrMatcher.
  3. Pickle opcode scan FP on binary data: non-Pickle binary data with byte 0x63 (c) followed by 0x52 (R) → false GLOBAL+REDUCE match. Fix: validate Pickle format first via magic bytes; only scan opcode sequence after PROTO confirmation.
  4. Deserialization payload is compressed: Java serial stream wrapped in GZIP — magic bytes are GZIP header, not 0xACED. Root cause: adapter strips GZIP before RASP sees it, or doesn't. Mitigation: detect GZIP magic [0x1F, 0x8B], flag as CompressedPayload signal; policy can block on unknown compressed deserialization.
  5. URL redirect chain not tracked: app fetches URL, gets 301 to http://169.254.169.254; RASP only sees original URL. Root cause: redirect followed by HTTP client outside RASP's hook. Mitigation: hook HttpRequest on each redirect follow if HTTP client supports it; document limitation.
  6. Class name false-negative via obfuscation: gadget class name obfuscated (0rg.apache... with digit). Root cause: string matching. Mitigation: fuzzy match with edit distance ≤ 2 for class names in denied_classes; flag as High with reduced confidence.
  7. SsrfPolicy reload race: request in-flight reads old policy while new policy being swapped. Fix: ArcSwap<SsrfPolicy> guarantees atomic pointer swap; readers always see consistent snapshot; document that reload takes effect on next request, not current.

7.7 Performance Concerns

  • URL parsing (url::Url::parse): allocates String for normalized URL; cache parsed Url keyed by raw URL string using LruCache<Arc<str>, Arc<Url>> (capacity 1024, per-thread) — saves re-parse on redirect or repeated identical URLs.
  • CidrMatcher binary search: O(log n) on n CIDR entries (typically < 20); effectively O(1) in practice; no heap.
  • HostMatcher prefix tree: BTreeMap<String, ()> adequate for < 1000 entries; for large allowlists, switch to trie (radix_trie crate); benchmark at 10K entries.
  • ClassNameScanner on Java stream: linear scan of payload bytes; payload can be large (MB). Mitigation: scan only first 64 KB (gadget class names appear early in stream); configurable via max_scan_bytes.
  • Pickle opcode scan: O(payload_size); same 64 KB cap.
  • propagate_url_param: query-string parse is O(query_len); URL query params typically < 1 KB → fast; avoid allocation via in-place byte scan.
  • DeserializePolicy denied_classes: HashSet<String> with FxHasher; build once at startup from config, store in Arc; no lock in hot path.

7.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty URL string "", tainted: parse fails → Detection{Medium, MalformedUrl}.
  • URL with no host (/relative/path): url.host_str() returns None → treat as UnallowedHost (conservative); document.
  • Deserialization payload length = 0: return None (no magic bytes possible).
  • Payload exactly 4 bytes (Java magic only): format detected but no class names extractable → Detection{Low, FormatMatch}.
  • Concurrent taint map access in async context: TaintMap in RequestContext is per-task-local; verify with tokio::task::spawn_local isolation test.
  • URL with embedded credentials (http://admin:pass@internal.corp): parse strips credentials; host still checked against allowlist; credentials themselves trigger Detection{Medium, CredentialsInUrl} regardless of taint.
  • Deserialization of null/empty Java stream (only stream magic, no objects): benign; ClassNameScanner finds no TC_CLASSDESC → Detection{Low} only if tainted (tainted deserialization invocation itself is suspicious).
  • PHP serial with deeply nested O: chain (stack depth attack on scanner): cap scanner recursion at depth 32; treat deeper as TooDeep signal.

7.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery
SsrfAnalyzer URL parse panic rasp_hook_panic_total spike Single request catch_unwind → fail_mode Log + apply fail_mode
CidrMatcher returns wrong result (regression) FN rate spike in metrics All HTTP requests Disable SSRF sink hook via policy.disabled_sinks Fix + redeploy policy
ClassNameScanner OOM on 100 MB payload Memory pressure alert Single thread max_scan_bytes = 65536 hard cap prevents Return Detection{Medium, ScanCapped}
SsrfPolicy TOML parse error on reload rasp_policy_reload_error_total Old policy retained ArcSwap keeps old policy Fix config, retry reload
Gadget class list out of date FN on new gadget chain All deserialize sinks Alert on rasp_denied_class_miss_total (requires runtime threat intel) Update denied_classes in policy

Postmortem: for SSRF incidents, capture (raw_url, parsed_host, taint_source, allowlist_snapshot) in telemetry; replay with rasp-bench --replay-ssrf.

7.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: rasp_ssrf_detections_total{evidence_type}, rasp_deserialize_detections_total{format,gadget}, rasp_ssrf_allowed_total, rasp_deserialize_scan_capped_total, rasp_hook_latency_us{sink="HttpRequest"}, rasp_hook_latency_us{sink="Deserialize"}.
  • Policy hot-reload: SsrfPolicy and DeserializePolicy loaded from rasp.toml; file-watch via notify crate signals rasp-policy reloader thread; ArcSwap swap < 1 µs.
  • Audit log: every Detection includes {request_id, sink, url_or_format, matched_evidence, action, policy_version} — CEF format for SIEM.
  • Tracing: tracing::span!(Level::DEBUG, "ssrf_analyze", host, tainted) gated on RASP_TRACE=debug.
  • Deployment: update allowed_hosts in rasp.toml for new partner integrations; no redeploy; reload propagates within 100 ms.
  • DeserializePolicy.denied_classes can be updated from external threat-intel feed via API → policy hot-reload; track policy_version in metrics for correlation.
  • Rollback: policy.disabled_sinks = [HttpRequest, Deserialize] disables both analyzers instantly; revert policy file and reload.

20. Phase 8 — Detection engine framework — rule abstraction (signature + semantic), per-sink analyzer plug-ins, severity/confidence scoring, cross-sink correlation/dedup per request

8.1 Objectives

  • Define the unified DetectionEngine framework in rasp-detect/src/engine.rs: a registry of per-sink Analyzer trait objects, unified Detection scoring, cross-sink correlation within a RequestContext, and deduplication.
  • Formalize Rule + Policy evaluation pipeline: Rule { id, sink, kind: Signature|Semantic, min_confidence, action } matched against Detection.confidence; policy override (waiver, virtual patch).
  • Implement ScoringModel in rasp-detect/src/scoring.rs: multi-factor severity × confidence → RiskScore; define thresholds for block vs monitor escalation.
  • Implement CrossSinkCorrelator in rasp-detect/src/correlator.rs: within a RequestContext, accumulate Detections across sinks; detect multi-sink attack patterns (e.g., path traversal + file read + exec in same request).
  • Implement DetectionDeduplicator: within a request, suppress duplicate Detections (same rule_id + sink within sliding 100 ms window) to avoid telemetry floods.
  • Extend rasp-telemetry: per-Detection event emission with full correlation context; aggregate RiskScore per request for SIEM.
  • Constraint: the framework adds ≤ 500 ns overhead per analyzer dispatch call (trait object call + scoring + dedup check).
  • Constraint: all framework state per request lives in RequestContext; no global mutable state in hot path.

8.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

  • rasp-detect/src/analyzer.rs (Analyzer trait):
pub trait Analyzer: Send + Sync {
    fn sink_type(&self) -> SinkType;
    fn analyze(&self, event: &HookEvent, ctx: &RequestContext) -> Option<Detection>;
    fn name(&self) -> &'static str;
}

AnalyzerRegistry: HashMap<SinkType, Vec<Box<dyn Analyzer>>> (multiple analyzers per sink, e.g. SQLi + SemanticSQL both on SqlQuery); dispatch(event, ctx) -> Vec<Detection> calls all registered analyzers for event.sink_type, collects results; built via AnalyzerRegistry::builder().register(analyzer)...build() -> Arc<AnalyzerRegistry>.

  • rasp-detect/src/engine.rs (DetectionEngine): fields registry: Arc<AnalyzerRegistry>, scorer: ScoringModel, correlator: CrossSinkCorrelator, dedup: DetectionDeduplicator; process(event, ctx) -> Vec<ScoredDetection> — (1) detections = registry.dispatch(event, ctx); (2) score = scorer.score(&detection) per detection; (3) dedup.filter(&mut detections, ctx) suppresses duplicates; (4) correlator.accumulate(&detections, ctx) updates correlation state; (5) correlator.emit_cross_sink_detections(ctx) emits multi-sink pattern detections past threshold; (6) returns Vec<ScoredDetection> (Detection+RiskScore+CorrelationTag).
  • rasp-detect/src/scoring.rs (ScoringModel): RiskScore = f32 in [0.0,1.0]; risk = severity_weight(severity) * confidence * taint_coverage_factor (severity_weight: Critical=1.0, High=0.75, Medium=0.50, Low=0.25; taint_coverage_factor = tainted_bytes/total_arg_bytes from Detection.evidence); thresholds from policy block_threshold (default 0.7), monitor_threshold (default 0.3); score(detection) -> RiskScore pure, no allocation; escalate(score, base_action, policy) -> Action: score≥block_threshold && mode==Block ⇒ Block, score≥monitor_threshold ⇒ Monitor, else Allow. This pure, non-random scoring function is what §7 Invariant 7.3 (Block Determinism) and §6.3 (Deterministic Block Decisions) require.
  • rasp-detect/src/correlator.rs (CrossSinkCorrelator): per-request cross_sink_state: CrossSinkState { detections_by_sink: HashMap<SinkType, Vec<ScoredDetection>>, pattern_matched: Vec<PatternId> } in RequestContext; PatternDef { id, required_sinks, within_ms, min_total_risk }; built-ins — PathThenExec: FileOpen(High+)→Exec(High+) within 500 ms ⇒ Critical composite; SsrfThenRead: HttpRequest(High+)→FileOpen(High+) within 500 ms; SqlThenDeserialization: SqlQuery(High+)→Deserialize(High+) within 1 s; accumulate(detections, ctx) appends to detections_by_sink; emit_cross_sink_detections(ctx) -> Vec<Detection> checks each pattern against required sinks within window, emits composite Detection{kind: CrossSinkPattern, severity: Critical}; time tracked via HookEvent.timestamp (monotonic, no syscall).
  • rasp-detect/src/dedup.rs (DetectionDeduplicator): per-request HashMap<(RuleId, SinkType), Instant> dedup window in RequestContext; filter(detections, ctx) removes a detection if the same (rule_id, sink) was seen within dedup_window_ms (default 100 ms), else records timestamp; across-request flood control uses a token-bucket rate limiter per rule_id in rasp-telemetry (1000 events/s default, configurable).
  • rasp-policy/src/engine.rs (PolicyEvaluator): evaluate(detections, policy) -> Vec<(ScoredDetection, Action)> looks up policy.rules by rule_id, applies rule.action if score ≥ rule.min_confidence; policy.waivers (by request_path/client_ip/rule_id) override to Allow; policy.virtual_patches override to Block for a specific rule_id regardless of score; aggregates: any Block ⇒ final Block, else any Monitor ⇒ Monitor, else Allow.
  • rasp-protect/src/guard.rs (InlineGuard::handle_event): calls engine.process(event, ctx) → Vec<ScoredDetection>, then policy_evaluator.evaluate(&scored, &policy) → Vec<(ScoredDetection, Action)>; takes the most restrictive final action, enforces it, emits all ScoredDetections + actions to rasp-telemetry.
  • Tests: rasp-detect/tests/engine_tests.rs (two analyzers on SqlQuery both fire ⇒ two Detections, scored + deduplicated); rasp-detect/tests/correlator_tests.rs (PathThenExec: FileOpen(High) then Exec(High) within 500 ms ⇒ composite Detection{Critical}); rasp-detect/tests/scoring_tests.rs (Critical×0.9 confidence×0.8 taint coverage ⇒ RiskScore≥0.7 ⇒ Block); rasp-detect/tests/dedup_tests.rs (same (rule_id, sink) twice in 50 ms ⇒ second suppressed).

8.3 Deliverable

  • rasp-detect: DetectionEngine, AnalyzerRegistry, ScoringModel, CrossSinkCorrelator, DetectionDeduplicator compile + test-pass.
  • rasp-policy: PolicyEvaluator handles waivers, virtual patches, mode-based escalation.
  • rasp-protect: InlineGuard uses DetectionEngine end-to-end for all registered sink types.
  • All Phase 5–7 analyzers (SqliAnalyzer, CmdInjectionAnalyzer, PathTraversalAnalyzer, SsrfAnalyzer, DeserializeAnalyzer) registered in AnalyzerRegistry; dispatched uniformly.
  • rasp-bench: framework overhead measured at < 500 ns per dispatch call; cross-sink correlation overhead < 1 µs per accumulate call.
  • Operator-visible: per-request RiskScore emitted in telemetry; cross_sink_pattern_detected_total{pattern_id} counter; dedup_suppressed_total{rule_id} counter.

8.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
cargo test -p rasp-detect (engine suite) 100% pass
cargo test -p rasp-policy (evaluator suite) 100% pass
Framework dispatch overhead p99 < 500 ns per event
Cross-sink correlation overhead p99 per accumulate < 1 µs
Dedup: second identical detection within 100 ms suppressed verified by dedup_tests
PatternDef::PathThenExec fires within 500 ms window verified by correlator_tests
PolicyEvaluator waiver overrides Block → Allow verified by waiver_test
Virtual patch overrides Allow → Block verified by virtual_patch_test
ScoringModel: Critical × 1.0 confidence → RiskScore ≥ 0.95 verified by scoring_tests
