8.5 Conformance Fixtures

Filename Purpose Expected Output
fixtures/engine/multi_analyzer_same_sink.toml Two analyzers on SqlQuery; input triggers both Two Detections; scored independently
fixtures/engine/single_analyzer_no_taint.toml Analyzer registered; args untainted Vec::empty()
fixtures/scoring/critical_high_confidence.toml Critical, confidence=0.95 RiskScore >= 0.95; action=Block
fixtures/scoring/medium_low_confidence.toml Medium, confidence=0.3 RiskScore <= 0.15; action=Allow
fixtures/scoring/threshold_boundary.toml Score at exactly block_threshold Block
fixtures/correlator/path_then_exec.toml FileOpen(High) + Exec(High) within 400 ms Composite Detection{Critical, PatternId::PathThenExec}
fixtures/correlator/path_then_exec_timeout.toml FileOpen(High) + Exec(High) at 600 ms gap No composite (outside 500 ms window)
fixtures/correlator/ssrf_then_read.toml HttpRequest(High) + FileOpen(High) within 300 ms Composite Detection{Critical, PatternId::SsrfThenRead}
fixtures/dedup/same_rule_50ms.toml Same (rule_id, SqlQuery) twice in 50 ms Second suppressed; dedup_suppressed_total += 1
fixtures/dedup/same_rule_150ms.toml Same (rule_id, SqlQuery) at 150 ms gap Both emitted (outside window)
fixtures/policy/waiver_override.toml Block detection + matching waiver Final action = Allow
fixtures/policy/virtual_patch.toml Allow detection + virtual patch Final action = Block
fixtures/policy/monitor_mode_no_block.toml Monitor mode; any Detection → Monitor, never Block Monitor purity invariant verified
fixtures/engine/regression_phase5_via_registry.toml SQLi via registry dispatch (not direct call) Same detection as Phase 5 direct test

8.6 Pitfalls

  1. Trait object vtable overhead accumulation: dispatching 6 analyzers per SqlQuery event via Box<dyn Analyzer> adds ~30 ns each (vtable indirect + cache miss) → ~180 ns total. For hot sinks, consider enum Analyzer { Sqli(..), Semantic(..) } static dispatch for known types; keep trait for runtime-loaded plugins only.
  2. Correlator state not reset between requests: CrossSinkState left in RequestContext after request end → stale detections from previous request contaminate next. Fix: RequestContext::reset() must clear cross_sink_state; test with back-to-back request fixture.
  3. **Dedup window uses wall clock from Instant::now(): if system clock jumps (container live migration), Instant is monotonic on Linux (CLOCK_MONOTONIC) — safe. On macOS, verify Instant is monotonic; document.
  4. Scoring formula integer truncation: if taint_coverage_factor computed as integer / integer → 0 in Rust. Fix: cast to f32 before division; add lint #[deny(clippy::integer_division)] in scoring module.
  5. Virtual patch masking legitimate Block: virtual patch rule_id misconfigured to broad rule → blocks benign traffic. Root cause: overly broad rule_id. Mitigation: virtual patches require exact rule_id + optional request_path_pattern; audit log {virtual_patch_applied, rule_id, request_path} on every use.
  6. Correlator pattern fires on different requests' detections: if cross_sink_state accidentally shared across requests (e.g., moved into global Arc instead of per-request). Root cause: architectural error. Prevention: CrossSinkState is !Sync; store only in RequestContext; RequestContext is !Send (thread-local) or task-local. Test with compile_fail for &CrossSinkState: Sync.
  7. Registry dispatch order non-deterministic on rebuild: HashMap<SinkType, Vec<Box<dyn Analyzer>>> — insertion order not preserved in older HashMap. Fix: use IndexMap to preserve registration order; deterministic dispatch order required for reproducible test output.
  8. Monitor purity violation under race: concurrent evaluate and policy.mode hot-reload both on the same ArcSwap<Policy> snapshot; since ArcSwap::load() is atomic, the snapshot is consistent for the duration of one evaluate call — no race. Verify with loom test.

8.7 Performance Concerns

  • AnalyzerRegistry::dispatch: inner loop over Vec<Box<dyn Analyzer>>; typically 1–3 analyzers per sink; loop unrollable by LLVM if ≤ 4.
  • ScoringModel::score: all f32 arithmetic, no allocation; should compile to 2–4 FP instructions; verify with cargo asm.
  • CrossSinkCorrelator::accumulate: HashMap insert per detection; amortized O(1); use FxHasher; pre-allocate map with with_capacity(8) per request.
  • DetectionDeduplicator::filter: Vec retain + HashMap lookup; O(d) where d = detections per event (typically 1–3); negligible.
  • PolicyEvaluator::evaluate: linear scan of policy.rules (typically < 100 rules); O(r * d); if > 1000 rules, switch to HashMap<RuleId, Rule> lookup.
  • Avoid clone() on Detection in scoring/dedup pipeline (use &Detection references throughout, clone only for telemetry emit on the async path); AnalyzerRegistry in Arc has no lock in the read path — Arc::clone is atomic but avoidable by storing the registry ref in the guard struct for the request's lifetime.

8.8 Edge Cases

  • No analyzers registered for a sink type: dispatch returns empty Vec → Allow; emit rasp_unregistered_sink_total{sink} counter to alert operator.
  • Detection with confidence = 0.0: RiskScore = 0.0; action = Allow; still emitted to telemetry for audit if rule.min_confidence = 0.0.
  • All analyzers return None for a tainted event: final action Allow; emit rasp_tainted_undetected_total{sink} — signals potential FN; useful for tuning.
  • PatternDef with required_sinks containing a sink that never fires in request: pattern never triggered; no false composite.
  • Waiver list empty: evaluator fast-paths waiver check with policy.waivers.is_empty() guard; virtual patch list with duplicate rule_id entries applies once (idempotent, deduplicated on policy load); PolicyEvaluator called with empty detections slice returns empty Vec, no allocation.
  • RequestContext outlives request due to async task leak: cross_sink_state accumulates indefinitely → memory leak. Mitigation: RequestContext has deadline: Instant; guard rejects context older than request_timeout_ms.

8.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery
AnalyzerRegistry lock (if added) deadlock Latency spike; rasp_hook_latency_us p99 > 10 µs All requests Registry is Arc<AnalyzerRegistry> read-only in hot path — no lock If lock added mistakenly: remove, use Arc
ScoringModel NaN from 0/0 RiskScore = NaN; action undefined All scored detections Add if total_bytes == 0 { coverage = 1.0 } guard; NaN check with if score.is_nan() { score = severity_weight } Deploy fixed scoring function
CrossSinkCorrelator pattern emits duplicate composite detections Telemetry flood; dedup_suppressed_total stays 0 (composite has unique rule_id) Telemetry; not app blocking Rate-limit composite detection emission; pattern_matched set prevents re-fire in same request Fix pattern check: only emit once per (PatternId, request_id)
PolicyEvaluator waiver list corrupted on reload Some requests incorrectly allowed All waived routes On reload error, retain old policy snapshot Alert rasp_policy_reload_error_total; fix config
Analyzer panic inside dispatch rasp_analyzer_panic_total{analyzer_name} spike Single request catch_unwind per analyzer call in dispatch; other analyzers still run Disable faulting analyzer via registry; file bug
Postmortem: capture {event, detections, scores, policy_version, correlation_state_snapshot} on block events; reproducible via rasp-bench --replay.

8.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: rasp_detection_engine_dispatch_latency_ns{sink}, rasp_scored_detections_total{severity,action}, rasp_cross_sink_pattern_total{pattern_id}, rasp_dedup_suppressed_total{rule_id}, rasp_unregistered_sink_total{sink}, rasp_tainted_undetected_total{sink}, rasp_policy_waiver_applied_total{rule_id}, rasp_virtual_patch_applied_total{rule_id}.
  • Tracing: tracing::span!(Level::DEBUG, "detection_engine_process", sink, num_analyzers, num_detections) — sampled at 1% in production.
  • Structured audit log per ScoredDetection: {request_id, rule_id, sink, severity, confidence, risk_score, action, waiver_applied, virtual_patch_applied, taint_path, timestamp, policy_version} → CEF/SIEM.
  • Live policy operations: add/remove/modify Rule, Waiver, VirtualPatch via rasp-cli policy patch; propagates via ArcSwap<Policy>; verify with rasp-cli policy status --version.
  • Deployment: AnalyzerRegistry built at rasp-protect startup; adding new analyzer requires recompile (plugins via trait objects do not require FFI if statically linked).
  • Rollback: set policy.mode = Monitor to prevent all blocks while investigating; FN/FP rates still measured.
  • Compatibility: Detection struct is non_exhaustive; downstream consumers (telemetry, CLI) must use ..Default::default() pattern; version-gated fields via #[cfg(feature = "v2_fields")].
  • Registry inspection: rasp-cli detect list-analyzers shows registered analyzers per sink, version, last-fired timestamp — for operational audit of what's active.

21. Phase 9 — Policy + enforcement — monitor vs block modes, per-rule actions, virtual patching, allow/deny, waivers, fail-open vs fail-closed, blocking mechanism (abort sink + safe error)

9.1 Objectives

Goal Constraint
Implement rasp-policy crate: Policy, Rule, Waiver, Action evaluation Evaluation cost ≤ 2 µs p99 per Detection
Monitor purity invariant: Monitor mode → zero side effects on app execution Enforced by type-level MonitorGuard; compile-time check
Block determinism: (sink, context, policy) → deterministic Action + Evidence No random or time-dependent branching
Virtual patching: runtime-loaded rules override built-in rules per sink/vuln Rules hot-reloadable without restart; version-tagged
Fail-open / fail-closed: RASP internal error → policy-driven fallback `fail_mode: Open
Waivers: per-user / per-path / per-rule suppression with TTL + justification Waiver matched before rule; audit trail mandatory
Blocking mechanism: Block → abort sink operation + return RaspError::Blocked to app No panic, no hang; app handles error gracefully
Scope: rasp-policy lib + rasp-protect enforcement path. No analyzer changes.

9.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

  • rasp-policy/src/lib.rs (core types):
pub struct Policy { pub mode: PolicyMode, pub rules: Vec<Rule>, pub waivers: Vec<Waiver>, pub fail_mode: FailMode, pub version: u64 }
pub enum PolicyMode { Monitor, Block }
pub enum FailMode { Open, Closed }
pub enum Action { Allow, Block, Monitor }
pub struct Rule { pub id: RuleId, pub sink: SinkType, pub kind: RuleKind, pub severity_min: Severity, pub action: Action, pub priority: u16, pub enabled: bool }
pub struct Waiver { pub id: WaiverId, pub rule_id: Option<RuleId>, pub path_pattern: Option<Glob>, pub expires_at: Option<Instant>, pub justification: String }

rules evaluated in priority order (lower priority = higher), waivers matched first, version monotonic and checked on each eval; Rule.kind ∈ Signature|Semantic|VirtualPatch, severity_min ignores detections below threshold; Waiver.rule_id: None = applies to all rules.

  • rasp-policy/src/engine.rs (evaluation): PolicyEngine::evaluate(detection, ctx, policy) -> PolicyDecision — (1) check waivers (expiry + path match + rule match) ⇒ Action::Allow if waived, emit waiver-used audit event; (2) find matching rules by sink + severity ≥ severity_min + enabled, sort by priority; (3) first matching rule → action, downgraded to Monitor if policy.mode==Monitor and rule says Block; (4) no matching rule → use policy.mode as default; (5) RASP internal error in evaluation → FailMode determines action, emits rasp_policy_fallback_total.
  • Monitor-mode type guard: zero-cost in Monitor mode — Action wrapped in MonitorGuard<Action>; compile-time, MonitorGuard::enforce() always returns Allow when mode==Monitor.
  • Hot-reload: PolicyStore: Arc<RwLock<Policy>> + AtomicU64 version counter; reload_policy(new) write-locks, swaps, increments version (in-flight requests complete with old policy); rasp-cli reload-policy --file policy.toml → HTTP POST to management endpoint → PolicyStore::reload_policy; TOML schema validated at load time, malformed input rejected and old policy retained.
  • Blocking mechanism (rasp-protect/src/guard.rs): InlineGuard::enforce(action) -> Result<(), RaspError> — Block returns Err(RaspError::Blocked { rule_id, detection_id, sink }); caller (adapter trampoline) translates RaspError::Blocked into a runtime-appropriate exception (e.g. SecurityException); no panic path, all internal errors caught by catch_unwind at the trampoline boundary.
  • Virtual patching: RuleKind::VirtualPatch rules loaded from virtual_patches.toml ({ sink, pattern, action: Block }), applied before built-in Semantic rules in priority order; cve_id field on Rule for traceability. This mechanism implements P10 (Brumley et al., Automatic Patch-Based Exploit Generation) and enforces §6.12 (Virtual Patching Is Time-Bounded) via mandatory waiver expires_at.
  • Audit log (via rasp-telemetry): every PolicyDecision emits {decision_id, rule_id, waiver_id, action, sink, severity, mode, fail_mode, ts}; waiver-used events tagged event_type: WaiverApplied.
  • Test harness: rasp-policy/tests/policy_eval.rs — 50+ table-driven cases covering Monitor/Block mode, waiver expiry, priority ordering, fail-open/closed, virtual patch override.

9.3 Deliverable

  • rasp-policy crate compiles; PolicyEngine::evaluate callable from rasp-protect.
  • rasp-cli reload-policy hot-swaps policy; existing requests unaffected.
  • Block path: InlineGuard::enforce(Block) returns Err(RaspError::Blocked); no panic.
  • Monitor mode: Block decisions logged, not enforced; app behavior unchanged.
  • Waiver match + audit event visible in telemetry stream.
  • Virtual patches loadable from TOML; CVE-tagged rules in policy dump.

9.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Threshold
policy_eval unit suite (50+ cases) 100% pass
Monitor mode: Block detection → app not interrupted 100% of injected detections
Block mode: SQLi Detection + Block rule → RaspError::Blocked 100% deterministic
Waiver with expired TTL → rule applies 100% correct
Hot-reload: new policy active within 100 ms p99 ≤ 100 ms
Policy eval latency p99 ≤ 2 µs (microbench)
Fail-closed: RASP internal panic in eval → Block returned 100% of injected panics
Fail-open: fail_mode: Open + internal error → Allow + audit log 100%
Virtual patch rule overrides built-in → Block at sink Integration test

9.7 Performance Concerns

Waiver list linear scan Waivers indexed by rule_id + path prefix trie; O(log n) match

Allocation in PolicyDecision Stack-allocated PolicyDecision; rule_id is SmallString<[u8;36]>
Glob pattern matching for waiver paths Patterns compiled to regex::Regex at load; no recompile per request
Fail-mode path audit log write Async channel to telemetry; non-blocking; backpressure bounded

22. Phase 10 — Performance + low-overhead — inline fast-path, sampling, taint-cost control, analyzer-result caching, async-context cost, overhead budget (<5% p95)

10.1 Objectives

Goal Constraint
Inline guard p95 added latency < 5% of baseline request latency Measured on synthetic + real-app benchmark
Short-circuit: untainted sink args → skip all analyzers Taint check before any analyzer invocation
Taint propagation cost budget: ≤ N tags per value (configurable, default 32) Tag count saturation with soundness flag
Analyzer result cache: identical (sink, normalized_arg) → cached Detection? LRU, bounded by entry count + TTL
Sampling: high-rate benign sinks → probabilistic sampling of full analysis Rate-configurable; attack paths always full-depth
Async-context propagation: negligible overhead in async/await tasks Task-local via tokio::task_local!; no per-poll alloc
Allocations on hot path: ≤ 2 heap allocs per hook event in steady state Measured by dhat / jemalloc profiling
Scope: rasp-protect hot path, rasp-taint cost control, rasp-detect caching, rasp-bench overhead harness.

10.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

  • Fast-path short-circuit (rasp-protect/src/guard.rs): InlineGuard::check(event, ctx) -> Action — (1) ctx.taint_map.is_empty() ⇒ Action::Allow immediately (no context, no taint possible); (2) rasp_taint::is_tainted(&event.args) false ⇒ Action::Allow (short-circuit before analyzers); (3) proceed to rasp-detect only when ≥1 arg tainted; #[cold] branch-prediction hint on the detect path (attack is rare). This short-circuit is the direct enforcement mechanism for §6.2 (Inline Latency Budget Is Non-Negotiable) and §7 Invariant 7.6 (Overhead Budget Invariant).
  • Taint tag cost control (rasp-taint/src/propagation.rs): TaintedValue.tags: SmallVec<[TaintTag; 4]> heap-spills only past 4 tags; MAX_TAGS: usize = 32 (configurable via Policy.taint_budget); on saturation sets TaintedValue.saturated = true (retains conservative worst-case taint, emits rasp_taint_saturated_total); string concat unions source tags, trims to budget if exceeded; no-op propagation (e.g. format string with no tainted args) returns the non-tainted value directly.
  • Analyzer result cache (rasp-detect/src/cache.rs):
struct AnalyzerCache { inner: LruCache<CacheKey, CachedResult>, ttl: Duration }  // lru crate, capacity=4096, ttl default 10s
struct CacheKey { sink: SinkType, arg_hash: u64 }   // xxhash3 of normalized arg
struct CachedResult { action: Action, ts: Instant }

cache lookup precedes the analyzer, hit returns the cached Action; miss runs the analyzer and stores the result only if Action::Allow (never cache Block — attacker can mutate the arg); invalidated by ttl expiry or policy-version change (version stored in the entry); thread-safe via Mutex<AnalyzerCache> per CPU through thread_local! (no cross-thread contention).

  • Sampling (rasp-protect/src/sampler.rs): Sampler::should_analyze(sink, ctx) -> bool; per-sink rate: f64 (0.0 = always analyze, 1.0 = always skip); applies only to benign-suspected sinks (tainted args already short-circuit; sampling is a second tier for high-rate tainted-but-likely-benign traffic); a prior Detection on the same (path, sink) pair forces full analysis (no sampling bypass); rasp_sampled_total counter per sink type.
  • Async-context cost (rasp-context/src/task_local.rs): tokio::task_local! { static REQUEST_CTX: Arc<RequestContext>; }; Arc clone on task spawn (O(1) refcount, no per-poll allocation); with_context(ctx, future) wraps REQUEST_CTX.scope(ctx, future); sync fallback thread_local! { static SYNC_CTX: RefCell<Option<Arc<RequestContext>>>; } for non-async runtimes; cost is 1 Arc::clone per task spawn + 1 atomic load per sink interception.
  • Allocation audit (rasp-protect): #[cfg(test)] use dhat::Dhat in the bench entry asserts heap-alloc count per hook event ≤ 2; SmallVec/SmallString used throughout HookEvent and TaintedValue; rasp-bench/src/alloc_tracker.rs wraps the global allocator to count allocs on the hot path.
  • rasp-bench/src/overhead.rs: baseline benchmark (HTTP server serving a SQL-backed endpoint without RASP) vs RASP-on benchmark (same server + inline guard active); metric is p50/p95/p99 latency delta and throughput delta (req/s); attack corpus (10k SQLi + cmd-injection requests) measures full-path overhead; benign corpus (100k realistic requests) measures short-circuit overhead.

10.3 Deliverable

  • rasp-protect hot path compiles with #[cold] annotation on detect branch.
  • Short-circuit provable: 100k benign requests → rasp_detect_invocations_total = 0 (if all untainted).
  • Analyzer cache hit rate ≥ 80% on benign corpus (measured by rasp_cache_hit_total / rasp_cache_lookup_total).
  • rasp-bench produces CSV: {sink_type, p50_us, p95_us, p99_us, baseline_p95_us, overhead_pct}.
  • Async context cost: ≤ 50 ns per task spawn (measured via Criterion microbench).
  • Heap alloc per hook event ≤ 2 in steady state (dhat assertion in CI).

10.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Threshold
rasp-bench benign corpus overhead p95 latency delta ≤ 5% of baseline
rasp-bench attack corpus (full path) p95 latency delta ≤ 15% of baseline
Short-circuit: untainted args → rasp_detect_invocations_total 0 for 100% benign untainted requests
Analyzer cache hit rate (benign corpus) ≥ 80%
Heap allocs per hook event (steady state) ≤ 2 (dhat assertion)
Taint tag saturation under corpus rasp_taint_saturated_total ≤ 0.1% of events
Async context overhead per task spawn ≤ 50 ns p99
Criterion microbench: policy eval ≤ 2 µs p99
Criterion microbench: taint check (is_tainted) ≤ 200 ns p99

fixtures/bench/sqli_10k.jsonl 10k SQLi attack requests Full path exercised; blocks ≥ 99%

10.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
Short-circuit masks attack Attack bypasses detector Taint source not registered → args appear untainted rasp_taint_source_miss_total counter Ensure all HTTP input points are taint-sourced before sink
Cache serves stale Block Benign request blocked after policy change Cache not invalidated on policy version bump rasp_cache_version_mismatch_total Store policy version in cache entry; evict on mismatch
Sampling skips real attack Attack not analyzed Sampler rate too high; attack path not in "seen-attack" set rasp_sampled_total high; no corresponding rasp_blocks_total Sampling only on confirmed-untainted paths; any tainted arg → always full analysis
SmallVec heap spill undetected Alloc count > 2; performance regression Tag count > 4 more common than expected dhat alloc count assertion fails in CI Lower MAX_TAGS default or increase SmallVec inline size
Thread-local cache + fork Cache state duplicated across fork RASP used in forking server (pre-fork model) Cache hit rate anomalously high post-fork Flush thread-local caches on pthread_atfork handler

Criterion microbench unstable Overhead threshold flaps in CI System noise; insufficient iterations High variance in Criterion output Pin bench to isolated CPU; run 1000+ iterations

10.7 Performance Concerns

Concern Mitigation
is_tainted on large TaintMap Hash map lookup O(1); key = arg pointer/value hash
LruCache under contention (thread-local) Thread-local: no lock; one cache per OS thread
Arc::clone per task spawn Atomic increment; negligible vs I/O latency
xxhash3 for cache key ~1 ns/byte; bounded by arg length (truncate at 4096 B)
Cold cache on process restart Warm-up phase: first N requests bypass cache; configurable
#[cold] hint missed by compiler Verify with cargo asm that detect path is cold branch; add likely!/unlikely! if needed
Sampler RNG overhead SmallRng (non-cryptographic); seeded once per thread-local

10.8 Edge Cases

Case Handling
Arg longer than 1 MB Truncate at MAX_ARG_BYTES = 1 MiB for cache key + analyzer; rasp_arg_truncated_total++
Taint map with 10k entries (param explosion) Bound TaintMap at MAX_TAINT_ENTRIES = 512; discard beyond; rasp_taint_overflow_total++
Clock skew (cache TTL) Use Instant (monotonic); immune to wall-clock skew
Cache eviction during high-rate attack Attack results not cached; eviction harmless (re-analyze)
Zero-latency baseline (trivial handler) 5% overhead budget expressed as absolute cap ≤ 500 µs added p95 for trivial handlers
Async runtime with custom executor Sync fallback via thread_local for non-tokio executors

10.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery
Cache OOM (LRU grows unbounded) rasp_cache_size_bytes gauge exceeds limit Per-thread cache; isolated to one thread Cap at 4096 entries; evict LRU Reduce cache_capacity config; restart cache
Short-circuit bug: attack allowed rasp_blocks_total = 0 under attack corpus All attacks undetected Switch to fail_closed; disable short-circuit Fix taint source registration; re-deploy
Overhead budget exceeded in prod p95 latency SLO breach; RASP overhead metric > 5% App performance degraded Switch RASP to Monitor-only (no analysis cost) or increase sampling Profile with perf; identify hot analyzer; cache or optimize
Async context leak between requests Taint from request A observed in request B False positives; cross-request data leak Context isolation test (rasp_context_isolation_test) Fix with_context scope; ensure Drop cleans context

23. Phase 11 — Evasion resistance + robustness — encoding/obfuscation normalization (URL/hex/unicode/double-encode), SQL comment/whitespace tricks, fail-safe under attack, anti-bypass

11.1 Objectives

Goal Constraint
Normalize all attacker-controlled input before analysis: URL-encode, hex-encode, unicode-escape, double-encode, HTML-entity Normalization applied in rasp-detect before each analyzer
SQL evasion: handle comment injection (--, /**/, /*!*/), whitespace substitution, case folding, concatenation tricks rasp-detect/src/sqli/normalizer.rs
Command injection evasion: shell metachar obfuscation, IFS tricks, $'...' quoting, null-byte injection rasp-detect/src/cmdi/normalizer.rs
Path traversal evasion: ..%2f, %2e%2e, null-byte, overlong UTF-8, Windows ..\ rasp-detect/src/path/normalizer.rs
Fail-safe under active attack: analyzer must not crash, OOM, or spin under adversarial input All analyzers bounded: MAX_ITERATIONS, MAX_DECODE_DEPTH = 5
Anti-bypass: normalization is idempotent; apply until fixpoint (≤ MAX_DECODE_DEPTH iterations) Detect fixpoint; stop early
Throughput under attack corpus must not degrade beyond overhead budget Normalization cost included in ≤5% p95 budget
Scope: rasp-detect normalizers; rasp-taint taint-through-normalization; rasp-bench evasion corpus.

11.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

  • rasp-detect/src/normalize/mod.rs (unified normalizer pipeline):
pub struct NormalizerPipeline { stages: Vec<Box<dyn NormStage>>, max_depth: usize, max_len: usize }  // max_depth default 5, max_len default 65536
pub trait NormStage: Send + Sync { fn normalize(&self, input: &str) -> Cow<str>; fn name(&self) -> &'static str; }
pub fn normalize_to_fixpoint(input: &str, pipeline: &NormalizerPipeline) -> (String, NormMeta);  // NormMeta: {stages_applied, depth_reached, truncated}

fixpoint loop: for depth in 0..max_depth, apply all stages, stop when output equals input (fixpoint reached), else continue; returns (result, meta{depth, stages_applied}).

  • Stages (each implements NormStage): UrlDecoder (normalize/url.rs) — %XX, %uXXXX, +→space; HexDecoder (hex.rs) — 0x41, \x41, &#xNN;; UnicodeNorm (unicode.rs) — NFKC normalization, overlong UTF-8 rejection; HtmlEntityDecoder (html.rs) — &lt;, &#60;, &#x3c;; NullByteStripper (null.rs) — \0 removal (path traversal, SQLi); CaseFold (case.rs) — ASCII lowercase (SQL keywords case-insensitive).
  • SQL-specific evasion (rasp-detect/src/sqli/normalizer.rs): strips /*...*/ and /*!...*/ (MySQL version comments) before tokenization; collapses whitespace variants (\t,\n,\r,\x0b,\x0c) to a single space; reconstructs SQL string-concat evasion ('SEL'||'ECT' → 'SELECT') before keyword match; case-folds SQL keywords for matching only (preserves original for evidence); applied to SinkType::SqlQuery args before libinjection invocation.
  • Command injection normalization (rasp-detect/src/cmdi/normalizer.rs): decodes shell $'...' ANSI-C quoting (\x41→A); flags ${IFS}/$IFS word-splitting tricks as high-severity; strips null-byte injection and flags presence; counts backtick/$(...) nesting depth, flags depth > 2 as high-severity.
  • Path traversal normalization (rasp-detect/src/path/normalizer.rs): canonicalizes ..%2f, %2e%2e%2f, ..%252f (double-encoded) to ../; rejects overlong UTF-8 sequences (rasp_overlong_utf8_total); canonicalizes Windows ..\ and ..%5c to ../; strips null-byte in path + flags; applies Path::canonicalize-equivalent logic on the normalized string with no filesystem call.
  • Taint-through-normalization (rasp-taint/src/propagation.rs): normalization is a taint-propagating operation — the output of normalize_to_fixpoint inherits all input tags; NormMeta attaches to TaintedValue as evidence, showing which stages applied (signals an evasion attempt).
  • Anti-bypass invariant: NormMeta.depth_reached == max_depth emits rasp_normalization_max_depth_total, treated as suspicious and defaults to Block in fail-closed mode; truncation at max_len emits rasp_normalization_truncated_total, the truncated string is still analyzed with the limitation noted in evidence. The bypass-technique catalog normalized here follows B1 (The Web Application Hacker's Handbook, Ch. 9–14 injection/bypass techniques) and enforces §6.8 (Fail-Safe Defaults) on max-depth exhaustion.
  • rasp-bench/src/evasion_corpus.rs: 500+ evasion payloads from the OWASP testing guide + custom (double-encoded SQLi, comment-obfuscated, unicode-bypass, null-byte path traversal), each {input, attack_type, expected_action: Block}; pass criterion rasp_blocks_total / corpus_size ≥ 0.99.
  • Test harness: rasp-detect/tests/normalizer_tests.rs (fixpoint correctness, max-depth termination, overlong UTF-8 rejection, each stage independently, pipeline idempotency); rasp-detect/tests/evasion_sqli.rs, evasion_cmdi.rs, evasion_path.rs (per-attack-class evasion suites, 50+ cases each).

11.3 Deliverable

  • NormalizerPipeline + all 6 stages compile; normalize_to_fixpoint terminates for all inputs within max_depth.
  • SQL comment stripping + whitespace normalization applied before libinjection; rasp-detect evasion test suite ≥ 99% pass.
  • Path traversal canonical form: ..%252f/etc/passwd → ../../etc/passwd detected.
  • NormMeta attached to TaintedValue; visible in Detection.evidence field.
  • rasp-bench evasion-corpus report: block rate, false-negative list, per-stage hit counts.

11.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Threshold
Evasion corpus block rate (500+ payloads) ≥ 99% (Block action)
Fixpoint loop terminates for all inputs 100% within max_depth = 5
Overlong UTF-8 sequence Rejected; rasp_overlong_utf8_total++
Double-encoded %2522 → %22 → " Decoded in 2 iterations; analyzed correctly
SQL /*!50000 UNION*/ → UNION Detected by SQLi analyzer post-strip
Null-byte in path ../etc/pass\0wd Stripped; traversal still detected
Normalization overhead (per event) ≤ 50 µs p99 (Criterion microbench)
Normalizer on 1 MB adversarial input Truncated at max_len; no OOM; ≤ 100 ms
rasp_normalization_max_depth_total > 0 → audit event 100% emitted

Fixture Purpose Expected Output

fixtures/evasion/path_double_encoded.json ..%252f..%252fetc%252fpasswd Canonical ../../etc/passwd; Block

fixtures/evasion/cmdi_dollar_quote.json $'\x6c\x73' → ls Decoded; command injection detected

11.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
Fixpoint loop infinite cycle RASP hangs; request times out Decode stage produces output that re-triggers itself rasp_normalization_max_depth_total never increments (loop doesn't terminate) Hard iteration cap MAX_DECODE_DEPTH; panic-safe with catch_unwind
Over-normalization → false positive Benign encoded param decoded to SQL keyword Too-aggressive normalization of non-injection context rasp_false_positive_total metric; user reports Context-aware normalization: only normalize args bound for SQL sink for SQL normalizer
Null-byte not stripped before filesystem check Path traversal through \0 bypass Normalizer runs after path check Missing NullByteStripper in pipeline Assert NullByteStripper is first stage in path pipeline
Unicode NFKC changes meaning Ligature ﬁ → fi; false match NFKC too aggressive for non-SQL strings False positive on content with ligatures Apply NFKC only to sink-specific normalizer (SQL, cmd); not to raw content analyzers
Max-length truncation hides attack tail Attack payload crafted to exploit post-truncation max_len too small; attack in last segment rasp_normalization_truncated_total high Log truncation in evidence; increase max_len; sliding window analysis
MySQL version comment /*!N...*/ not stripped /*!50000UNION*/ bypasses detector Regex only matches /**/; misses versioned form SQLi test sqli_comment_obfuscation.json fails Extend strip regex: r#"/\*!?\d*\*/"#
Double-encoded slash in Windows path %255c → %5c → \; traversal missed Single decode iteration only Path evasion fixture fails on Windows paths Ensure pipeline runs 2+ passes; Windows path normalizer handles \ → /

11.7 Performance Concerns

Concern Mitigation
NFKC normalization allocation unicode-normalization crate; Cow<str> → allocates only on change
Regex compilation for SQL comment strip Pre-compiled lazy_static! Regex; no per-event compile
Fixpoint loop on short inputs Early exit: input len < 8 → skip multi-stage loop
Pipeline stage chain allocation Cow<str> threading: each stage borrows if no change; allocates only on mutation
Adversarial large inputs max_len truncation at entry; O(max_len) not O(input_len)
normalize_to_fixpoint on every tainted arg Skipped entirely if !is_tainted(&arg) (short-circuit from Phase 10 applies first)
Thread-local pipeline instance Avoid per-event Vec allocation for stages; stages are Box<dyn NormStage> allocated once at init

11.8 Edge Cases

Case Handling
Empty string input Pipeline returns empty; no stages applied; Allow
Input is pure binary (non-UTF8) from_utf8_lossy; replace invalid sequences with U+FFFD; flag rasp_invalid_utf8_total
Multiple concurrent normalizations of same arg Thread-local pipeline; no shared mutable state
Normalizer applied to non-string arg type (e.g., blob) Convert to hex string; apply hex decoder stage only
Recursive URL-encoding depth > max_depth Stopped; flagged suspicious; rasp_normalization_max_depth_total++
Payload exactly at max_len boundary Analyzed; no truncation; boundary tested in fixture
Mixed encoding in single arg (%2522%3B DROP) Full pipeline decodes entire arg to canonical before analysis
Windows CRLF in SQL arg Collapsed to space by whitespace normalizer

11.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery
Normalizer bug allows encoded bypass Evasion corpus regression test fails in CI All requests with that encoding variant bypass Deploy virtual patch rule for undetected pattern; fix normalizer Hotfix normalizer stage; hot-reload policy with virtual patch
Normalizer OOM on adversarial 10 MB input Process OOM; RASP agent crash Fail-closed: protected app unaffected if agent is out-of-process max_len cap prevents; in-process: catch_unwind + fail_mode Reduce max_len; add memory limit to agent process
False positive surge after normalizer change rasp_false_positive_total spike; user complaints Legitimate requests blocked Switch to Monitor mode; rollback normalizer version Revert normalizer; re-run benign corpus; fix over-normalization
Regex catastrophic backtracking on SQL strip Analyzer hangs; request timeout Single-request hang; bounded by MAX_ITERATIONS limit Use linear-time regex engine (regex crate guarantees linear) Already mitigated: regex crate is linear; add timeout wrapper as defense-in-depth
Postmortem: attach NormMeta.stages_applied, depth_reached, truncated from Detection.evidence to each evasion incident.

11.10 Operational Notes

Item Detail
Metrics rasp_normalization_stage_hits_total{stage} (counter per stage), rasp_normalization_depth_histogram, rasp_normalization_max_depth_total, rasp_normalization_truncated_total, rasp_overlong_utf8_total, rasp_invalid_utf8_total
Evidence in SIEM Detection.evidence includes NormMeta: stages applied, depth, truncated; enables forensic reconstruction of attacker's payload
Evasion corpus CI rasp-bench evasion-corpus --fail-below 0.99; blocks PR on regression
Normalizer tuning rasp-cli config set normalizer.max_depth=5 normalizer.max_len=65536; hot-reloadable
Adding new evasion stage Implement NormStage; register in NormalizerPipeline::default(); add fixtures; run evasion corpus
Version compatibility Normalizer pipeline version stored in NormMeta; SIEM consumers can filter by version for regression analysis
Threat intel integration New evasion techniques from OWASP/CVE feed → new NormStage or SQL strip regex update → virtual patch + normalizer deploy

24. Phase 12 — Observability — attack telemetry, SIEM/CEF export, metrics (detections/blocks/overhead/FP rate), tracing, audit log, dashboards

12.1 Objectives

  • Emit structured attack events from rasp-telemetry within <1 ms of Detection finalization; no blocking of the inline guard path.
  • Support CEF (Common Event Format) v23 and JSON-LD structured log as dual wire formats; pluggable transport (UDP syslog, TCP syslog-TLS, HTTP/HTTPS webhook, file sink).
  • Expose Prometheus-compatible /metrics endpoint from rasp-telemetry; counters: rasp_detections_total{sink,severity,action}, rasp_blocks_total{rule_id}, rasp_hook_latency_us{sink,p50,p95,p99}, rasp_taint_ops_total, rasp_fp_rate_gauge.
  • Emit OpenTelemetry spans per hook invocation (parent = request trace span) to a configurable OTLP collector; propagate correlation_id as span attribute.
  • Maintain tamper-evident append-only audit log (HMAC-SHA256 chained entries) for block decisions; operators can replay + verify.
  • Constraints: telemetry channel MUST be bounded (backpressure); on channel full → drop + increment rasp_telemetry_drops_total; NEVER block the guard thread.

12.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

  • rasp-telemetry crate layout:
rasp-telemetry/src/
  lib.rs         — TelemetryHandle, init(), shutdown()
  event.rs       — AttackEvent, AuditEntry, TelemetryPayload
  export/cef.rs, json.rs, syslog.rs, webhook.rs, file.rs   — CEF/JSON-LD serializers; UDP/TCP+TLS, HTTP POST batching, rotating file sink
  metrics.rs     — prometheus::Registry wrapper; gauge/counter/histogram builders
  tracing.rs     — opentelemetry-otlp span emitter; hook-span lifecycle
  audit.rs       — append-only audit log; HMAC chain; rotation + verification
  channel.rs     — bounded crossbeam channel (cap=4096); producer API

AttackEvent fields: event_id: Uuid, timestamp: DateTime<Utc>, detection: Detection, request_ctx_snapshot: HttpInfo, taint_path: Vec<TaintTag>, action: Action, rasp_version: &'static str.

  • Dispatch path (inline guard side): (1) rasp-protect calls TelemetryHandle::send(AttackEvent) — non-blocking try_send on the crossbeam bounded channel, full ⇒ increment drop counter + log warn to stderr (never to the channel); (2) background tokio task drains the channel, fans out to enabled sinks in parallel; (3) audit log entry written synchronously (append to mmap'd file) before fan-out — must not be skipped on drop.
  • CEF serialization (cef.rs): header CEF:0|Vendor|rasp-engine|{version}|{rule_id}|{sink}|{severity_int}|; extensions src={client_ip} request={path} msg={evidence_truncated_512} cs1={taint_sources} cs2={confidence}; severity mapping Critical→10, High→7, Medium→5, Low→3. CEF field layout follows S9 (ArcSight CEF Standard); the unconditional per-event emission itself is §6.7 (Observability by Default) in force.
  • Prometheus metrics (metrics.rs): rasp_hook_latency_us is a Histogram with buckets [10,50,100,250,500,1000,5000]; FP rate is operator-reported via rasp-cli report-fp --event-id=<uuid> (stored in a side-channel log), rasp_fp_rate_gauge updated from a sliding window of the last 10k decisions; all labels pre-validated against an allowlist to prevent cardinality explosion.
  • OTel span (tracing.rs): one span per HookEvent, parent resolved from RequestContext.trace_ctx (W3C TraceContext); attributes rasp.sink, rasp.action, rasp.rule_id, rasp.confidence, rasp.tainted; error status set on Block, span closed in rasp-protect post-action.
  • Audit log (audit.rs): file {audit_dir}/rasp-audit-{date}.log, one NDJSON line per entry { seq, timestamp, event_id, action, rule_id, hmac_prev, hmac_self }; hmac_self = HMAC-SHA256(key, seq||timestamp||event_id||action||hmac_prev); key loaded from env RASP_AUDIT_KEY (32-byte hex), rotation re-chains on rollover with a new key; verified via rasp-cli audit verify --file=<path> --key=<hex>.
  • Dashboard spec (not shipped, spec only): Grafana JSON template in rasp-telemetry/dashboards/rasp-overview.json; panels — detections/min by sink, block rate, p95 hook latency by sink, FP rate trend, top-5 attack sources.

12.3 Deliverable

  • rasp-telemetry compiles, exposes TelemetryHandle consumed by rasp-protect.
  • rasp-cli telemetry test sends a synthetic AttackEvent through all enabled sinks and prints delivery confirmation.
  • Prometheus /metrics returns valid exposition format (tested via prometheus_parse crate).
  • Audit log written + verifiable via rasp-cli audit verify.
  • OTel spans visible in local Jaeger (docker-compose.telemetry.yml in rasp-bench).
  • CEF output validated by running snare or offline CEF parser fixture.

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Threshold
test_telemetry_nonblocking — 10k rapid sends on full channel zero guard-thread blocks; drop counter == overflow count
test_cef_format — parse emitted CEF with libcef parser 0 parse errors; all mandatory ext fields present
test_audit_chain — verify 1k-entry audit log chain valid; tampered entry detected at correct seq
test_metrics_cardinality — inject 1k distinct rule_ids label allowlist rejects overflow; no OOM
test_otel_span_parent — hook inside traced request child span has correct parent trace_id + span_id
Overhead: telemetry path (send + audit append) <200 µs p99 on inline path
Drop rate under 100% channel saturation >0% drop logged; guard never blocks
test_fp_rate_update gauge updates within 1s of CLI report-fp call

12.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected Output
fixtures/telemetry/attack_event_sqli.json canonical SQLi event CEF line matches golden attack_event_sqli.cef
fixtures/telemetry/attack_event_cmdi.json command injection JSON-LD output validates against schema
fixtures/telemetry/audit_chain_1000.ndjson 1k-entry chain rasp-cli audit verify exits 0
fixtures/telemetry/audit_chain_tampered.ndjson entry 500 modified exits nonzero, reports seq=500
fixtures/telemetry/cef_malformed_evidence.txt evidence with ` =` chars
fixtures/telemetry/metrics_exposition.txt expected Prometheus text prometheus_parse round-trips identically
fixtures/telemetry/otel_span_golden.json OTel span JSON parent/child IDs correct; attributes present
fixtures/telemetry/channel_overflow_50k.sh flood script drop counter matches overflow; no panic

12.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
Blocking on full channel Guard latency spikes to ms+ try_send replaced by send under review rasp_hook_latency_us p99 jump Revert to try_send; add drop counter
CEF field injection Log aggregator parses wrong fields Unescaped ` or=` in evidence Corrupted CEF in SIEM search
Audit key in env visible to child procs Key exfiltrated fork/execve inheriting env strace on child proc Use sealed secret / prctl unshare
OTel exporter goroutine leak FD exhaustion after 10k requests Span not closed on Block path FD count via /proc/self/fd Always close span in finally-equivalent
Metric label cardinality explosion Prometheus OOM Attacker-controlled rule_id in label Prometheus heap grows unbounded Pre-validated label allowlist; reject unknowns
Audit file rotation race Two processes write same file Fork + exec without lock Duplicate seq numbers in log File lock (flock) around rotation; unique suffix
Webhook retry storm Outbound connection flood Exponential retry without jitter outbound connection count Capped retry (3x) + jitter + circuit breaker
Partial audit write Truncated NDJSON line SIGKILL during write syscall verify reports JSON parse error at last line Atomic write via temp file + rename

12.7 Performance Concerns

  • Channel drain latency: background task uses recv_timeout(1ms) to batch up to 64 events before fan-out → amortize per-event overhead.
  • CEF serialization alloc: pre-allocate String::with_capacity(512) per event; avoid format! macro per field.
  • Audit append: mmap + msync(MS_ASYNC) preferred over write syscall per entry; flush on shutdown or 100ms timer.
  • Histogram buckets: pre-compute bucket boundaries at init; observe() is O(log n) binary search — keep bucket count ≤12.
  • OTel batch exporter: configure max_export_batch_size=512, scheduled_delay_ms=5000; do not export per-span.
  • Syslog TCP writes: buffer 64KB before flush; on TLS, avoid renegotiation per event (session resume).
  • Label intern: intern sink/rule_id strings at registration; compare by pointer in hot path.
  • FP gauge update: sliding window maintained as VecDeque<bool> of last 10k; pop-front/push-back on each decision — O(1).

12.8 Edge Cases

  • Evidence string contains NUL bytes (binary payload) → CEF truncates at first NUL; JSON-LD base64-encodes raw bytes.
  • Clock skew between RASP and SIEM → embed rasp_monotonic_ns alongside wall clock; SIEM can detect reorder.
  • Audit log dir not writable → fail closed (block all ops) if fail_mode=Closed; emit rasp_telemetry_audit_error_total.
  • Concurrent report-fp CLI calls → Mutex<SlidingWindow> guard; deadlock impossible (single lock, no nesting).
  • OTel collector unreachable → spans queued in-memory up to max_queue_size=2048; overflow silently dropped (not guard concern).
  • Event UUID collision (astronomically unlikely) → log warning; downstream deduplicate by (timestamp, rule_id, request_id).
  • Very long taint path (>1000 hops) → truncate taint_path at 64 entries in AttackEvent; append taint_path_truncated: true.
  • Telemetry shutdown race → TelemetryHandle::shutdown() drains channel with recv_timeout(500ms) before closing sinks.

12.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery Operator Visibility
Channel permanently full rasp_telemetry_drops_total rising Events lost; attacks silently unlogged Guard unaffected; sink consumers restarted Increase channel cap or throttle source Alert on drop rate >1%
Audit log corruption rasp-cli audit verify exits nonzero Audit untrustworthy from corruption point Rotate to new file; preserve corrupt for forensics Re-initialize chain rasp_audit_chain_errors_total
SIEM sink TLS failure rasp_telemetry_sink_errors_total{sink=syslog_tls} Events queued in channel; eventually dropped Fallback to file sink Cert rotation; reconnect with backoff Error logged to stderr
Prometheus OOM Process crash Metrics lost; no alerting Restart with cardinality guard Label allowlist enforcement kube OOMKilled event
OTel exporter deadlock Span close blocks guard thread Guard latency →∞ Timeout on exporter (500ms max) Async exporter with detached task Hook latency histogram spike
Postmortem guidance: on attack event gap, check rasp_telemetry_drops_total + rasp_telemetry_sink_errors_total; correlate gap timestamp with rasp_audit_chain_errors_total; diff audit file seqs to bound lost event window.

12.10 Operational Notes

  • Deploy: RASP_AUDIT_KEY, RASP_SIEM_ENDPOINT, RASP_OTLP_ENDPOINT via env or mounted secret; never in config file committed to repo.
  • Rollback: telemetry is a side-channel; disabling it (set telemetry.enabled=false) has zero effect on guard correctness.
  • Rotation: audit logs rotated daily + at 256MB; retain 30 days by default; rasp-cli audit prune --older-than=30d.
  • Compatibility: CEF v23 format; bump version field on schema change; SIEM parser version pinned in rasp-telemetry/Cargo.toml comment.
  • Dashboard migration: Grafana JSON versioned in rasp-telemetry/dashboards/; changelog in adjacent CHANGELOG.md.
  • Key rotation: rasp-cli audit rotate-key --new-key=<hex> closes current file, starts new chain; old file remains verifiable with old key.
  • Tracing sampling: default 100% for Block events; configurable telemetry.trace_sample_rate (0.0–1.0) for Monitor events to control OTel volume.
  • Alert thresholds: rasp_blocks_total rate drop-to-zero for >5min → alert (guard may be disabled); rasp_hook_latency_us{p99} >1000µs → page.

25. Phase 13 — Conformance + security testing — OWASP attack corpus, benign false-positive corpus, differential vs WAF, analyzer fuzzing, overhead benchmarks, soak

13.1 Objectives

  • Validate detection accuracy against a curated OWASP-aligned attack corpus (SQLi, CMDi, path traversal, SSRF, deserialization, XXE, LDAP): ≥95% true-positive rate across corpus.
  • Validate low false-positive rate against benign corpus: ≤1% FP rate across 10k benign requests per sink type.
  • Differential testing vs WAF (project 338): identify class of attacks RASP detects that WAF misses (encoded/obfuscated payloads reaching sink) and vice versa; document decision boundary.
  • Fuzz every per-sink analyzer in rasp-detect with cargo-fuzz + libFuzzer; zero panics/UB in fuzz targets.
  • Overhead benchmark: inline guard adds <5% p95 latency on benign traffic; taint tracking adds <2% on untainted paths.
  • Soak test: 72-hour continuous load at 5000 req/s; zero memory leaks (RASP_LEAK_THRESHOLD=10MB/hr), zero panics, zero host-safety violations.
  • Constraints: all tests run in CI (rasp-bench crate); fuzz targets require nightly only for libFuzzer driver; benchmarks run on cargo bench stable.

13.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

  • rasp-bench crate layout:
rasp-bench/
  src/lib.rs                 — shared corpus loader, fixture runner types
  src/corpus/attack.rs, benign.rs, loader.rs   — AttackFixture{payload,sink,expected_action,rule_id}; BenignFixture{payload,sink}; loads fixtures/corpus/**/*.json + validates schema
  src/harness/end_to_end.rs, differential.rs, overhead.rs, soak.rs   — full-pipeline driver; RASP-vs-WAF-stub comparator; Criterion TSC latency; multi-threaded load + leak/panic detector
  src/fuzz/fuzz_{sqli,cmdi,path,xml,deser,url}.rs   — libFuzzer targets: SQLi, CMDi, path traversal, XML/XXE, deserialization, SSRF URL analyzers
  benches/inline_guard.rs, taint_propagation.rs, detect_sqli.rs   — Criterion: benign p50/p95/p99, string-concat taint cost, SQLi throughput
  fixtures/corpus/attack/, benign/, differential/   — attack payloads, benign payloads, RASP-vs-WAF divergent payloads
  • Attack corpus construction: sourced from the OWASP Testing Guide, SQLi cheat sheet, libinjection test suite, SecLists payloads; schema { id, sink, payload_b64, taint_source, expected_action, expected_rule_id, notes }; minimum counts per sink — SQLi ≥200, CMDi ≥100, PathTraversal ≥100, SSRF ≥80, Deser ≥60, XXE ≥50, LDAP ≥40; augmented with encoding variants (URL-encode, double-encode, unicode normalization, hex escape, comment obfuscation) via corpus/loader.rs::expand_variants(). Corpus composition follows S4 (OWASP Top 10 attack taxonomy) and reuses the methodology of R5 (libinjection's 100k+ vector test corpus).
  • Benign corpus construction: real-world param values (product names, search terms, legitimate file paths, JSON API bodies, XML configs) sourced from Common Crawl samples, Wikipedia titles, OpenAPI example values; sink-specific — benign parameterized SQL queries, benign shell commands (no meta-chars), safe file paths (no ..).
  • End-to-end harness (harness/end_to_end.rs): (1) construct RequestContext with payload as HTTP param taint source; (2) invoke the rasp-hook mock with HookEvent { sink, args: SinkArgs::from(payload) }; (3) run the full pipeline through rasp-protect::guard(); (4) assert Detection.action == expected_action (and rule_id match if Block); (5) record result, accumulating TP/TN/FP/FN counts.
  • Differential test (harness/differential.rs): WAF stub trait WafDecision { fn decide(raw_http_param: &str) -> Action; } reads from a golden WAF output file; compares (RASP=Block, WAF=Allow) ⇒ RASP advantage (encode bypass), (RASP=Allow, WAF=Block) ⇒ WAF advantage (protocol-level block), both agree ⇒ concordant; outputs differential_report.json with counts + example payloads per quadrant.
  • Fuzz targets (fuzz/*.rs): each deserializes arbitrary::Arbitrary input, calls the analyzer directly, asserts no panic via std::panic::catch_unwind; corpus seeds are the existing attack fixtures in fixtures/corpus/attack/; run via cargo +nightly fuzz run fuzz_sqli -- -max_len=65536 -runs=1000000; coverage via cargo +nightly fuzz coverage fuzz_sqli → lcov.info artifact.
  • Overhead benchmark (benches/inline_guard.rs): Criterion BenchmarkGroups benign_no_taint, benign_with_taint_untriggered, benign_tainted_all_sinks; baseline is a raw sink call without RASP interception (#[cfg(feature="no-rasp")] shim); overhead = (rasp_latency - baseline_latency) / baseline_latency * 100; TSC measurement via criterion::measurement::WallTime, thread pinned to a core via core_affinity.
  • Soak test (harness/soak.rs): spawns 16 worker threads, each driving the end-to-end harness at 312.5 req/s (5000 total req/s); monitors jemalloc_ctl epoch + stats.allocated sampled every 10s (leak = slope >10MB/hr); panic detector via std::panic::set_hook fails the test immediately on any panic; host-safety check wraps the harness in catch_unwind, asserting zero propagated panics; duration 72h in nightly CI, 5min smoke in PR CI (SOAK_DURATION=300s override).

13.3 Deliverable

  • cargo test -p rasp-bench runs corpus tests; prints TP/FP/FN/TN counts + rates per sink.
  • cargo bench -p rasp-bench produces Criterion HTML report with overhead percentages.
  • cargo +nightly fuzz run fuzz_sqli runs without panic for ≥1M inputs.
  • differential_report.json generated, showing RASP vs WAF decision quadrant counts.
  • Soak smoke (5min) passes in CI; 72h soak passes in nightly pipeline.
  • Coverage report (lcov.info) archived as CI artifact; rasp-detect line coverage ≥85%.

13.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Threshold
SQLi TP rate (corpus) ≥95%
CMDi TP rate ≥95%
Path traversal TP rate ≥93%
SSRF TP rate ≥90%
XXE TP rate ≥90%
Deserialization TP rate ≥88%

Benign FP rate (SQL parameterized queries) ≤0.1%
Inline guard overhead p95 (benign, no taint) <2%
Inline guard overhead p95 (benign, tainted) <5%
Fuzz: zero panics in 1M runs per target pass
Soak 5min smoke: zero leaks, panics pass

rasp-detect line coverage ≥85%

13.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
Corpus label noise TP rate appears high; FP rate masked Incorrectly labeled fixtures (attack marked benign) Manual audit 10% sample; rule_id mismatch Fixture review pipeline; cross-check with libinjection
Encoding variant gap Encoded SQLi bypasses; FN Analyzer decodes only one encoding layer Payload %2527 not decoded twice Add multi-pass URL decode in rasp-detect::sqli pre-process
Fuzz target UB without sanitizer ASAN/MSAN catches heap corruption Unsafe block in rasp-detect parser cargo fuzz with ASAN_OPTIONS Fix unsafe block; add #[deny(unsafe_code)] guard
Benchmark warm-up bias Overhead appears lower than reality JIT-like branch predictor warm-up; cache primed Criterion warm-up iteration count too low warm_up_time(Duration::from_secs(5))
Soak memory growth from interned strings Leak detected Unbounded HashSet of taint source strings Monotone stats.allocated slope Cap intern table size; LRU eviction
False alarm from benign XML XXE FP on self-contained DTDs DTD presence triggers XXE rule unconditionally FP rate spike on fixtures/corpus/benign/xml_doctype.json Refine rule: block only external entity refs
Differential report drift WAF golden file stale WAF behavior changed; golden not updated Quadrant counts shift unexpectedly Lock WAF golden to versioned snapshot; CI diff alert
Soak thread starvation Req/s drops below 5000 Lock contention in RequestContext store Throughput counter from soak harness Sharded context store; benchmark under contention

13.7 Performance Concerns

  • Corpus loader allocation: load all fixtures at process start into Arc<[AttackFixture]>, shared across test threads (avoids repeated deserialization); Criterion measurement variance mitigated by pinning the benchmark thread to an isolated CPU core (core_affinity::set_for_current) and disabling CPU frequency scaling in CI via the cpufreq governor.
  • Fuzz throughput: cargo-fuzz -jobs=$(nproc) for parallel corpus exploration, no I/O in the fuzz target critical path; soak thread count of 16 balances CPU utilization vs. context-switch overhead on an 8-core CI runner (tunable via SOAK_THREADS).
  • Memory sampling overhead: jemalloc_ctl epoch refresh is O(1), sampled every 10s to avoid interfering with the allocation hot path; overhead measurement baseline uses a #[cfg(feature="no-rasp")] compile-time gate (same code path, no guard calls) to isolate RASP cost cleanly.
  • Benign short-circuit: verify rasp-taint::is_tainted() returns false fast for benign untainted args, benchmarked separately (benign_no_taint group); differential report generation runs RASP and the WAF stub sequentially per payload (not parallel) to avoid scheduling jitter affecting latency measurement.

13.8 Edge Cases

  • Payload exactly at max_len boundary (65535 bytes) in fuzz: analyzer must handle without buffer overread (explicit length check); concurrent corpus test threads modifying shared RequestContext: each test creates an isolated RequestContext instance, no sharing.
  • Soak test clock wraparound (monotonic > 72h): uses Instant::elapsed().as_secs() as u64, no overflow in a realistic soak window; fixture file with invalid UTF-8 in the payload field: loader.rs base64-decodes payloads, so arbitrary bytes are valid.
  • Fuzz input triggers std::fmt::Debug recursion on a deeply nested taint path: capped at 64 before Debug formatting; benign corpus entry with a SQL keyword in a product name ("SELECT brand"): the parameterized-query rule must not flag keyword-in-value without injection syntax.
  • Zero-length payload: analyzers must return Allow without panic (fixtures/corpus/edge/empty_payload.json); replay of soak corpus from a prior run: loader.rs supports --replay-corpus=<dir> for deterministic reproduction.

13.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery Operator Visibility
TP rate below threshold CI corpus test fails Attack class undetected in production Monitor mode; raise alert Improve analyzer rules; add fixture regression CI report with per-sink breakdown
FP rate above 1% CI benign test fails Legit requests blocked Switch to Monitor mode Refine rule; add waiver FP histogram in telemetry
Fuzz panic discovered CI fuzz job fails Potential crash in production on adversarial input Quarantine analyzer variant Fix + regression fixture Fuzz crash artifact in CI
Soak leak detected Memory slope exceeds threshold OOM in long-running production deployment Restart RASP; heap profile Identify allocation source via jemalloc stats rasp_process_memory_bytes gauge
Overhead >5% p95 Benchmark CI gate fails Production latency SLA breach Disable expensive analyzer Profile hot path; optimize Criterion HTML report diff
Differential: RASP misses WAF-caught attacks Differential quadrant count Attacks bypass RASP at sink level WAF as complementary layer Add RASP rule for missed class differential_report.json quadrant
Postmortem: on production FP spike, correlate with last corpus test FP rate; check if new benign corpus entry was missing; replay known_fp fixture to reproduce; add to regression set.

13.10 Operational Notes

  • CI integration: cargo test -p rasp-bench --test corpus in every PR; cargo bench in nightly; cargo +nightly fuzz in weekly scheduled job.
  • Corpus versioning: fixtures/corpus/ is git-tracked; corpus schema version in fixtures/corpus/VERSION; breaking changes require migration script.
  • Fuzz artifact retention: crash inputs saved to fuzz/artifacts/; .gitignore exempts them; archived as CI artifacts for 90 days.
  • Coverage gating: cargo llvm-cov --lcov run in CI; coverage drop >2% blocks merge.
  • Differential update workflow: when WAF behavior changes, regenerate golden with rasp-cli diff regenerate-golden; commit updated fixtures/corpus/differential/waf_golden.json.
  • Soak nightly scheduling: triggered by cron in CI; results posted to Slack channel #rasp-soak; failure pages on-call.
  • Benchmark baseline pinning: Criterion baseline stored in benches/.criterion/ (cargo bench -- --save-baseline main on merge to main; PRs compared via -- --load-baseline main); regulatory note: attack corpus payloads are test artifacts, fixtures/corpus/attack/ is access-controlled in repo settings and never deployed to production systems.

Threat Model

Trust Boundaries and Attack Surface

Boundary Direction Risk
App runtime → rasp-hook ABI Inbound Malicious hook args (oversized, malformed, adversarial encoding)
rasp-hook → rasp-protect Internal Panic propagation, context corruption, deadlock
rasp-protect → protected app Outbound Block signal: must be safe error, not crash
rasp-telemetry → SIEM Outbound Log injection, CEF format abuse, credential leak in events
Policy config load Inbound Tampered rules file, supply-chain poisoning
rasp-cli operator interface Inbound Privilege escalation via misconfiguration, unsafe reload

Malicious Client Threats

Threat Vector Mitigation Telemetry
SQLi payload crafted to evade tokenizer HTTP param → SQL sink libinjection-backed semantic + signature dual pass; obfuscation normalization Detection.evidence includes raw + normalized form
Encoding abuse (%%2527, Unicode bypass) HTTP path/param Decode-before-taint in rasp-context; normalize UTF-8/NFC at source capture TaintSource records pre/post-decode pair
Null-byte injection FileOpen args rasp-detect path-traversal check rejects embedded NUL Emit Severity::Critical event
Overlong arg DoS Any sink Args capped at MAX_SINK_ARG_BYTES (configurable, default 1 MiB); excess truncated, flag set TruncatedArg flag in HookEvent
Replay attack on correlation ID Context header injection correlation_id derived server-side (UUID v7 at request ingress); app-supplied header ignored by default Mismatch → log warning, use server-side id
Polyglot payload (SQLi + XSS in same string) SQL sink SQLi analyzer operates on SQL-tokenized view; XSS not a RASP sink (different analyzer domain) Detection tagged per-sink-type
Blind injection (time-based) SQL sink Hook fires at query construction time, not DB round-trip; query content analyzed regardless of outcome No DB timing dependency
Deserialization gadget chain Deserialize sink Gadget class-allowlist + deny-list; deny unknown classes by default (fail-closed for deserialize sink) Class name + serialized blob length in event
XXE entity expansion bomb XmlParse sink Entity depth + expansion count limits enforced before parse completes XXEExpansion detection + entity counts
SSRF via DNS rebinding HttpRequest sink Allowlist checked at construction time; re-check after resolution if adapter provides resolved IP SSRFViaRebind detection
LDAP injection via DN component LdapQuery sink DN tokenization + disallow unescaped special chars (comma, semicolon, slash, null) Evidence includes DN tokens

Resource Exhaustion and DoS

Vector Mitigation
Taint map growth (huge request, many params) TaintMap capped at MAX_TAINT_ENTRIES; oldest evicted LRU; TaintOverflow flag set
Call stack depth explosion rasp-context call stack capped at MAX_CALL_DEPTH; deeper frames dropped, StackTruncated flag
High sink-call rate (tight loop calling SQL) Per-request sink call counter; after MAX_SINKS_PER_REQUEST exceeded, subsequent calls fast-path allow + emit ThrottleEvent
Analyzer regex catastrophic backtracking All regex pre-compiled and bounded by linear-time engine (regex crate NFA); no PCRE backtracking
rasp-telemetry queue saturation Bounded channel (default 64k events); on full → drop oldest + increment DroppedEvents counter; never block hook path
Policy reload loop (misconfigured watch) Debounce 5s; exponential back-off on parse error; max reload rate 1/min
Context store leak (tasks that never complete) Async task-local scoped to task lifetime; thread-local cleared at request boundary via RAII guard

Memory Corruption and Privilege Escalation

Risk Mitigation
Unsafe in hook trampoline unsafe blocks confined to rasp-hook trampolines; reviewed/audited section; #[forbid(unsafe_code)] on all other crates
Use-after-free via shared SinkArgs reference SinkArgs cloned into HookEvent immediately at hook entry; no borrowed refs escape hook boundary
Stack overflow in recursive analyzer Analyzers iterative-only; recursion depth bounded via explicit stack in rasp-detect
Privilege escalation via policy file Policy file loaded by app process (same privilege); RASP does not escalate privilege; file integrity via HMAC or file-system permissions
FFI boundary corruption (adapter ABI) Hook ABI uses extern "C" with defined null-pointer contracts; rasp-hook validates all pointer args before deref

Supply-Chain and Persistence Corruption

Risk Mitigation
Malicious crate dependency cargo vet + cargo-deny license + advisory checks in CI; lock file committed
Policy file tampering at rest Optional HMAC-SHA256 signature on policy; mismatch → refuse load, emit SecurityAlert
Rule database corruption Rules validated at load (schema + semantic); partial load rejected; atomic swap of rule set
Side-loading a fake rasp-hook Adapter registration requires capability token; duplicate registration rejected

Operator Mistakes

Mistake Guard-rail
Deploy in Block mode without baseline CLI warns; recommend Monitor-first workflow; audit log captures mode change
Waiver covering all rules Policy validation rejects catch-all waiver without explicit --force flag
Forget to rotate correlation ID secret rasp-cli check-config warns if correlation key unchanged > 90 days
Point telemetry at plain HTTP endpoint Telemetry TLS required by default; plain HTTP requires explicit allow_insecure_telemetry = true

Failure Modes & Recovery

Taxonomy Overview

Failure Classes:
  F1  Hook ABI fault (panic, OOM, timeout in hook path)
  F2  Context store corruption / leak
  F3  Taint engine failure (budget exceeded, propagation bug)
  F4  Analyzer crash / hang
  F5  Policy load failure (parse error, schema mismatch, HMAC fail)
  F6  Telemetry pipeline failure (channel full, SIEM unreachable)
  F7  Config / CLI failure (bad flag, missing file)
  F8  Adapter / runtime-instrumentation mismatch
  F9  Resource exhaustion (memory, CPU, file descriptors)
  F10 Cascading: multiple subsystems failing simultaneously

F1 — Hook ABI Fault

Attribute Detail
Symptoms Hook path returns error to adapter; app receives no interception; InternalError events in telemetry
Root causes Panic in rasp-protect pipeline; OOM allocating HookEvent; clock read failure
Detection catch_unwind wraps entire hook dispatch; panic → log backtrace + emit F1 event
Automatic recovery On panic: execute policy fail_mode (Open → allow, Closed → block); hook returns without crashing app
Host-safety invariant Hook NEVER propagates panic into instrumented runtime — catch_unwind boundary is absolute
Manual recovery Inspect telemetry InternalError events; upgrade rasp-* if known panic; set fail_mode=Open temporarily
Forensic artifacts Structured panic log with backtrace, HookEvent partial state, context_id
Long-term mitigation Fuzz hook path with malformed SinkArgs; add regression test for panic scenario

F2 — Context Store Corruption / Leak

Attribute Detail
Symptoms Wrong taint data associated to request; context_id mismatch; memory grows unbounded
Root causes Thread-local not cleared at request boundary; async task-local scope escaped; concurrent mutation
Detection RequestContext validates context_id on every lookup; mismatch → ContextMismatch event
Automatic recovery On mismatch → discard stale context, create fresh; taint data lost → conservative: treat args as tainted
Manual recovery Restart app process to clear all thread-locals; inspect heap for rasp-context allocation pattern
Forensic artifacts ContextMismatch events with stale vs current context_id
Long-term mitigation RAII guard (RequestGuard) enforces clear on drop; async integration tests with concurrent requests

F3 — Taint Engine Failure

Attribute Detail
Symptoms TaintOverflow flag on HookEvent; false negatives (missed injections on budget-exceeded args)
Root causes TaintMap budget exceeded; propagation op not tracked (untracked op documented gap)
Detection TaintOverflow flag emitted in telemetry; per-request TaintBudgetUsed metric
Automatic recovery On budget exceeded: set TaintOverflow, mark all sink args as conservatively tainted → triggers detection even without precise path
Manual recovery Increase max_taint_entries in config; review untracked ops in taint soundness doc
Forensic artifacts TaintOverflow events with budget watermark
Long-term mitigation IAST-style offline taint analysis to discover untracked ops; corpus-based taint budget tuning

F4 — Analyzer Crash / Hang

Attribute Detail
Symptoms Hook latency spike; analysis returns AnalyzerTimeout error; missed detections
Root causes Regex engine edge case (bounded, but possible); oversized arg bypassing arg cap (bug); infinite loop in semantic analyzer
Detection Per-analyzer timeout (default 2 ms wall clock via Instant check at loop iterations); AnalyzerTimeout event
Automatic recovery Timeout → return Detection::Uncertain; policy treats Uncertain as fail_mode default
Manual recovery Identify which analyzer timed out (AnalyzerTimeout event includes SinkType); add arg to fuzz corpus
Forensic artifacts AnalyzerTimeout events with SinkType, arg truncated preview, elapsed_us
Long-term mitigation libFuzzer harness on all analyzer entry points; property-based tests with arbitrary args

F5 — Policy Load Failure

Attribute Detail
Symptoms Engine runs with stale policy; policy reload rejected; HMAC mismatch error
Root causes Parse error in TOML/JSON rule file; schema version mismatch; disk corruption; tampered file
Detection PolicyLoadError event with parse error detail; rasp-cli policy validate pre-checks
Automatic recovery Keep current (last-good) policy in memory; reject new load; emit alert; do NOT fail-open automatically
Manual recovery Fix rule file; re-sign HMAC; trigger reload via rasp-cli reload-policy
Forensic artifacts PolicyLoadError with file path, parse error, HMAC actual vs expected
Long-term mitigation CI validates all policy files before deploy; checksums stored in version control

F6 — Telemetry Pipeline Failure

Attribute Detail
Symptoms DroppedEvents counter increments; SIEM receives no events; disk write errors
Root causes SIEM endpoint unreachable; TLS cert expired; telemetry channel full; disk full
Detection DroppedEvents gauge; TelemetryError events on stderr fallback; SIEM delivery failure counter
Automatic recovery On channel full: drop oldest events, log to stderr fallback; on SIEM TCP fail: buffer to local file up to max_local_buffer_bytes
Manual recovery Fix SIEM endpoint; rotate TLS cert; drain local buffer with rasp-cli flush-telemetry
Forensic artifacts Local buffer file with missed events; DroppedEvents total in metrics
Long-term mitigation Dual-sink telemetry (SIEM + local file); circuit breaker on SIEM exporter

F7 — Config / CLI Failure

Attribute Detail
Symptoms rasp-cli exits non-zero; engine starts with defaults; incorrect mode
Root causes Missing required flag; file not found; permission denied; deprecated field used
Detection CLI validates all required fields before engine starts; exits with structured error JSON
Automatic recovery N/A — CLI fail is pre-start; engine not started if config invalid
Manual recovery Fix config; re-run CLI
Forensic artifacts CLI stderr structured error with field name + error kind

F8 — Adapter / Runtime Instrumentation Mismatch

Attribute Detail
Symptoms Hooks not firing (silent miss); wrong SinkType reported; HookEvent arg types mismatched
Root causes Adapter built against different rasp-hook ABI version; bytecode weaving missed a sink method
Detection AdapterRegistration records ABI version; mismatch → AdapterVersionMismatch error; missing-sink audit via rasp-bench
Automatic recovery On ABI mismatch: refuse adapter registration; engine continues without that adapter's sinks
Manual recovery Rebuild adapter against current rasp-hook version; re-instrument target runtime
Forensic artifacts AdapterVersionMismatch event with expected vs actual ABI semver

F9 — Resource Exhaustion

Attribute Detail
Symptoms OOM kill; CPU pegged; file descriptor leak
Root causes TaintMap unbounded (bug in cap); telemetry channel not bounded; FD leak in policy file watch
Detection Heap allocation gauge; FD count metric; CPU% per-hook metric
Automatic recovery TaintMap hard cap enforced in allocator; telemetry channel bounded; inotify FD closed on error
Manual recovery Restart process; reduce max_taint_entries; check for inotify watch leak

F10 — Cascading Failure

Attribute Detail
Symptoms Multiple InternalError + TelemetryError + PolicyLoadError simultaneously
Root causes Deployment of bad build; corrupt shared library; OOM-kill mid-request
Detection CascadeAlert fired when 3+ distinct failure classes within 60s window
Automatic recovery Fail-open to minimize app impact; isolate RASP from request path via kill-switch config flag
Kill-switch rasp.kill_switch = true in config or env RASP_KILL_SWITCH=1 → hook ABI returns Allow for all events immediately; no analysis
Manual recovery Re-deploy last-known-good build; verify kill-switch is toggled off after fix
Forensic artifacts CascadeAlert event with contributing failure class list

Operational Readiness

Metrics Strategy

Metric Type Labels Purpose
rasp_hook_latency_us Histogram sink_type Overhead budget tracking; alert on p95 > 5% baseline
rasp_detections_total Counter sink_type, severity, action Detection rate; spike → attack wave or FP regression
rasp_blocks_total Counter sink_type, rule_id Block effectiveness; confirm rules firing
rasp_taint_budget_used Gauge — Taint engine headroom; alert on sustained > 80%
rasp_dropped_events_total Counter — Telemetry back-pressure signal
rasp_internal_errors_total Counter failure_class Engine health; any nonzero is pagerworthy
rasp_policy_reload_total Counter outcome (ok/fail) Config management health
rasp_sink_calls_total Counter sink_type Baseline activity; denominator for FP rate
rasp_analyzer_timeout_total Counter sink_type Analyzer reliability
rasp_false_positive_reported_total Counter sink_type, rule_id Operator FP feedback loop

Export: Prometheus /metrics endpoint on loopback (default 127.0.0.1:9112); configurable push-gateway for serverless.

Tracing Strategy

  • Per-request trace: span per rasp-protect pipeline stage (hook, context, taint, detect, policy, enforce).
  • Trace IDs propagated from app's existing trace context (W3C TraceContext header); RASP appends child spans.
  • On detection: span tagged with rasp.detection=true, rasp.rule_id, rasp.action.
  • Sampling: 100% for blocked requests; configurable rate (default 1%) for allowed requests.
  • Exporter: OTLP gRPC (opentelemetry-otlp crate); stdout exporter for debug builds.

Structured Logging

Field Type Mandatory
timestamp RFC 3339 yes
level ERROR/WARN/INFO/DEBUG yes
context_id UUID yes (if in request scope)
sink_type string on detection
rule_id string on detection
action Allow/Block/Monitor on detection
evidence string (sanitized) on detection
failure_class string on internal error
elapsed_us u64 on any hook path

Format: JSON-L to stderr; syslog transport optional. Log level at INFO by default; DEBUG requires RASP_LOG=debug.

Dashboard Design

Row 1: Hook Latency p50/p95/p99 (time series) | Detections/s by sink (stacked bar)
Row 2: Blocks/s by rule_id (top-N table)       | Internal Errors/s (single stat, red if >0)
Row 3: Taint Budget Used % (gauge)              | Dropped Events (counter delta)
Row 4: Sink Calls/s by type (area)              | Analyzer Timeouts (bar by sink_type)
Row 5: Policy Reload Status (event overlay)     | FP Reports (counter + trend)

Annotations: deployment events, policy reloads, kill-switch toggles.

Alerting Philosophy

  • Alert on symptoms (latency, errors, drops), not implementation details.
  • Critical (page): rasp_internal_errors_total rate > 0/min sustained 5 min; kill-switch activated; rasp_hook_latency_us p95 > 2x baseline.
  • Warning (ticket): rasp_dropped_events_total rate > 100/min; rasp_analyzer_timeout_total rate > 10/min; policy reload failure.
  • Info: policy reload success; mode change (Monitor ↔ Block).
  • FP rate alert: rasp_false_positive_reported_total / rasp_blocks_total > 5% over 1h → review rule set.

SLO Definitions

SLO Target Measurement Window
Inline latency overhead p95 ≤ 5% of baseline request p95 24h rolling
Detection availability (hook firing) ≥ 99.9% of sink calls intercepted per week
Block effectiveness (confirmed attacks blocked) ≥ 99% given policy=Block and detection confidence ≥ High per incident batch
Telemetry delivery to SIEM ≥ 99.5% events delivered within 60s per hour
False-positive rate (operator-reported) ≤ 0.1% of blocks per week per week

Incident Response

  1. Alert fires → on-call acknowledges within 5 min (pagerworthy) or next business day (warning).
  2. Check kill-switch: if cascading failure, toggle RASP_KILL_SWITCH=1 to stop RASP overhead immediately.
  3. Gather: rasp-cli status, recent telemetry events, metrics snapshot, app error rate before/after.
  4. Triage: distinguish RASP internal fault vs app regression vs attack wave.
  5. Resolution path A (RASP fault): roll back RASP build; re-enable after fix.
  6. Resolution path B (FP wave): add waiver rule; switch affected rule to Monitor; tune threshold.
  7. Resolution path C (attack wave): verify blocks are correct; escalate to security team; preserve evidence.
  8. Post-incident: write PIR; update runbook; add corpus test case.

Runbooks

Runbook Trigger Key Steps
rasp-kill-switch.md Cascading failure, unacceptable latency Set env var; verify hook returns Allow; restore after fix
rasp-fp-remediation.md FP rate alert Identify rule_id; add waiver; test corpus; re-enable
rasp-policy-reload.md Policy load failure Validate file; fix parse error; re-sign HMAC; trigger reload
rasp-siem-reconnect.md Telemetry pipeline failure Fix endpoint; drain local buffer; verify delivery resumed
rasp-upgrade.md New RASP version Canary deploy; monitor latency/FP; full rollout; verify ABI compat

Deployment Topology

  • RASP ships as a native shared library (librasp.so) loaded by the runtime adapter; no separate process.
  • Sidecar-free: runs in-process to guarantee zero-copy access to hook args.
  • Config file: rasp.toml on local filesystem or mounted ConfigMap (Kubernetes).
  • Telemetry: OTLP exporter + Prometheus scrape endpoint on loopback.

Canary and Rolling-Upgrade Strategy

  • Canary: deploy new RASP version to 1% of instances; validate rasp_hook_latency_us p95, error rate, FP rate for 30 min.
  • Gate: if any SLO breached → automatic rollback (orchestrator feature flag).
  • Rolling: 10% → 25% → 50% → 100% with 5-min bake at each step.
  • Rollback: redeploy previous build; no data migration needed (RASP is stateless per-request).
  • Zero-downtime: adapter reload supported if runtime allows library hot-swap; otherwise rolling instance restart.

Backup and Restore

  • Policy files: backed up in version control (git); no runtime state to back up (RASP is stateless).
  • Telemetry local buffer: flushed to SIEM on restart; maximum buffer size configured.
  • Restore testing: monthly drill — simulate SIEM outage, verify local buffer fills and flushes on reconnect.

Compatibility & Versioning

Semantic Versioning Policy

  • rasp-core, rasp-hook, rasp-protect follow SemVer 2.0 strictly.
  • MAJOR bump: breaking change to hook ABI, core type layout, or wire format.
  • MINOR bump: additive enum variant (with #[non_exhaustive]), new optional config field, new sink type.
  • PATCH bump: bug fix, performance, doc; no behavior change at existing inputs.
  • MSRV policy: MSRV 1.78 guaranteed for lifetime of current MAJOR; MSRV increase requires MINOR bump with 12-week notice.

Hook ABI Compatibility

Element Guarantee
extern "C" hook function signatures Stable within MAJOR; additive new sinks are MINOR
HookEvent C-repr layout Frozen within MAJOR; new fields appended with MINOR; old fields never removed within MAJOR
Adapter registration protocol ABI version integer in AdapterInfo; engine rejects adapter with incompatible MAJOR
SinkType enum wire repr Stable integer values assigned on stabilization; new variants appended; never renumbered

Wire Compatibility (Telemetry / CEF / SIEM)

Format Policy
CEF event format Fields added additively; existing field names/types frozen; deprecated fields kept for 2 MAJOR versions
OTLP span attributes rasp.* namespace attributes additive; no removal within MAJOR
